# FILE: L7/0.md
*edited by this branch; path is still live on main — 2,892 words*

# L7/

**Object:** External provenance, public/legal governance, peering, publications, exposure, and exchange.
**Path:** `L7/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/ — this layer owns the external relations of the bounded object (provenance, governance, peering, and publication state), not its lower-layer scientific meaning.

L7 is the forward face of the bounded object: the place where it meets external people, institutions, systems, repositories, legal and public conditions, publications, and data sources. The specification states the position directly: "L7 = Desktop" and "L7 is the desk: the current external surface where finished work, external bindings, provenance, governance, peering, and publications become addressable without moving their scientific meaning out of the USO." Its core question is: "What provenance, governance, peer relation, publication, or other external representation must exist at the object's forward face?"

## Position in the architecture

The following statements are quoted from the specification's global parts and control how this directory sits against `USO/`.

- "L0 through L6 are the seven interior dependency layers. L7 is the numbered external Surface / Forward Face; it participates in traversal but is not an eighth interior dependency layer."
- By the owner's definition `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/FIRST_IS_ALWAYS_0.md` (FIRST is 0), the seventh layer is `l6`, and 7 is the magnitude of this position, the eighth reached in traversal: numbered, not a layer.
- The name is design, not decoration. L7, pronounced El-Seven, is the desk: the uppercase L is the lower left hand corner and the 7 the upper right, the two opposite corners that frame the surface this layer is, the specification's "Surface / Forward Face", its "L7 = Desktop". The owner's words are in `USO/l6/history/specification-history.md`.
- "L7 is outside the USO and is physically a sibling boundary to USO/. L6 through L0 are the scientific interior. Physical siblinghood and semantic sequence are different relations: crossing L7 <-> L6 is a boundary/peering event, not filesystem nesting."
- "Canonical direct L7 branches are L7/provenance/, L7/governance/, L7/peering/, and L7/publications/."
- "Scientific construction proceeds from L0 outward only as dependencies are established. External inspection begins at L7 and proceeds inward. Description does not become prescription merely because the structure can be traversed."
- Under the spec's heading "Contextual semantic route": "Source L0 definition → source L7 egress → receiving L7 ingress → receiving L0. A source object's L0 definition is an L7 egress object at its boundary; to us it is L7 ingress directed to our L0. Its semantic identity remains source-owned L0 throughout the exchange."

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | What provenance, governance, peer relation, publication, or other external representation must exist at the object's forward face? |
| Function | Provide the external Surface for provenance, prior art, public/legal governance, peering, publications, and release without absorbing external authority or granting lower-layer validity. |
| Directional inputs | Outward release: approved L6-backed packages. Ingress: peer objects, source/authority information, and governance conditions initiate inward inspection and may carry an exact source-owned L0 definition to the receiving L0 without transferring authorship. |
| Output | A bounded provenance record, governance record, ingress/egress binding, or publication/presentation with explicit source, authority, permissions, status, and direction where applicable. |
| Hard boundary | L7 owns the external relation, not the lower-layer scientific meaning. Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify provenance bindings, public/legal governance, visibility, ingress/egress direction, peer/object identity, release state, permissions, and NO EDIT/NO DELETE constraints for peer-owned ingress. Metadata does not transfer semantic authority. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents

- provenance: prior art, sources, lineage, authority
- public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions
- peering: exposure/custody, ingress/egress, peer/object bindings
- publications and release identity

### Permitted actions

- receive, bind, attribute, disclose, present, publish, release, route
- record provenance and prior art
- apply public/legal permissions and restrictions
- authorize, deny, quarantine, retire a binding without deleting its history

### Acceptance / boundary check

- Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required.
- Applicable public/legal governance is explicit where required.
- External ingress is not silently admitted to L6-L0.
- Publication or legal status does not strengthen scientific evidence.
- Unused canonical branches may remain empty without implying failure.

## Directory structure

```text
L7/0
├── provenance/0
│   ├── prior-art/0
│   ├── sources/0
│   ├── lineage/0
│   └── authority/0
├── governance/0
│   ├── copyright/0
│   ├── licensing/0
│   ├── patents/0
│   ├── permissions/0
│   ├── access/0
│   └── restrictions/0
├── peering/0
│   ├── private/0
│   │   ├── ingress/0
│   │   │   └── peer-1/0
│   │   │       └── object-1/0
│   │   └── egress/0
│   │       └── peer-1/0
│   │           └── object-1/0
│   └── public/0
│       ├── ingress/0
│       │   └── peer-1/0
│       │       └── object-1/0
│       └── egress/0
│           └── peer-1/0
│               └── object-1/0
└── publications/0
```

`/0` denotes the directory's local `0.md`; the specification states that "Directory metadata is attached to the directory itself and is therefore not drawn as another branch in the tree." No directory metadata is instantiated in this repository.

**Boundary / traversal rule.** L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| L7/ | External Surface / forward-face container outside the USO where the bounded object meets external people, institutions, systems, repositories, legal/public conditions, publications, and data sources. | Keeps external relations separate from the scientific interior while giving those relations an explicit owner. |
| provenance/ | Directory for the external provenance of the bounded object: where it came from, what preceded it, what source/version is being used, and what authority is being represented. | Places externally meaningful source and lineage information at the forward face rather than burying it inside internal memory. |
| provenance/prior-art/ | Directory for identified prior art and antecedent work relevant to the bounded object. | Makes the antecedent landscape explicit without confusing prior art with the object's locally authored L0 meaning. |
| provenance/sources/ | Directory for source identity and exact source/version references. | Keeps source identification inspectable at the external face. |
| provenance/lineage/ | Directory for externally relevant lineage and derivation relationships. | Makes origin and relation traceable without turning lineage into scientific validity. |
| provenance/authority/ | Directory for represented source/owner authority associated with the external object or record. | Distinguishes who owns or speaks for an external object from what the local research concludes about it. |
| governance/ | Directory for public/legal/custodial conditions attached to externally exposed or received objects. | Separates legal and access conditions from provenance, peering direction, publication, and scientific judgment. |
| governance/copyright/ | Directory for copyright-related representation when applicable. | Copyright governs external use of an artifact; it does not change the artifact's scientific meaning. |
| governance/licensing/ | Directory for licensing terms or status when applicable. | Keeps permissions of use explicit at the external face. |
| governance/patents/ | Directory for patent-related representation when applicable. | Provides an agnostic forward-face position for patent status or materials without requiring every project to use it. |
| governance/permissions/ | Directory for explicit permissions attached to external use, disclosure, or exchange. | Keeps permission separate from ownership and from scientific validity. |
| governance/access/ | Directory for access conditions applicable to externally facing objects. | Makes who may access an object a boundary condition rather than an internal scientific claim. |
| governance/restrictions/ | Directory for restrictions that constrain external disclosure, use, transfer, or handling. | Keeps restrictions explicit and machine-addressable without redefining the underlying research. |
| peering/ | Directory for relationships to externally owned peers and the bounded objects exchanged with them. | Preserves external ownership and authority instead of absorbing the peer into the USO. |
| private/ | Peering branch for non-public exposure or custody. | Keeps exposure/custody distinct from direction. |
| private/ingress/ | Private inbound peering position. | Represents something entering from a peer without making it public or internally owned. |
| private/egress/ | Private outbound peering position. | Represents something leaving toward a peer without making it a public release. |
| public/ | Peering branch for public exposure or custody. | Keeps public exposure distinct from whether the object is entering or leaving. |
| public/ingress/ | Public inbound peering position. | Represents a publicly exposed external object entering the local boundary without transferring ownership. |
| public/egress/ | Public outbound peering position. | Represents public outward exchange toward a peer; the released publication object itself is held by the L7 publications branch. |
| */peer-1/ | Directory identifying the external peer within an ingress or egress position. | Keeps peer identity distinct from the particular object exchanged with that peer. |
| */peer-1/object-1/ | Directory identifying one bounded peer-owned or peer-directed object. | Allows one peer to expose or receive multiple objects without treating the peer itself as the object. Peer-owned ingress is reference/binding only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository. |
| publications/ | Directory for released publication objects at the forward face. | Keeps publication release at L7 while writing remains L4, research meaning remains L5, and internal history remains L6. |

The peering rows above are the root table's record of the peering branch; the peering sheets themselves are at `L7/peering/` and beneath it.

## In the case of

### L7 scientific desktop and forward-face semantics

The Surface is organized around four owned external relations: provenance, governance, peering, and publications. Within peering, exposure/custody (public versus private) and direction (ingress versus egress) remain independent semantic distinctions.

```text
L7/peering/
├── private/
│   ├── ingress/peer-1/object-1/
│   └── egress/peer-1/object-1/
└── public/
    ├── ingress/peer-1/object-1/
    └── egress/peer-1/object-1/
```

Public ingress is the natural peering position for externally owned, publicly available definitions or peer objects. Private ingress is available for controlled inbound material. Public egress handles outward public exchange toward peers; private egress supports restricted outbound exchange. Released publications are represented by the L7 publications branch, while provenance and public/legal governance remain separately addressable.

**Peer-object contract.** peer-1 identifies the external peer and object-1 identifies the bounded object exposed by that peer. The object may be a document, database, foreign/federated data endpoint, or repository. A peer-owned ingress object is bound/reference-only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository. Retirement or supersession of the internal binding history is preserved at USO/l6/retired/ rather than erased.

**Author note.** The agnostic L7 topology carries admissible external-facing capabilities even when a project does not use them. A classroom experiment may leave governance and publication branches empty; a science fair, public release, publication, patent, or controlled exchange can populate the relevant forward-face positions without changing the architecture.

### External definitions and inward interpretation

A definition lives once at its owner. When 7LM does not author the definition, its exact version is bound at L7 peering and may be carried inward to receiving L0 and referenced at any needed layer with its immutable external-ownership tag intact. L7 provenance records source, version, authority, and prior-art relation; L7 governance records applicable public/legal/access conditions; L6 preserves binding history; L5 compares and interprets it. No receiving layer thereby becomes the definition's author.

### Autonomous and Auditonomous: peering without semantic annexation

The Autonomous/Auditonomous root issue is the originating case for the agnostic peer-1/object-1 relation. The repositories are implementation work in progress; their current layouts do not control this architecture.

The originating case, in its concrete form. Autonomous owns the locally authored definition of Autonomous and exposes an exact version at its L7 public egress. Auditonomous binds that version at its L7 public ingress as peer-owned and reference-only, NO EDIT and NO DELETE, and may carry it inward to its own L0 as externally authored semantic input; receipt does not copy authorship. Auditonomous L6 preserves the binding event, exact version, use, correction, supersession, and retirement; Auditonomous L5 interprets the bound definition without taking semantic ownership. A change to the definition returns to the Autonomous owner, who issues a new version; the receiver rebinds or retires its binding and does not edit the peer-owned object.

Reference implementations: https://github.com/aybllc/autonomous and https://github.com/aybllc/auditonomous

Autonomous owns the locally authored definition of Autonomous.

Autonomous L7 public egress may expose an exact version of that Autonomous L0 definition.

Auditonomous L7 public ingress binds that version as a peer-owned, reference-only object under NO EDIT and NO DELETE.

Source L0 definition → source L7 egress → receiving L7 ingress → receiving L0. Auditonomous L0 may receive and bind the Autonomous definition as externally authored semantic input while remaining responsible only for the locally authored definition of Auditonomous. Receipt at L0 does not copy authorship.

Auditonomous does not have to redefine the peer definition. Wherever it is referenced internally, the exact bound version carries an immutable external-ownership tag containing object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding.

Declared semantic-deference rule. Auditonomous intentionally takes the owner's exact bound definition as authoritative for what the owner means and does not re-prove or locally redefine that meaning. This is not a claim that the definition is objectively true, an endorsement of downstream claims, or a transfer of authorship.

Auditonomous L6 preserves the binding event, exact version, use, correction, supersession, and retirement history. Auditonomous L5 interprets the bound definition without taking semantic ownership.

An argument about Autonomous cannot silently stand in for an argument about Auditonomous. Any cross-object claim requires an explicit named relation or handoff; the same identity-tagging rule applies to agnostic methods as they are made now.

Autonomous freezes the narrowest reasonable agreement among its exact source-tagged standards inputs as the Autonomous consensus baseline. It must not manufacture consensus where sources materially disagree. Auditonomous then authors the Auditonomous difference as an explicit local delta; the baseline and delta remain separately owned and overlaid.

An upstream change creates a new binding; it does not mutate the pinned object. A change to the Autonomous definition returns to the Autonomous owner. Auditonomous updates or retires its binding; it does not edit the peer-owned definition.

**Clarification.** This is not an optional exemplar. It is the concrete originating boundary test for the agnostic architecture.

**Author note.** Public/private and ingress/egress are explicit semantic positions in the canonical research tree. Their presence does not assert that every external implementation must use the same filesystem vocabulary.

```text
external owner L0 definition
 |
 v
source L7 public egress
 |
 v
receiving L7 public ingress — exact immutable binding
 |
 v
receiving L7 provenance/governance — source, version, authority, conditions
 |
 v
receiving L6 through L1 — only needed layer-specific references, identity tag retained
 |
 v
receiving L0 — externally authored semantic input; local definition only if WE author one
```

This direction preserves ownership and makes interpretation inspectable. It also prevents a copied external definition from masquerading as a locally authored semantic object.

### Forward-face model

L7 is the desk: the current external surface where finished work, external bindings, provenance, governance, peering, and publications become addressable without moving their scientific meaning out of the USO.

Conceptual distinctions include consumer <-> product; customer <-> service; request <-> response; need <-> offer; incoming object <-> outgoing object; and private custody <-> public exchange.

Presence on the desk establishes nothing by itself. Provenance, custody, authority, copyright, patent status, permissions, internal consistency, and publication each answer different questions; none of them alone establishes scientific truth.

### Standing rules

A definition lives once, at its owner. L7 holds the boundary binding and provenance relation. The exact bound version may be carried inward with its immutable ownership tag, but it never becomes a copied substitute or locally authored definition.

To change an immutable external object, return to its owner. The repository using the object does not edit the peer-owned source.

Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed.

No outsider traverses the USO by default. What an outside reader or system needs is represented at the forward face.

Machine-readable metadata may support automation, but the architecture does not assume that automation has been enabled or validated. Current research practice may remain manual until the metadata contract is tested.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters from `USO/l6/` as "approved L6-backed packages" and leaves outward toward external peers, publications, and readers; inspection enters from the external peer, citizen, or practitioner at this face and proceeds toward `USO/l6/`, and from there inward to `USO/l0/`. These are the specification's two valid directions — "Authorized external inspection inward: L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0" and "Scientific construction outward: L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7" — and crossing between `L7/` and `USO/l6/` is a boundary/peering event, not filesystem nesting.

# FILE: L7/governance/0.md
*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 580 words*

# L7/governance/

**Object:** Directory for public/legal/custodial conditions attached to externally exposed or received objects.
**Path:** `L7/governance/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

This is the governance branch of the forward face. The specification's prose cell: "Directory for public/legal/custodial conditions attached to externally exposed or received objects." It is divided into six canonical positions — `copyright/`, `licensing/`, `patents/`, `permissions/`, `access/`, and `restrictions/` — and the layer "owns / represents" exactly that set: "public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions."

In practice, a person filing here records a condition that attaches to an object crossing the boundary in either direction: what may be copied, licensed, claimed, permitted, accessed, or must be withheld. The permitted actions are to "apply public/legal permissions and restrictions" and to "authorize, deny, quarantine, retire a binding without deleting its history"; the acceptance check is that "Applicable public/legal governance is explicit where required." On the inward route, "L7 governance records applicable public/legal/access conditions" for a bound definition after provenance has recorded its source, version, and authority.

## Why it exists

"Separates legal and access conditions from provenance, peering direction, publication, and scientific judgment." Each of those answers a different question; the specification's forward-face model states that "Provenance, custody, authority, copyright, patent status, permissions, internal consistency, and publication each answer different questions; none of them alone establishes scientific truth."

## Holds

- Public, legal, and custodial conditions attached to externally exposed or received objects, each placed in the child directory that owns the condition.
- The governance side of every peering binding and every release: the conditions under which an ingress object was received and an egress object or publication may leave.
- Records of a binding being authorized, denied, quarantined, or retired, with its history retained.

## Does not hold

- the source, version, authority, or prior-art relation of the object — `L7/provenance/`
- the bound object itself or its peer identity — `L7/peering/`
- the released publication object — `L7/publications/`
- the internal history of when a condition was applied or changed — `USO/l6/history/`; retired bindings — `USO/l6/retired/`
- any scientific judgment about the object — `USO/l5/research/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored." "Publication or legal status does not strengthen scientific evidence."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. The specification's author note: "A classroom experiment may leave governance and publication branches empty; a science fair, public release, publication, patent, or controlled exchange can populate the relevant forward-face positions without changing the architecture."
- The prior scaffold's `L7/corpus/`, `L7/private/`, and `L7/public/` positions were cut by the owner in commit c0f46e1, which created this directory empty; governance was not a named directory before that commit. No inherited term in the Harmonization Record is placed at this directory.
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation. The specification's machine-metadata row names "public/legal governance, visibility, ... permissions, and NO EDIT/NO DELETE constraints for peer-owned ingress" as things metadata may identify; none is enabled here.

# L7/governance/access/

**Object:** Directory for access conditions applicable to externally facing objects.
**Path:** `L7/governance/access/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for access conditions applicable to externally facing objects." A record here states who may access an externally facing object and under what condition: public, paywalled, institutionally licensed, sign-in gated, private, or embargoed. It is one of the six governance positions the layer owns ("public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions").

A person filing here records the access condition as it stands at the boundary, for both directions: the condition under which an external source could be obtained (or could not), and the condition under which a local object is exposed. Access is a governance condition. Exposure/custody (public versus private) is a peering distinction recorded at `L7/peering/`; the spec states that "Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions."

## Why it exists

"Makes who may access an object a boundary condition rather than an internal scientific claim." An access condition is what an outside reader or system meets first; it says nothing about the object's content.

## Holds

- Access conditions on externally facing objects: who may reach them, by what route, and under what gate.
- Access conditions met when obtaining external sources, including the record that a source was not obtained because its access condition was not met.
- The access condition attached to a publication or egress object.

## Does not hold

- the source or object itself — `L7/peering/` (ingress, NO EDIT and NO DELETE) or `L7/publications/`
- whether the object is publicly or privately held — `L7/peering/public/` and `L7/peering/private/`
- licence terms that follow from the access route — `L7/governance/licensing/`
- restrictions on onward disclosure — `L7/governance/restrictions/`
- the identity and version of the source obtained — `L7/provenance/sources/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"No outsider traverses the USO by default. What an outside reader or system needs is represented at the forward face."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- No inherited term in the Harmonization Record is placed at this directory.
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation. The specification's machine-metadata row names "visibility" among what metadata may identify; none is enabled here.

# FILE: L7/governance/copyright/0.md
*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 641 words*

# L7/governance/copyright/

**Object:** Directory for copyright-related representation when applicable.
**Path:** `L7/governance/copyright/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for copyright-related representation when applicable." A record here states the copyright condition attached to an object at the boundary: who holds copyright in an external object that has been received, what copyright is asserted over an object being released, and what that condition permits or bars. It is one of the six governance positions the layer owns ("public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions").

A person filing here records the condition as a fact about external use. The permitted actions are to "apply public/legal permissions and restrictions" and to "authorize, deny, quarantine, retire a binding without deleting its history"; a copyright condition may therefore be the reason a binding is quarantined or a source is held cite-only, and that reason is recorded here while the history of the decision stays at `USO/l6/`.

## Why it exists

"Copyright governs external use of an artifact; it does not change the artifact's scientific meaning." The condition is explicit at the desk so that an outside reader knows what may be reproduced, and no interior layer has to carry it.

## One place

Copyright has one place in a 7LM repository, and it is this directory. The rule: the place to look for copyright is only one place, and from there it is obvious to the observer what to do next; intuition is enforced by not defining many rules. So a README, a sheet, a ledger row, or a binding record never restates a copyright rule; it names this directory. The sheet here states, once, every copyright and licence condition the repository is under and, in a few numbered steps, what to do next: what may be quoted, what may be reproduced or published, what to do when a source is blocked, what to do when material turns out to be under copyright after the fact, and whose act it is to remove anything from history. Few rules, stated once, so the next step is obvious to the observer. The sibling positions `licensing/` and `restrictions/` stay admissible and point here; `access/` records what was obtained.

## Holds

- Copyright-related representation for received objects: the holder, the condition, and what reproduction it permits or bars.
- Copyright-related representation for released objects at `L7/publications/` or `L7/peering/*/egress/`.
- The copyright reason for a binding's quarantine or cite-only status, with its history preserved rather than deleted.
- The route: the short statement of what to do next, written for the observer, so that no other file needs a copyright rule.

## Does not hold

- the copyrighted object itself when bound — `L7/peering/public/ingress/peer-1/object-1/` or the private position (NO EDIT, NO DELETE)
- a licence instrument itself, when one is held — `L7/governance/licensing/`; the condition it imposes is stated here
- who owns or speaks for the object as a matter of authority — `L7/provenance/authority/`
- the source's identity and version — `L7/provenance/sources/`
- the object's scientific meaning or the verdict on it — `USO/l0/` through `USO/l5/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- No inherited term in the Harmonization Record is placed at this directory.
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

**Object:** Directory for licensing terms or status when applicable.

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for licensing terms or status when applicable." A record here states the licence under which an external object was received, or the licence under which a local object is released: the grantor, the terms, the status, and what use the terms permit. It is one of the six governance positions the layer owns ("public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions").

A person filing here records the terms as they stand at the boundary. The permitted actions are to "apply public/legal permissions and restrictions" and to "authorize, deny, quarantine, retire a binding without deleting its history"; the acceptance check is that "Applicable public/legal governance is explicit where required."

## Why it exists

"Keeps permissions of use explicit at the external face." A licence is a condition an outside reader or a receiving peer must be able to see before using an object; it is not a fact about the object's science.

## Holds

- Licensing terms or status for received objects, including single-user, institutional, or click-wrap terms that constrain redistribution.
- Licensing terms or status for objects released at `L7/publications/` or exchanged through `L7/peering/*/egress/`.
- The licence reason for a binding being held cite-only, quarantined, or retired, with its history preserved.

## Does not hold

- copyright ownership as such — `L7/governance/copyright/`
- explicit permissions granted for a particular use, disclosure, or exchange — `L7/governance/permissions/`
- restrictions that bar disclosure, transfer, or handling — `L7/governance/restrictions/`
- the source's identity, version, and issuer — `L7/provenance/sources/` and `L7/provenance/authority/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

# L7/governance/patents/

**Object:** Directory for patent-related representation when applicable.

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for patent-related representation when applicable." A record here states the patent status or patent-related condition attached to an object at the boundary: a filing, a claim scope, a determination that material is withheld pending filing, or a determination that an object is excluded from a filing. It is one of the six governance positions the layer owns ("public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions").

A person filing here records the status as a boundary fact. The specification's author note names the case: "a science fair, public release, publication, patent, or controlled exchange can populate the relevant forward-face positions without changing the architecture." The acceptance check is that "Applicable public/legal governance is explicit where required."

"Provides an agnostic forward-face position for patent status or materials without requiring every project to use it." Most projects leave it empty; a project that files keeps the status here rather than inside the science it covers.

## Holds

- Patent-related representation: status, scope of what is claimed or withheld, and its relation to objects released or exchanged at the forward face.
- The patent status from which a restriction on disclosure derives; the restriction itself is recorded at `L7/governance/restrictions/`.

- licences granted over patented material — `L7/governance/licensing/`
- restrictions on disclosure as such — `L7/governance/restrictions/`
- prior art searched against a claim — `L7/provenance/prior-art/`
- any judgment that patent status strengthens a claim — no layer; "Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored."

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

# L7/governance/permissions/

**Object:** Directory for explicit permissions attached to external use, disclosure, or exchange.
**Path:** `L7/governance/permissions/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for explicit permissions attached to external use, disclosure, or exchange." A record here states a permission that has been granted or declared: who may use, disclose, or exchange which object, in which direction, and on what condition. It is one of the six governance positions the layer owns ("public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions"), and "apply public/legal permissions and restrictions" is a permitted L7 action.

A person filing here records the permission as granted, with its grantor and scope. The peer-object contract supplies the standing permission that applies to every peer-owned ingress object: it "is bound/reference-only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository." A permission here does not stand in for ownership and does not settle validity.

## Why it exists

"Keeps permission separate from ownership and from scientific validity." Who may act on an object is a boundary condition; who owns it is provenance; whether it is sound is interior research.

## Holds

- Explicit permissions attached to external use, disclosure, or exchange of objects crossing the boundary in either direction.
- The declared permission conditions under which an egress object or publication may leave, and under which an ingress object was received.
- Custodial permissions governing the repository's own handling of the forward face, when the owner states them.

## Does not hold

- the object the permission concerns — `L7/peering/` or `L7/publications/`
- ownership or represented authority — `L7/provenance/authority/`
- licence terms as such — `L7/governance/licensing/`
- who may access an object — `L7/governance/access/`
- what is barred — `L7/governance/restrictions/`
- the history of permission changes — `USO/l6/history/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"To change an immutable external object, return to its owner. The repository using the object does not edit the peer-owned source."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- No inherited term in the Harmonization Record is placed at this directory.
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation. The specification's machine-metadata row says metadata "may identify ... permissions, and NO EDIT/NO DELETE constraints for peer-owned ingress"; here those constraints are stated in prose only.

# L7/governance/restrictions/

**Object:** Directory for restrictions that constrain external disclosure, use, transfer, or handling.
**Path:** `L7/governance/restrictions/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for restrictions that constrain external disclosure, use, transfer, or handling." A record here states what may not be done with an object at the boundary: not redistributed, not reproduced at length, not used on-line, not transferred, not disclosed, not opened. It is one of the six governance positions the layer owns ("public/legal governance: copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, restrictions"), and "apply public/legal permissions and restrictions" is a permitted L7 action.

A person filing here records the restriction, its source (a licence, a copyright holder, a patent filing, an owner ruling), and the objects it binds. A restriction may be the reason a binding is denied, quarantined, or retired; the permitted action is to "authorize, deny, quarantine, retire a binding without deleting its history," so the restriction stays recorded here and the decision's history stays at `USO/l6/`.

## Why it exists

"Keeps restrictions explicit and machine-addressable without redefining the underlying research." A restriction constrains handling at the desk; it does not change what the object means or what the research concludes.

## Holds

- Restrictions on external disclosure, use, transfer, or handling of received or exposed objects.
- Restrictions that follow from a copyright, licence, patent, permission, or access condition recorded in the sibling governance positions, cross-referenced by path.
- Owner rulings that restrict what crosses outward, for example a ruling that a repository or object is not released.

## Does not hold

- the restricted object itself — `L7/peering/` or `L7/publications/`
- the copyright, licence, or patent condition from which the restriction derives — `L7/governance/copyright/`, `L7/governance/licensing/`, `L7/governance/patents/`
- who may access the object — `L7/governance/access/`
- what is affirmatively permitted — `L7/governance/permissions/`
- the history of a quarantine or retirement decision — `USO/l6/history/`; the retired binding — `USO/l6/retired/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.

# FILE: L7/peering/0.md
*edited by this branch; path is still live on main — 1,375 words*

# L7/peering/

**Object:** Directory for relationships to externally owned peers and the bounded objects exchanged with them.
**Path:** `L7/peering/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

Directory for relationships to externally owned peers and the bounded objects exchanged with them. It is one of the four canonical direct L7 branches: "Canonical direct L7 branches are L7/provenance/, L7/governance/, L7/peering/, and L7/publications/." The layer sheet at L7/ owns "peering: exposure/custody, ingress/egress, peer/object bindings"; this directory is where that ownership is filed.

Two independent distinctions organize the branch: "Within peering, exposure/custody (public versus private) and direction (ingress versus egress) remain independent semantic distinctions." Exposure is filed at L7/peering/public/ or L7/peering/private/; direction is filed at ingress/ or egress/ beneath each. The peer is identified at peer-1/ and each bounded object at peer-1/object-1/. "Public ingress is the natural peering position for externally owned, publicly available definitions or peer objects. Private ingress is available for controlled inbound material. Public egress handles outward public exchange toward peers; private egress supports restricted outbound exchange. Released publications are represented by the L7 publications branch, while provenance and public/legal governance remain separately addressable."

In practice a person filing here records one relation to one peer and one bounded object exchanged with it, in the position that names its exposure and its direction, using the L7 permitted actions: "receive, bind, attribute, disclose, present, publish, release, route" and "authorize, deny, quarantine, retire a binding without deleting its history". The filing passes the L7 acceptance checks: "Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required." "Applicable public/legal governance is explicit where required." "External ingress is not silently admitted to L6-L0." A bound peer object is used inward only with its tag: "Every internal representation that uses it carries the exact bound version with an immutable external-ownership tag: object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding. An upstream change creates a new binding; it does not mutate the pinned object."

The Peer-object contract ("peer-1 identifies the external peer and object-1 identifies the bounded object exposed by that peer.") and the originating case ("The Autonomous/Auditonomous root issue is the originating case for the agnostic peer-1/object-1 relation.") are carried verbatim at L7/; the source side is exemplified at L7/peering/public/egress/ and the receiving side at L7/peering/public/ingress/.

## Why it exists

Preserves external ownership and authority instead of absorbing the peer into the USO. The peer stays outside the boundary; only the relation and the bound object are represented here, and "Presence on the desk establishes nothing by itself."

## Many intelligences at one face

The peering positions are not sized for one counterpart. `peer-1/` opens an enumeration that is the same on all four faces (the specification numbers peers and objects from 1, while the layers count from 0 under the owner's definition `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/FIRST_IS_ALWAYS_0.md`; the difference is reported here, not resolved): private ingress, private egress, public ingress, public egress. So the bounded object can meet any number of intelligences at once — a person, an institution, a standards body, another repository, an archive, a model session, an orchestrator — each at its own numbered peer position, each delivery or hand-off its own `object-M/`, and none of them in another's directory. Nothing has to be added to the topology for this; it is what the enumeration already provides.

What the directory system then gives:

- **Separate incoming directories.** Each intelligence brings its material into its own `ingress/peer-N/`. Its files are bound there reference-only, NO EDIT and NO DELETE, and "External ingress is not silently admitted to L6-L0." Provenance is positional: the path says who brought a thing before anyone reads it.
- **Separate outgoing directories.** What the object hands each intelligence — a task, a definition, a question — leaves through the same peer's `egress/peer-N/`, so a hand-off and its answer are traceable by number.
- **Standing one level above the object.** The peer's `0.md`, one directory above its objects, is the owner's register of that peer's objects and their standing — received, under review, adopted with where the accepted content was filed, denied, quarantined, retired. A bound object never moves and never changes when a decision is taken; the register records the decision, and its history stays at `USO/l6/`. That is how one party learns whether the other has processed something: the answer sits in the register above the object. What any intelligence may or may not write is not stated by the tree (owner's direction, 2026-09-06).
- **One acceptance path for all of them.** Whatever any intelligence brings is compared and interpreted at `USO/l5/`, and only what survives is carried inward, as the object's own authorship, citing the bound object. A person's manuscript, a body's clause, a model's draft, and an orchestrator's result take the same route. The rule is the same for a professor and a program.
- **Exposure per counterpart.** An intelligence whose relation is controlled sits under `private/`; one whose exchange is public sits under `public/`; the same intelligence may hold both, because exposure and direction stay independent.
- **Predictability for machines and people alike.** The pattern `L7/peering/{private,public}/{ingress,egress}/peer-N/object-M/` is identical in every 7LM repository. Which number is which counterpart is read once from the face's register. Nothing moves once bound, so a session, a watcher, or a colleague can be written to the path and never has to relearn it.

Described here; not instantiated by this sheet. Which counterparts are bound, at which positions, and who writes the registers is each repository's owner's decision, and the register is written by a person; nothing here presumes an automated mover.

## Holds
- the exposure branches L7/peering/private/ and L7/peering/public/, each with an ingress/ and an egress/ position
- peer identities at */peer-1/ and bounded peer-owned or peer-directed objects at */peer-1/object-1/
- an "ingress/egress binding" with "explicit source, authority, permissions, status, and direction where applicable"
- the standing of each binding — authorized, denied, quarantined, or retired — "without deleting its history"

## Does not hold
- source identity, exact version, represented authority, and prior-art relation of a bound object — L7/provenance/
- copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, and restrictions attached to a bound object — L7/governance/
- the released publication object — L7/publications/
- binding history — USO/l6/; retirement or supersession of a binding — USO/l6/retired/
- comparison and interpretation of a bound definition — USO/l5/
- locally authored definitions — USO/l0/semantics/definitions/
- lower-layer scientific meaning: "L7 owns the external relation, not the lower-layer scientific meaning. Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored."

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

Standing rules that bind this branch: "A definition lives once, at its owner. L7 holds the boundary binding and provenance relation. The exact bound version may be carried inward with its immutable ownership tag, but it never becomes a copied substitute or locally authored definition." "To change an immutable external object, return to its owner. The repository using the object does not edit the peer-owned source." "Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed."

Author note. "Public/private and ingress/egress are explicit semantic positions in the canonical research tree. Their presence does not assert that every external implementation must use the same filesystem vocabulary."

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing
- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. "Unused canonical branches may remain empty without implying failure."
- Reserved terms once kept under the old L7/private/ desk (found; caught; naught; regress) remain Reserved with topology unadjudicated; see L7/peering/private/0.md. Nothing in this branch gives them a home.
- Machine metadata: described at L7/; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: L7/peering/private/0.md
*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 549 words*

# L7/peering/private/

**Object:** Peering branch for non-public exposure or custody.
**Path:** `L7/peering/private/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

Peering branch for non-public exposure or custody. This directory names one value of the exposure/custody distinction only; direction is named beneath it, at L7/peering/private/ingress/ and L7/peering/private/egress/. The two are independent: "Within peering, exposure/custody (public versus private) and direction (ingress versus egress) remain independent semantic distinctions." Private is the custody side of the forward-face distinction "private custody <-> public exchange".

Private does not mean internally owned. What enters here enters "from a peer without making it public or internally owned"; what leaves here leaves "toward a peer without making it a public release". A person filing here records a relation whose exposure is non-public — "Private ingress is available for controlled inbound material" and "private egress supports restricted outbound exchange" — and passes the L7 acceptance checks: "Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required." "Applicable public/legal governance is explicit where required." "External ingress is not silently admitted to L6-L0." Access conditions and restrictions applicable to the object are recorded at L7/governance/access/ and L7/governance/restrictions/, not here.

## Why it exists

Keeps exposure/custody distinct from direction. A private relation can run in either direction, and its privacy is a property of custody, not of which way the object moves.

## Holds
- L7/peering/private/ingress/ — "Private inbound peering position."
- L7/peering/private/egress/ — "Private outbound peering position."
- beneath each, */peer-1/ and */peer-1/object-1/ for the peer and the bounded object exchanged under non-public custody

## Does not hold
- public exposure or custody — L7/peering/public/
- access conditions and restrictions that constrain disclosure — L7/governance/access/ and L7/governance/restrictions/
- permissions attached to controlled exchange — L7/governance/permissions/
- source, version, and authority of a controlled object — L7/provenance/
- binding history — USO/l6/; retirement or supersession of a binding — USO/l6/retired/
- any reserved term of the old desk (see Standing)

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"No outsider traverses the USO by default. What an outside reader or system needs is represented at the forward face."

## Standing

- Reserved: found; caught; naught. Harmonization Record disposition, verbatim: "caught remains the zero state; found remains the observer role; naught remains the empty or settled state. Their topology remains unadjudicated." This directory does not give them a home.
- Reserved: regress. Harmonization Record disposition, verbatim: "Preserved as a prior unadjudicated holding concept; active path and owner remain unadjudicated." This directory does not give it a home.

Private outbound peering position. Exposure (private) is fixed by the parent L7/peering/private/; direction (egress) is fixed here; the two are independent distinctions. The L7 Directional inputs row for outward movement reads "Outward release: approved L6-backed packages."; this position is not a public release. A person filing here records what is directed to the peer, the exact version exposed, and the restriction that keeps the exchange non-public, with the conditions themselves recorded at L7/governance/. Leaving does not promote: "Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed."

## Why it exists

Represents something leaving toward a peer without making it a public release. Restricted exchange is a peering relation; release is a publication relation.

## Holds
- peer-1/ — the external peer toward which restricted material leaves
- peer-1/object-1/ — one bounded peer-directed object, the exact version exposed to that peer
- the binding's standing: authorized, denied, quarantined, or retired, "without deleting its history"

## Does not hold
- public outbound exchange — L7/peering/public/egress/
- inbound material — L7/peering/private/ingress/
- restrictions, permissions, and access conditions that keep the exchange non-public — L7/governance/restrictions/, L7/governance/permissions/, L7/governance/access/
- the released publication object — L7/publications/
- the package's internal memory — USO/l6/
- the object's scientific meaning, which stays at the interior layer that owns it

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

## What this directory is

Directory identifying one bounded peer-owned or peer-directed object. Here the object is peer-directed and leaving under private custody — "private egress supports restricted outbound exchange" — where exposure (private) and direction (egress) are independent distinctions fixed by the parent directories. `object-1` is a canonical position name, not the name of any actual object. Allows one peer to expose or receive multiple objects without treating the peer itself as the object. Peer-owned ingress is reference/binding only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"An upstream change creates a new binding; it does not mutate the pinned object." A change to what is exposed here is made by its owner, and the peer updates or retires its binding.

# L7/peering/private/ingress/

**Object:** Private inbound peering position.
**Path:** `L7/peering/private/ingress/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

Private inbound peering position. Exposure (private) is fixed by the parent L7/peering/private/; direction (ingress) is fixed here; the two are independent distinctions. "Private ingress is available for controlled inbound material." What is filed is a binding to a peer object entering under non-public custody: the peer at peer-1/, the bounded object at peer-1/object-1/.

The L7 directional-input rule governs entry: "Ingress: peer objects, source/authority information, and governance conditions initiate inward inspection and may carry an exact source-owned L0 definition to the receiving L0 without transferring authorship." A person filing here binds the exact version — "A peer-owned ingress object is bound/reference-only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository." — and records source, version, authority and applicable public/legal/access conditions at L7/provenance/ and L7/governance/. "External ingress is not silently admitted to L6-L0."

## Why it exists

Represents something entering from a peer without making it public or internally owned. Privacy of custody and non-ownership are separate facts; this position records both.

## Holds
- peer-1/ — the external peer from which controlled material enters
- peer-1/object-1/ — one bounded peer-owned object, bound reference-only, with its immutable external-ownership tag ("object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding")
- the binding's standing: authorized, denied, quarantined, or retired, "without deleting its history"

## Does not hold
- public inbound material — L7/peering/public/ingress/
- outbound material — L7/peering/private/egress/
- access conditions, restrictions, and permissions — L7/governance/access/, L7/governance/restrictions/, L7/governance/permissions/
- source, version, authority — L7/provenance/sources/, L7/provenance/authority/
- retirement or supersession of a binding — USO/l6/retired/
- a locally authored definition — USO/l0/semantics/definitions/

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"To change an immutable external object, return to its owner. The repository using the object does not edit the peer-owned source."

## The designated curated-source archive

`aybllc/l6` enters here: the archive the owner designated to serve curated sources to this program, not edited from a consuming repository. Its binding is a private-ingress peer binding — the repository is INTERNAL — reference-only, NO EDIT and NO DELETE, pinned at commit `53b28f6` at the time of this record. Sources are consumed from it by pointer rows at `L7/provenance/sources/` (ID, citation, `l6` path, `l6` commit, access, label), never by copying a file inward. Its own `durable/README.md` states an access-control list for sessions that mirrors this rule from the inside — a session "can ONLY create new `.md` files inside `durable/orphan/`" and "CANNOT edit, append to, overwrite, or delete any file once created" — the same position-is-state lifecycle this scaffold's Harmonization Record reserves (found; caught; naught), left unadjudicated here.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing
- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Peer-owned ingress is reference/binding only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository.
- `aybllc/l6` is bound here as the designated curated-source archive; read-only from every consuming repository.
- Machine metadata: described at L7/; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# L7/peering/public/

**Object:** Peering branch for public exposure or custody.
**Path:** `L7/peering/public/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

Peering branch for public exposure or custody. This directory names one value of the exposure/custody distinction only; direction is named beneath it, at L7/peering/public/ingress/ and L7/peering/public/egress/. The two are independent: "Within peering, exposure/custody (public versus private) and direction (ingress versus egress) remain independent semantic distinctions." Public is the exchange side of the forward-face distinction "private custody <-> public exchange".

Public exposure transfers nothing. An object entering here enters "without transferring ownership"; an object leaving here is "public outward exchange toward a peer; the released publication object itself is held by the L7 publications branch." A person filing here records a relation whose exposure is public — "Public ingress is the natural peering position for externally owned, publicly available definitions or peer objects" and "Public egress handles outward public exchange toward peers" — and passes the L7 acceptance checks: "Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required." "Applicable public/legal governance is explicit where required." "External ingress is not silently admitted to L6-L0." "Publication or legal status does not strengthen scientific evidence."

## Why it exists

Keeps public exposure distinct from whether the object is entering or leaving. A public relation can run in either direction, and its publicity is a property of exposure, not of which way the object moves.

## Holds
- L7/peering/public/ingress/ — "Public inbound peering position."
- L7/peering/public/egress/ — "Public outbound peering position."
- beneath each, */peer-1/ and */peer-1/object-1/ for the peer and the bounded object exchanged under public exposure

## Does not hold
- non-public exposure or custody — L7/peering/private/
- the released publication object — L7/publications/ ("Released publications are represented by the L7 publications branch")
- copyright, licensing, and permissions that govern public use — L7/governance/
- source, version, and authority of a public object — L7/provenance/
- binding history — USO/l6/; retirement or supersession of a binding — USO/l6/retired/
- lower-layer scientific meaning: "Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored."

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed."

## Standing
- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.

# L7/peering/public/egress/peer-1/object-1/

**Object:** Directory identifying one bounded peer-owned or peer-directed object. `object-1` "identifies the bounded object exposed by that peer"; at an egress position this repository is the exposing peer.
**Path:** `L7/peering/public/egress/peer-1/object-1/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

Directory identifying one bounded peer-owned or peer-directed object. Here the object is peer-directed and leaving under public exposure — "Public egress handles outward public exchange toward peers" — where exposure (public) and direction (egress) are independent distinctions fixed by the parent directories. `object-1` is a canonical position name, not the name of any actual object. "The object may be a document, database, foreign/federated data endpoint, or repository." What is filed is the exact version directed to the peer and the relation under which it leaves; the L7 Directional inputs row for outward movement reads "Outward release: approved L6-backed packages."; this position is not the released publication object. Leaving is not release and adds nothing to the object: "the released publication object itself is held by the L7 publications branch", and "Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed." In the originating case the object at this position, seen from Autonomous, is "an exact version of that Autonomous L0 definition".

## Why it exists

Allows one peer to expose or receive multiple objects without treating the peer itself as the object. Peer-owned ingress is reference/binding only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository.

## Holds
- one relation for one bounded peer-directed object: exact version exposed, the peer it is directed to, direction, and standing (authorized, denied, quarantined, or retired) "without deleting its history"

## Does not hold
- the object's owning representation, which lives once at the interior layer that owns it — USO/l0/semantics/definitions/ when it is a locally authored definition
- copyright, licensing, permissions, and access conditions on the exchange — L7/governance/
- source, version, and authority as represented outward — L7/provenance/
- the package's internal memory and the relation's binding history — USO/l6/
- the released publication object — L7/publications/

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"An upstream change creates a new binding; it does not mutate the pinned object. A change to the Autonomous definition returns to the Autonomous owner. Auditonomous updates or retires its binding; it does not edit the peer-owned definition."

## Standing
- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- On the peer's side this object is peer-owned ingress: reference/binding only, NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository.
- Machine metadata: described at L7/; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# L7/peering/public/ingress/

**Object:** Public inbound peering position.
**Path:** `L7/peering/public/ingress/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

Public inbound peering position. Exposure (public) is fixed by the parent L7/peering/public/; direction (ingress) is fixed here; the two are independent distinctions. "Public ingress is the natural peering position for externally owned, publicly available definitions or peer objects." What is filed is a binding to a publicly exposed peer object entering the local boundary: the peer at peer-1/, the bounded object at peer-1/object-1/.

The L7 directional-input rule governs entry: "Ingress: peer objects, source/authority information, and governance conditions initiate inward inspection and may carry an exact source-owned L0 definition to the receiving L0 without transferring authorship." A person filing here binds the exact version — "A peer-owned ingress object is bound/reference-only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository." — and the object may then travel inward only as the spec routes it: "When 7LM does not author the definition, its exact version is bound at L7 peering and may be carried inward to receiving L0 and referenced at any needed layer with its immutable external-ownership tag intact. L7 provenance records source, version, authority, and prior-art relation; L7 governance records applicable public/legal/access conditions; L6 preserves binding history; L5 compares and interprets it. No receiving layer thereby becomes the definition's author." "External ingress is not silently admitted to L6-L0."

This is the receiving side of the originating case: "Auditonomous L7 public ingress binds that version as a peer-owned, reference-only object under NO EDIT and NO DELETE." "Declared semantic-deference rule. Auditonomous intentionally takes the owner's exact bound definition as authoritative for what the owner means and does not re-prove or locally redefine that meaning. This is not a claim that the definition is objectively true, an endorsement of downstream claims, or a transfer of authorship."

## Why it exists

Represents a publicly exposed external object entering the local boundary without transferring ownership. "This direction preserves ownership and makes interpretation inspectable. It also prevents a copied external definition from masquerading as a locally authored semantic object."

## Holds
- peer-1/ — the external peer whose publicly available object enters
- peer-1/object-1/ — one bounded peer-owned object, bound reference-only, with its immutable external-ownership tag ("object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding")
- the binding's standing: authorized, denied, quarantined, or retired, "without deleting its history"

## Does not hold
- controlled inbound material — L7/peering/private/ingress/
- outbound exchange — L7/peering/public/egress/
- source, version, authority, prior-art relation — L7/provenance/
- public/legal/access conditions — L7/governance/
- binding history — USO/l6/; retirement or supersession of a binding — USO/l6/retired/
- comparison and interpretation of the bound object — USO/l5/
- the receiving repository's own definition — USO/l0/semantics/definitions/ ("local definition only if WE author one")

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"A change to the Autonomous definition returns to the Autonomous owner. Auditonomous updates or retires its binding; it does not edit the peer-owned definition."

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing
- The rule for a receiving repository: Auditonomous gets the definitions of autonomous from Autonomous and nowhere else. The exact bound L0 object of aybllc/autonomous at this position is that definition's only source for the auditonomous repository — no standard, dictionary, corpus, or archive is bound directly for it.
- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Peer-owned ingress is reference/binding only: NO EDIT and NO DELETE by the receiving repository.
- Machine metadata: described at L7/; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: L7/provenance/0.md

# L7/provenance/

**Object:** Directory for the external provenance of the bounded object: where it came from, what preceded it, what source/version is being used, and what authority is being represented.
**Path:** `L7/provenance/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

This is the provenance branch of the forward face. The specification's prose cell: "Directory for the external provenance of the bounded object: where it came from, what preceded it, what source/version is being used, and what authority is being represented." It is divided into four canonical positions — `prior-art/`, `sources/`, `lineage/`, and `authority/` — each answering one of those four questions, and L7 "owns / represents" exactly that set: "provenance: prior art, sources, lineage, authority."

In practice, a person filing here records an external relation of the bounded object, not a claim about its truth. The permitted actions are to "record provenance and prior art" and to "receive, bind, attribute, disclose, present, publish, release, route"; the acceptance check is that "Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required." The route for an externally owned definition passes through here: "L7 provenance records source, version, authority, and prior-art relation; L7 governance records applicable public/legal/access conditions; L6 preserves binding history; L5 compares and interprets it."

## Why it exists

"Places externally meaningful source and lineage information at the forward face rather than burying it inside internal memory." Provenance about external objects is something an outside reader or system needs, so it is represented at the desk rather than inside `USO/l6/`.

## Holds

- Records of where an externally owned object or source came from, at what version, and who speaks for it — one record per relation, placed in the child directory that owns the question.
- The provenance relation of any peer object bound at `L7/peering/` (source, version, authority, prior-art relation).
- The source ledger `L7/provenance/sources/source-ledger.md` (see `L7/provenance/sources/0.md`).

## Does not hold

- the bound peer object itself — `L7/peering/public/ingress/peer-1/object-1/` or the corresponding private position
- copyright, licensing, patent, permission, access, or restriction conditions — `L7/governance/`
- the internal chronology of how a binding was made, used, corrected, or superseded — `USO/l6/history/`; retired bindings — `USO/l6/retired/`
- comparison, interpretation, or verdict on a source — `USO/l5/research/`
- the locally authored meaning of the bounded object — `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`
- released publication objects — `L7/publications/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

The hard boundary applies to every record here: "Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term "L6 provenance" — disposition Split: "Internal reconstructable memory remains L6; external source, authority, prior art, and public provenance belong at L7/provenance/."
- Inherited term "prior-art" — disposition Relocated; recorded at `L7/provenance/prior-art/0.md`.
- Supporting-evidence disposition, quoted: "Git history can support L6 reconstruction, but Git history and scientific memory/provenance are not the same object. External provenance remains L7-owned; internal reconstructable scientific memory remains L6-owned."
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.
