# L7/provenance/authority/

**Object:** Directory for represented source/owner authority associated with the external object or record.
**Path:** `L7/provenance/authority/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for represented source/owner authority associated with the external object or record." A record here states who owns or speaks for an external object — the standards body, publisher, author, or peer repository — and in what capacity. It is one of the four provenance positions the layer owns ("provenance: prior art, sources, lineage, authority"), and the layer's function is to provide this surface "without absorbing external authority or granting lower-layer validity."

A person filing here records represented authority as a fact about the external object. The declared semantic-deference rule shows how far that goes: "the receiver intentionally takes the owner's exact bound definition as authoritative for what the owner means and does not re-prove or locally redefine that meaning. This declared trust is not a claim that the definition is objectively true, not an endorsement of downstream claims, and not a transfer of authorship."

## Why it exists

"Distinguishes who owns or speaks for an external object from what the local research concludes about it." Authority about meaning stays with the owner; conclusions about the object stay with `USO/l5/`.

## Holds

- Represented source/owner authority for external objects and records: the owner, the source layer at the owner, and the capacity in which the owner speaks.
- The authority element of a binding's immutable external-ownership tag ("object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding").
- Where more than one external body speaks for a term, the represented authority of each is a separate external fact; the spec's Autonomous case states that disagreement among source-tagged standards inputs is kept visible: "It must not manufacture consensus where sources materially disagree; every disagreement remains source-tagged and visible."

## Does not hold

- the owner's object itself — `L7/peering/public/ingress/peer-1/object-1/` or the private position (NO EDIT, NO DELETE)
- exact source/version identity — `L7/provenance/sources/`
- what the local research concludes about the object — `USO/l5/research/`
- the locally authored definition of the bounded object — `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`
- legal permission to use or disclose the object — `L7/governance/permissions/` and the other governance positions

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"Metadata does not transfer semantic authority." Recording an authority here does not make this repository a second authority for the object, and "No receiving layer thereby becomes the definition's author."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term "L6 provenance" — disposition Split; the "authority" side belongs at `L7/provenance/`.
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# L7/provenance/lineage/

**Object:** Directory for externally relevant lineage and derivation relationships.
**Path:** `L7/provenance/lineage/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for externally relevant lineage and derivation relationships." A record here states that one external object derives from, descends from, was transferred from, or was composed out of another — the relation itself, named and dated. It is one of the four provenance positions the layer owns ("provenance: prior art, sources, lineage, authority").

A person filing here names both ends of the relation and the relation type. The record answers "where did this come from and what is it related to"; it does not answer whether the derivation is sound. The acceptance check is that "Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required."

## Why it exists

"Makes origin and relation traceable without turning lineage into scientific validity." A traceable origin is a boundary fact an outside reader can check; it is kept separate from any lower-layer claim that the derived object is correct.

## Holds

- Externally relevant lineage records: transfers of an object between owners or repositories, derivations of one external object from another, and the relation of a bound peer object to its upstream versions.
- The externally relevant relation between versions of a peer-owned object (the upstream version an existing binding points to and the upstream version a new binding points to). The binding event itself and its supersession are binding history and stay at `USO/l6/history/` and `USO/l6/retired/` ("L6 preserves binding history").

## Does not hold

- the internal chronology of this repository's own work and bindings — `USO/l6/history/`
- retired or superseded local states — `USO/l6/retired/`
- exact source identity and version — `L7/provenance/sources/`
- prior art as antecedent landscape — `L7/provenance/prior-art/`
- who owns or speaks for the related objects — `L7/provenance/authority/`
- any judgment that a derivation is valid — `USO/l5/research/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

The Harmonization Record's supporting-evidence disposition applies: "Git history can support L6 reconstruction, but Git history and scientific memory/provenance are not the same object." A commit graph is not a lineage record; a lineage record here is written prose with explicit ends and relation.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term "L6 provenance" — disposition Split; the external side ("external source, authority, prior art, and public provenance") belongs at `L7/provenance/`.
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# L7/provenance/prior-art/

**Object:** Directory for identified prior art and antecedent work relevant to the bounded object.
**Path:** `L7/provenance/prior-art/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for identified prior art and antecedent work relevant to the bounded object." A record here identifies an antecedent — a standard, paper, prior system, or earlier work — and its relation to the bounded object. It is one of the four provenance positions the layer owns ("provenance: prior art, sources, lineage, authority"), and "record provenance and prior art" is a permitted L7 action.

A person filing here states what the antecedent is, at what version, and how it relates to the bounded object. The identification of the antecedent belongs here; what the local research concludes from the comparison does not. The acceptance check is that "Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required."

## Why it exists

"Makes the antecedent landscape explicit without confusing prior art with the object's locally authored L0 meaning." Under the prior scaffold, prior art sat inside L0; the harmonized specification moves it to the forward face so that an antecedent is never mistaken for a locally authored distinction.

## Holds

- Identified prior art and antecedent work, each with explicit source, version, and the relation it bears to the bounded object.
- The prior-art relation of any definition or object bound at `L7/peering/` ("L7 provenance records source, version, authority, and prior-art relation").

## Does not hold

- the locally authored L0 meaning the prior art is compared against — `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`
- exact source/version identity as such — `L7/provenance/sources/`
- derivation relationships — `L7/provenance/lineage/`
- who owns or speaks for the antecedent — `L7/provenance/authority/`
- the comparison, interpretation, or judgment about whether the antecedent anticipates the local work — `USO/l5/research/`
- the antecedent's text itself when it is a bound peer object — `L7/peering/public/ingress/peer-1/object-1/` (NO EDIT, NO DELETE)

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.

# FILE: L7/provenance/sources/0.md
*edited by this branch; path is still live on main — 905 words*

# L7/provenance/sources/

**Object:** Directory for source identity and exact source/version references.
**Path:** `L7/provenance/sources/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for source identity and exact source/version references." A record here identifies an external source precisely enough that the same object at the same version can be named again: source, locator, issuer, exact version, retrieval, declared scope, and the local artifact or layer it bears on. The layer owns "provenance: prior art, sources, lineage, authority" and accepts a record when "Source, version, authority, and provenance are explicit where required."

The local table is `L7/provenance/sources/source-ledger.md`. It was relocated in this pass from `USO/l6/provenance/` and is header-only: it carries no rows. Its eight column headings are the fields a row carries:

> Source ID | Source and locator | Issuer / owner | Exact version | Retrieved | Declared scope | Relevant artifact or layer | Status / notes

Its header sentence states the limit the specification also states: "Inclusion records provenance and does not by itself endorse a source or claim."

## The designated curated-source archive: aybllc/l6

The owner designated `aybllc/l6` (an INTERNAL repository; pinned here at commit `53b28f6`) as the archive that serves curated sources to this research program; it is not edited from a consuming repository: it is used to serve sources. This is an implementation fact of the program recorded at the forward face, not part of the architecture; another instantiation of the topology may designate a different archive or none.

What the archive is, in its own words: "Archival copies of primary literature held for research provenance." It holds 1,368 tracked library files filed by the venue printed on the artifact (`library/journals/<venue>/`) or by issuing body (`library/standards/<body>/`), plus `books/`, `captures/`, and `paywall/`. Its record only grows: "`VERIFICATION_LOG.md` is append-only, `ACQUIRE.md` never deletes a row, and corrections are new dated statements beside the old ones — never edits of them." Fixity is recorded PREMIS-style — "the stored digest is NOT the fixity check; each verification run is an Event with a date and outcome" — and its README states the limit in one line: "The integrity check is copyright, not sha256." `ACCESS-ROUTES.md` records how each item was obtained as "a provenance fact, not licence status, which is a different fact"; items obtained through the owner's institutional access are held private under its IL-001-R2 class-2 rule ("never publish, mirror, or attach"), which is a condition for `L7/governance/restrictions/`, not for this directory.

How a source is served from it: by a pointer row, never by a copy. A consuming repository's register carries, per source, an ID, the citation, the `l6` path, the `l6` commit at which that path was read, the access class, and a label; "This directory holds no source files" is the register's own first rule.

The archive names itself "USO rail L6 — Provenance" under the earlier numbering. Seen from a consuming repository's forward face it is an external peer: its binding is at `L7/peering/private/ingress/`, its source rows are here, and its licence conditions are at `L7/governance/`. Nothing here adjudicates what it is inside itself. A gap in its holdings is a row for its own `ACQUIRE.md`, appended by its owner's process, not a file placed in any consuming repository.

**Author note.** Curated sources are served by pointer. A designated archive repository holds the source files; it is bound at private ingress and is read-only from every consuming repository. A consuming repository keeps one ledger row per source at provenance/sources/, with the archive path and the pinned commit, and no source file lives in it.

## Why it exists

"Keeps source identification inspectable at the external face." A source is something an outside reader must be able to check, so its identity sits at the desk rather than inside internal memory.

## Holds

- `source-ledger.md` — one row per exact source/version reference (the ledger's `Exact version` column identifies which version a row names).
- Exact source/version references for any object bound at `L7/peering/`, so that the binding's "object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding" tag has a source row to point to.

## Does not hold

- the source text or object itself — when bound, `L7/peering/public/ingress/peer-1/object-1/` or `L7/peering/private/ingress/peer-1/object-1/` (NO EDIT, NO DELETE); otherwise it stays with its owner
- who owns or speaks for the source — `L7/provenance/authority/`
- what the source says in comparison with local work — `USO/l5/research/`
- copyright, licence, access, or restriction conditions on the source — `L7/governance/`
- the internal history of when a source was adopted, corrected, or dropped — `USO/l6/history/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

A row in the ledger does not make its source valid, and "Publication or legal status does not strengthen scientific evidence."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. The ledger has no rows; that is admissible.
- Inherited term "L6 provenance" — disposition Split: "Internal reconstructable memory remains L6; external source, authority, prior art, and public provenance belong at L7/provenance/."
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 543 words*

# L7/publications/

**Object:** Directory for released publication objects at the forward face.
**Path:** `L7/publications/` · **Layer:** L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE · **Owner:** L7/

## What this directory is

The specification's prose cell: "Directory for released publication objects at the forward face." A publication object here is a released thing with a release identity: the version that left, when, in what form, and under what governance. The layer "owns / represents" "publications and release identity", and of the permitted actions "receive, bind, attribute, disclose, present, publish, release, route", the ones that apply here are present, publish, and release. The directional input is "Outward release: approved L6-backed packages."

A person filing here records the release, not the writing and not the argument. The specification separates the four senses: "Released publications are represented by the L7 publications branch, while provenance and public/legal governance remain separately addressable"; and the public egress row states that "the released publication object itself is held by the L7 publications branch." The acceptance check that governs every release is that "Publication or legal status does not strengthen scientific evidence."

## Why it exists

"Keeps publication release at L7 while writing remains L4, research meaning remains L5, and internal history remains L6." One document may exist in all four senses; each sense is owned by the layer that can state it.

## Holds

- Released publication objects with explicit release identity: source, version, authority, permissions, status, and direction where applicable.
- Released corpus/publication identity — the part of the inherited term "corpus" that the Harmonization Record places here.

## Does not hold

- the writing as produced work — `USO/l4/work/writing/`
- the research meaning, argument, or claim — `USO/l5/research/`
- the internal history of drafts, versions, and supersessions — `USO/l6/history/`; retired releases — `USO/l6/retired/`
- the provenance of a released object — `L7/provenance/`
- the copyright, licence, permission, access, or restriction conditions on a release — `L7/governance/`
- outward exchange toward a specific peer — `L7/peering/public/egress/peer-1/object-1/` or the private egress position
- drafts stamped internal, empty release slots, and material that has not left: none of these is a released publication object

## Boundary / traversal rule

L7 is the forward face outside the USO. It owns the external relations of the bounded object: provenance, governance, peering, and publication state. Public/private exposure and ingress/egress direction remain independent peering distinctions. L7 does not thereby acquire or change the lower-layer scientific meaning.

"Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. No released publication object is filed here at present. The harmonized specification is filed as a produced writing artifact at `USO/l4/work/writing/` (see `USO/l4/work/writing/0.md`); its release state, if the owner releases it, would be represented here.
- Inherited term "corpus" — disposition Split and reserved: "Released corpus/publication identity belongs at L7/publications/; the broader inherited term remains reserved where its exact role is not yet adjudicated." Only the released-identity sense is placed here.
- Machine metadata: described at `L7/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation. The specification's machine-metadata row names "release state" among what metadata may identify; none is enabled here.

# FILE: README.md
*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 153 words*

# 7lm — Seven-Layer Model (7LM)

The scaffold of the Seven-Layer Model research architecture: one canonical agnostic directory topology, one information sheet per directory.

This file is the start, not the documentation. The documentation is the sheets: every directory describes itself in its own `0.md`, and the controlling text is the Harmonized Authoritative Architecture Specification at `USO/l4/work/writing/7lm-harmonized-architecture-specification.md`. Where anything else in this repository differs from the specification, the specification controls.

Read in this order:

1. `L7/0.md` — the desk, the forward face: provenance, governance, peering, publications.
2. `USO/0.md` — the interior: the seven layers `l6/` down to `l0/`, the count rule, the dependency rules, the tree.
3. The `0.md` of every directory beneath them.

The root holds `README.md`, `L7/`, `USO/`, and `.github/` (one CI check, repository infrastructure and not a layer), and nothing else.

The owner's first README, the working sketch, is retired at `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/README.md`. The record of this repository's layout is `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

# FILE: USO/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/0.md` — 1,728 words*

# USO/

**Object:** USO as bounded scientific object.
**Path:** `USO/` · **Layer:** interior root — L0 through L6, the seven interior dependency layers · **Owner:** the bounded scientific object's interior; each interior layer (`USO/l0/` … `USO/l6/`) owns its own representation

The specification pairs the two top-level positions of the repository in two lines: "L7 = Desktop" and "USO = USO as bounded scientific object". This sheet describes the second. The retired README sketch at `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/README.md` is titled "Unified Science Object"; that title is the owner's and is not the specification's wording.

The specification names the second position L8 ("L8 = USO, the bounded scientific object's interior: the research packet on the desk"), keeps USO as the name of what it holds, and names the interior directories L0 through L6, the eight bits L0 through L7 and the extra bit L8. This scaffold's directories remain `USO/` and `l0/` through `l6/` until the rename pass, which is the owner's word; the sheets below quote the paths as they stand.

## What the USO is

The USO is the compartmentalized scientific interior that allows the components/representations of a bounded thing to occupy their correct layer without forcing the entire repository or corpus to share one maturity status. General repository infrastructure is not automatically a layer.

A bounded exemplar can be written or made at L4 while its semantic, state-space, formal, engineering, research, memory/history, provenance/governance, and surface representations remain separately addressable. The layer tree is the discourse structure of a thing, not a command to refile unrelated repository infrastructure.

## Seven-layer count and harmonization rule

L0 through L6 are the seven interior dependency layers. L7 is the numbered external Surface / Forward Face; it participates in traversal but is not an eighth interior dependency layer.

The owner's definition `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/FIRST_IS_ALWAYS_0.md` (FIRST is 0) is why those two sentences agree: the seven layers are the seven semantics FIRST through SEVENTH and the seven magnitudes `l0` through `l6`, so the seventh layer is `l6`; 7 is the magnitude of the desk's position, the eighth reached in traversal.

The coherent principal L0-L7 information sheets control. Repeated contracts and contradictory anchors are superseded; unique notes, cases, rationales, and boundary research are preserved. Questionable inherited concepts are reserved with topology unadjudicated unless this specification expressly places them.

## Topology

L7 is outside the USO and is physically a sibling boundary to USO/. L6 through L0 are the scientific interior. Physical siblinghood and semantic sequence are different relations: crossing L7 <-> L6 is a boundary/peering event, not filesystem nesting. In this repository `L7/` and `USO/` are siblings under the repository root; `L7/0.md` describes the forward face.

The canonical research tree keeps the interior physically flat: l6/, l5/, l4/, l3/, l2/, l1/, and l0/ are siblings under USO/. The arrows, groupings, and visual bands in this outline indicate traversal, dependency, or analytic view; they do not create child-directory containment.

### Directory structure (the USO part of the canonical tree)

Notation: /0 means the directory carries its local 0.md human-readable semantic description. Directory metadata is attached to the directory itself and is therefore not drawn as another branch in the tree.

```text
└── USO/0
    ├── l6/0
    │   ├── history/0
    │   └── retired/0
    ├── l5/0
    │   ├── research/0
    │   │   ├── lanes/0
    │   │   ├── review/0
    │   │   └── findings/0
    │   └── institution/0
    │       ├── peers/0
    │       └── undergraduates/0
    ├── l4/0
    │   └── work/0
    │       ├── writing/0
    │       ├── experiments/0
    │       └── computation/0
    ├── l3/0
    │   └── engineering-mathematics/0
    │       ├── approximation/0
    │       ├── uncertainty/0
    │       └── tolerances/0
    ├── l2/0
    │   └── formal-mathematics/0
    │       ├── relations/0
    │       ├── operators/0
    │       └── proofs/0
    ├── l1/0
    │   └── state-space/0
    └── l0/0
        ├── universality/0
        ├── pedagogy/0
        ├── epistemic/0
        ├── ontology/0
        └── semantics/0
            └── definitions/0
```

Canonical-tree rule. Unused now does not mean removed. The same complete topology is available to Autonomous, Auditonomous, OMMP, classroom research, institutional research, and future research repositories.

A canonical branch may remain empty when unused. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population. Every branch above may be empty in this repository; its presence in the tree does not oblige anyone to fill it.

### Analytic views

| Analytic view | Meaning |
|---|---|
| L6 / L5 upper interior | Memory/history and research/discourse are the upper scientific interior. They remain separate layer owners even when shown as one research-facing band. |
| L4 realization bay | A whole realized architecture may be contained here and may expand internally according to its own native structure without commandeering 7LM layer numbers. |
| L3 / L2 / L1 technical-possibility bundle | Engineering mathematics, exact formalization, and admissible state space can be examined together when the question requires all three dependencies. |
| L3 / L2 nested sub-bundle | Within the broader L3-L2-L1 view, L3-L2 is a tighter engineering/formal sub-bundle when L1 is already pinned and not itself under question. |
| L0 local semantic boundary | L0 is deliberately separated from L1. L0 establishes the local semantic/foundational contract; L1 establishes admissible states under that contract. |

Author note. These bundles are semantic and dependency views, not filesystem nesting and not mandatory traversal paths. The same scientific object may be railed through different subsets of the architecture according to the question being asked, while each representation remains owned by the layer that can state it correctly.

## The interior layers

| Layer | Name as the spec heads it | Core question |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | LAYER 0 — Local semantic/rail version of the bounded thing; origin contract, not a warehouse for external definitions. | What local ontological, semantic, epistemic, and universality distinctions does THIS object establish before an admissible state is declared? |
| L1 | STATE SPACE — Admissible states and static possibility bounds. | What states and distinctions are possible or admissible under the established L0 contract? |
| L2 | FORMAL MATHEMATICS — Exact formal objects and lawful operation over L1. | What follows exactly when lawful operations are defined over L1? |
| L3 | ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS — Approximation, numerical representation, tolerance, uncertainty, and feasibility. | How can the exact L2 relation be made feasible under declared constraints? |
| L4 | LAYER 4 — Conversion / realized work / identifiable output. | What conversion between intellectual property and energy, in either direction, was actually made, written, run, measured, instantiated, operated, manufactured, or recorded? |
| L5 | RESEARCH / DISCOURSE — Interpretation, review, judgment, argument, and teaching. | What may responsibly be concluded, questioned, taught, compared, or decided from the available work? |
| L6 | LIBRARY — Memory, history, reconstruction, retirement, and query orientation. | Can the object's internal history, prior states, failures, corrections, and retirement be reconstructed and correctly oriented for inspection? |

Each layer's full information sheet (Function, Directional inputs, Output, Hard boundary, Machine metadata, Owns / represents, Permitted actions, Acceptance / boundary check, directory prose and rationale) is carried in that layer's root sheet: `USO/l0/0.md` through `USO/l6/0.md`. The spec's topology diagram bands the same layers as L0 SEMANTIC / FOUNDATIONAL, L1 STATE SPACE, L2 FORMAL MATHEMATICS, L3 ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS, L4 CONVERSION / REALIZATION BAY, L5 RESEARCH / DISCOURSE, and L6 LIBRARY / MEMORY. L7 — SURFACE / FORWARD FACE (External provenance, public/legal governance, peering, publications, exposure, and exchange.) is outside the USO and is described at `L7/0.md`.

## Two valid directions

Authorized external inspection inward:

L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0

Scientific construction outward:

L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7

Direction rule. Scientific construction proceeds from L0 outward only as dependencies are established. External inspection begins at L7 and proceeds inward. Description does not become prescription merely because the structure can be traversed.

## Architecture-wide dependency rules

Higher layers do not silently repair lower layers. If a higher-layer object requires different lower-layer meaning, the lower-layer dependency is revised explicitly rather than reinterpreted in place.

Layer ownership is local. A topic may appear at several layers, but each representation belongs at the lowest layer where that representation can be stated correctly.

Change one layer at a time. A correction at one layer does not silently rewrite another layer.

A canonical branch may remain empty when unused. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population.

Directory metadata may enforce a machine rule, but it does not create the human semantic meaning carried by /0. Conversely, /0 does not substitute for machine instructions.

Operational containment and analytic expansion are different relations. A complete architecture may remain owned at one 7LM layer while questions about its origin, justification, development, or effects generate representations at other 7LM layers. The contained architecture is not remapped merely because the inquiry expands.

## Self-description and machine metadata

Standing. Each local 0.md first identifies the directory/object and its path, then supplies that directory's semantic description. Machine instructions belong to the directory metadata, not to the semantic prose.

/0 carries human-readable meaning; metadata carries machine-actionable instruction. Neither replaces the other. This separation keeps semantic description distinct from machine operation.

Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. The one CI check, `.github/workflows/integrity.yml`, is repository infrastructure and not a layer (no unresolved conflict markers; the root canonical, the same in every 7LM repository; every sheet headed by its path); it moves nothing. Machine-readable metadata may support automation, but the architecture does not assume that automation has been enabled or validated. Current research practice may remain manual until the metadata contract is tested.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters at `USO/l0/` and proceeds outward through `USO/l1/` … `USO/l6/` toward `L7/`; inspection enters from `L7/` and proceeds inward through `USO/l6/` toward `USO/l0/`. Scientific construction/prescription proceeds outward: L0 → L1 → L2 → L3 → L4 → L5 → L6 → L7, but only as far as established dependencies justify. Interpretive inspection of an externally owned object proceeds inward: L7 → L6 → L5 → L4 → L3 → L2 → L1 → L0.

# FILE: USO/l0/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L0/0.md` — 1,599 words*

# USO/l0/

**Object:** Local semantic/rail version of the bounded thing; origin contract, not a warehouse for external definitions.
**Path:** `USO/l0/` · **Layer:** L0 — LAYER 0 · **Owner:** USO/l0/, as the owner of the bounded object's locally authored foundational and rail representation.

Layer 0 is the lowest interior layer of the USO. It holds what this bounded object itself establishes before any admissible state is declared: its ontological, semantic, epistemic, and universality distinctions, the pedagogy of how the system is traversed and attached, and the vocabulary or semantic representation this object actually authored. It also holds the exact, immutable bindings by which externally authored semantic inputs reach this object, kept distinct from local authorship. The spec's core question for the layer is: "What local ontological, semantic, epistemic, and universality distinctions does THIS object establish before an admissible state is declared?"

The specification's analytic view states the reason L0 is a separate layer: "L0 is deliberately separated from L1. L0 establishes the local semantic/foundational contract; L1 establishes admissible states under that contract." Everything in this directory is read under that sentence. A canonical branch below may remain empty; presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population, and unused now does not mean removed.

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | What local ontological, semantic, epistemic, and universality distinctions does THIS object establish before an admissible state is declared? |
| Function | Hold the object's locally authored foundational representation and its exact, immutable bindings to externally authored semantic inputs, without confusing the two authorities. |
| Directional inputs | Construction: the bounded object's locally authored distinctions; no higher-layer scientific dependency. Inspection: an external definition first bound at L7 may be traced inward as an exact, immutable, externally owned semantic input to the receiving L0. |
| Output | A local rail-grounded L0 contract sufficient for L1. A rail may remain sparse or gate-only when no local representation has been authored. |
| Hard boundary | A source L0 definition may travel through source L7 egress and receiving L7 ingress to receiving L0. At receiving L0 it remains exact, externally authored, immutable, version-pinned, and explicitly tagged; it is not locally redefined or counted as local authorship. L0 does not own prior art, provenance, public/legal governance, state transition, operator, proof, numerical method, experiment, or research conclusion. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify the local rail or an external semantic binding, including object identity, owner, source layer, version, ingress binding, immutability, gate status, and allowed actions. It may enforce handling constraints, but it must not define or replace semantic meaning. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents
- Ontological, Semantic, Epistemic, and Universality rail boundaries for this object.
- System-level traversal/attachment pedagogy as a directory/function, without making Pedagogy a fifth rail.
- Local vocabulary or semantic representation actually authored by this object.

### Permitted actions
- bind local distinctions to their owning rail
- state ambiguity or unresolved meaning without filling it by guess
- version a changed local distinction rather than silently rewriting prior use

### Acceptance / boundary check
- Every L1 distinction resolves to locally authored L0 meaning or an exact named external semantic binding.
- Every external binding retains object identity, owner, source layer, version, ingress binding, and immutable status.
- No peer definition is locally redefined merely to make L0 look complete.
- No neighboring rail silently supplies another rail's local meaning.

## Directory structure

```text
USO/l0/0
├── universality/0
├── pedagogy/0
├── epistemic/0
├── ontology/0
└── semantics/0
    └── definitions/0
```

**Boundary / traversal rule.** L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| USO/l0/ | Layer 0 container for the bounded object’s locally authored foundational and rail representation. | Keeps foundational meaning distinct from state, formalization, engineering, realized work, research judgment, provenance, and surface exchange. |
| universality/ | Directory for the Universality rail content owned by L0. | Preserves Universality as its own rail instead of letting another rail silently supply it. |
| pedagogy/ | Directory for system-level traversal and attachment pedagogy. | Keeps pedagogy explicit without turning it into a fifth philosophical rail. |
| epistemic/ | Directory for the Epistemic rail content owned by L0. | Keeps epistemic distinctions inside the rail that owns them. |
| ontology/ | Directory for the Ontological rail content owned by L0. | Keeps ontological distinctions inside the rail that owns them. |
| semantics/ | Directory for the Semantic rail content authored by this bounded object. | Keeps local semantic meaning separate from externally owned definitions and later formal representation. |
| semantics/definitions/ | Directory for definitions actually authored by this object under its Semantic rail. | Prevents an external definition from being copied inward and mistaken for locally owned meaning. |

The four rails — Ontological, Semantic, Epistemic, Universality — each have their own directory. Pedagogy has a directory but is not a rail: the spec owns it "as a directory/function, without making Pedagogy a fifth rail." Only `semantics/` has a child, `definitions/`, for definitions actually authored by this object under its Semantic rail.

## In the case of

**Author note.** Current correction: L0/semantics is the semantic version we make of the thing. If we do not make one, the directory is not populated with someone else’s definition.

**Research note.** MOST IMPORTANT: I THINK DRIFT LIVES HERE! NOT ANYWHERE ELSE...(NEEDS A LOT OF STUDY)

**Standing.** This statement is retained as a research hypothesis: semantic drift is expected to originate when the local L0 meaning contract changes or is misapplied. The hypothesis remains open to testing rather than being promoted to a settled rule.

### In the case of:

In the Autonomous exemplar, IEEE/IEC have already been adjudicating relevant definitions through standards. The locally authored semantic definition used by this bounded object still lives at Layer 0, while the discourse, research, and state-space objects are distilled to their own layers. AUTONOMOUS and AUDITONOMOUS are retained as pedagogical exemplars: EXEMPLAR AUTO and EXEMPLAR AUDIT.

**Standing.** In this exemplar, the L0 object is the locally authored semantic definition of Autonomous. IEEE/IEC source definitions and standards remain externally owned inputs bound through L7 peering; their source identity, version, authority, and prior-art relation are represented through L7 provenance. Historical internal use remains reconstructable through L6.

The spec's originating constraint states the Autonomous/Auditonomous relation that this layer must preserve: "Autonomous has one bounded semantic job: state and freeze the narrowest reasonable agreement shared across exact, independently bound standards definitions. It must not manufacture consensus where sources materially disagree; every disagreement remains source-tagged and visible." And: "Auditonomous consumes the pinned Autonomous consensus baseline and authors the Auditonomous difference as an explicit local delta. The baseline and delta remain separately owned and overlaid rather than blended into a dual-owned definition."

**Clarification.** The Autonomous and Auditonomous repositories are implementation work in progress; their current layouts do not control the architecture. Architecture and implementation status are separate claims.

**Clarification (the spec's "Contextual semantic route").** "Source L0 definition → source L7 egress → receiving L7 ingress → receiving L0. A source object's L0 definition is an L7 egress object at its boundary; to us it is L7 ingress directed to our L0. Its semantic identity remains source-owned L0 throughout the exchange."

"The receiver does not have to redefine the peer definition. Every internal representation that uses it carries the exact bound version with an immutable external-ownership tag: object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding. An upstream change creates a new binding; it does not mutate the pinned object."

"Declared semantic-deference rule. For the bounded purpose of interpreting the peer, the receiver intentionally takes the owner's exact bound definition as authoritative for what the owner means and does not re-prove or locally redefine that meaning. This declared trust is not a claim that the definition is objectively true, not an endorsement of downstream claims, and not a transfer of authorship."

In practice: a bound external definition is referenced at L0 with its tag; it is not rewritten into `semantics/definitions/`. The binding itself, its source identity, version, authority, and prior-art relation are represented at `L7/peering/` and `L7/provenance/`, not here. Where this object has authored no local representation, the rail stays "sparse or gate-only" rather than being filled with someone else's definition.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction begins at `USO/l0/` — the bounded object's locally authored distinctions, with no higher-layer scientific dependency — and leaves toward `USO/l1/`; inspection enters from `USO/l1/` (having begun at `L7/` and proceeded inward) and ends here, where an external definition first bound at `L7/` is traced to its receiving L0 as an exact, immutable, externally owned semantic input. These are the spec's two valid directions, L0→L1→…→L7 for scientific construction and L7→L6→…→L0 for authorized external inspection; "Description does not become prescription merely because the structure can be traversed."

# FILE: USO/l0/epistemic/0.md
*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 396 words*

# USO/l0/epistemic/

**Object:** Directory for the Epistemic rail content owned by L0.
**Path:** `USO/l0/epistemic/` · **Layer:** L0 — LAYER 0 · **Owner:** USO/l0/

## What this directory is

Directory for the Epistemic rail content owned by L0. The Epistemic rail is one of the four rails the L0 sheet names as this object's own boundaries. What is held here is the epistemic distinction the bounded object itself establishes before an admissible state is declared at `USO/l1/`.

In practice a person filing here records an epistemic distinction this object authored, binds it to this rail, and where meaning is ambiguous or unresolved states so rather than filling it by guess; a changed distinction is versioned, not rewritten in place. An epistemic distinction is not a research conclusion, an evidence relation, or a verdict on a claim: L0 does not own "experiment, or research conclusion," and those belong above.

## Why it exists

Keeps epistemic distinctions inside the rail that owns them. Without this directory the epistemic standing of the object's meaning would be supplied silently by semantics or ontology, which the acceptance check "No neighboring rail silently supplies another rail's local meaning" forbids.

## Holds

- Epistemic-rail distinctions locally authored by this bounded object.
- Statements of ambiguity or unresolved meaning, recorded as unresolved.
- Versioned prior epistemic distinctions where a distinction has changed.
- References to any externally owned epistemic input, carried as an exact named external semantic binding with object identity, owner, source layer, version, ingress binding, and immutable status.

## Does not hold

- Externally owned definitions copied inward — bound through `L7/peering/`, represented at `L7/provenance/`.
- Ontological, semantic, or universality distinctions — `USO/l0/ontology/`, `USO/l0/semantics/`, `USO/l0/universality/`.
- Admissible states — `USO/l1/state-space/`.
- Research judgment, review, or findings about what is known — `USO/l5/research/`.
- Reconstructable development history and corrections beyond the versioned distinction itself — `USO/l6/history/`; superseded distinctions no longer current — `USO/l6/retired/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. A rail may remain sparse or gate-only when no local representation has been authored.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l0/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l0/ontology/

**Object:** Directory for the Ontological rail content owned by L0.
**Path:** `USO/l0/ontology/` · **Layer:** L0 — LAYER 0 · **Owner:** USO/l0/

## What this directory is

Directory for the Ontological rail content owned by L0. The Ontological rail is one of the four rails the L0 sheet names as this object's own boundaries. What is held here is the ontological distinction the bounded object itself establishes, stated before an admissible state is declared. The spec separates the two steps: "L0 establishes the local semantic/foundational contract; L1 establishes admissible states under that contract."

In practice a person filing here records an ontological distinction this object authored, binds it to this rail, states unresolved meaning as unresolved rather than filling it by guess, and versions a changed distinction rather than rewriting prior use. An ontological distinction here is not a state, a coordinate description, or a formal structure; those belong to `USO/l1/` and `USO/l2/`.

## Why it exists

Keeps ontological distinctions inside the rail that owns them. The acceptance check "No neighboring rail silently supplies another rail's local meaning" is satisfied only if the ontological distinctions have a directory of their own.

## Holds

- Ontological-rail distinctions locally authored by this bounded object.
- Statements of ambiguity or unresolved meaning, recorded as unresolved.
- Versioned prior ontological distinctions where a distinction has changed.
- References to any externally owned ontological input, carried as an exact named external semantic binding with object identity, owner, source layer, version, ingress binding, and immutable status.

## Does not hold

- Externally owned definitions or ontologies copied inward — bound through `L7/peering/`, represented at `L7/provenance/`.
- Semantic, epistemic, or universality distinctions — `USO/l0/semantics/`, `USO/l0/epistemic/`, `USO/l0/universality/`.
- Admissible states, possibilities, or exclusions — `USO/l1/state-space/`.
- Exact relations, operators, or proofs — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`, `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/`, `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/`.
- Realized work of any kind — `USO/l4/work/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory. An external ontology is bound and tagged, never re-authored here.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. A rail may remain sparse or gate-only when no local representation has been authored.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l0/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l0/pedagogy/

**Object:** Directory for system-level traversal and attachment pedagogy.
**Path:** `USO/l0/pedagogy/` · **Layer:** L0 — LAYER 0 · **Owner:** USO/l0/

## What this directory is

Directory for system-level traversal and attachment pedagogy. The L0 sheet owns "System-level traversal/attachment pedagogy as a directory/function, without making Pedagogy a fifth rail." This directory is that function: the place where the bounded object explains how it is traversed (construction outward from L0, inspection inward from L7) and how it attaches to other objects (the contextual semantic route through L7 egress and ingress, and the peer-1/object-1 form of that attachment), stated as teaching, not as a rule that changes what any layer owns.

In practice a person filing here writes the explanation a reader needs to walk this object's layers and its peering relations correctly. Pedagogy here is a directory and a function; it is not a rail, and it supplies no rail's local meaning. The spec's direction rule bounds what pedagogy may do: "Description does not become prescription merely because the structure can be traversed."

## Why it exists

Keeps pedagogy explicit without turning it into a fifth philosophical rail. The four rails carry distinctions; this directory carries the explanation of how the whole is used, so that explanation is never mistaken for a fifth source of foundational meaning.

## Holds

- System-level traversal pedagogy: how construction proceeds L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7 and how authorized external inspection proceeds L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0.
- Attachment pedagogy: how this object depends on another "without owning, copying, redefining, or maintaining both objects' foundational semantics," and the route "Source L0 definition → source L7 egress → receiving L7 ingress → receiving L0."
- The exemplar reading the spec retains for teaching: "AUTONOMOUS and AUDITONOMOUS are retained as pedagogical exemplars: EXEMPLAR AUTO and EXEMPLAR AUDIT," whose repositories "are implementation work in progress; their current layouts do not control the architecture."

## Does not hold

- Rail distinctions of any kind — `USO/l0/ontology/`, `USO/l0/semantics/`, `USO/l0/epistemic/`, `USO/l0/universality/`.
- Locally authored definitions — `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`.
- Research interpretation, institutional debate, or pedagogical framing of an inquiry — `USO/l5/research/` (the spec's L5 row in the OSI-inquiry table lists "pedagogical framing" among representations an inquiry may generate at L5).
- The peering records themselves — `L7/peering/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory. Pedagogy explains traversal and attachment; it does not perform a binding, declare a state, or settle a reserved term.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- The prior scaffold had no pedagogy directory; no inherited disposition touches this path.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l0/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l0/semantics/

**Object:** Directory for the Semantic rail content authored by this bounded object.
**Path:** `USO/l0/semantics/` · **Layer:** L0 — LAYER 0 · **Owner:** USO/l0/

## What this directory is

Directory for the Semantic rail content authored by this bounded object. The Semantic rail is one of the four rails the L0 sheet names, and it is the rail the spec's boundary rule names directly: "L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing." The spec's author note fixes the reading: "Current correction: L0/semantics is the semantic version we make of the thing. If we do not make one, the directory is not populated with someone else’s definition."

In practice a person filing here records the semantic representation this object actually authored — its own vocabulary, its own reading of what its terms mean — binds it to this rail, states ambiguity as ambiguity, and versions a changed distinction rather than rewriting prior use. Definitions actually authored by this object go one level down, in `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`. An externally owned definition that this object uses is referenced with its immutable external-ownership tag; it is not rewritten here, and it is not "counted as local authorship."

## Why it exists

Keeps local semantic meaning separate from externally owned definitions and later formal representation. The two things this directory is kept apart from have their own owners: external definitions at `L7/`, and the formal representation of the same meaning at `USO/l2/`.

## Holds

- Semantic-rail representation locally authored by this bounded object.
- The child directory `definitions/` for definitions actually authored by this object.
- Statements of ambiguity or unresolved meaning, recorded as unresolved.
- Versioned prior semantic distinctions where a distinction has changed.
- References to externally owned semantic inputs, each carried as an exact named external semantic binding with object identity, owner, source layer, version, ingress binding, and immutable status — the reference, not the copied definition.

## Does not hold

- Externally owned definitions copied inward — bound through `L7/peering/` (ingress) and represented at `L7/provenance/`.
- Prior art — `L7/provenance/prior-art/`.
- Ontological, epistemic, or universality distinctions — `USO/l0/ontology/`, `USO/l0/epistemic/`, `USO/l0/universality/`.
- Admissible states — `USO/l1/state-space/`.
- Exact formulas, relations, operators, or proofs — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`.
- Applied, tolerant, adaptive, or engineering formulas — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`.
- Research claims and thesis statements — `USO/l5/research/lanes/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory. The L0 acceptance check applies here in full: "No peer definition is locally redefined merely to make L0 look complete."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. A rail may remain sparse or gate-only when no local representation has been authored.
- semantic-formulas: Split and reserved. "Semantic reading remains L0; an exact formula is L2; an applied, tolerant, adaptive, or engineering formula is L3. The inherited compound term remains searchable."
- The research hypothesis recorded at `USO/l0/` (semantic drift expected to originate when the local L0 meaning contract changes or is misapplied) remains open to testing; this sheet does not localize it to any rail.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l0/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l0/semantics/definitions/

**Object:** Directory for definitions actually authored by this object under its Semantic rail.
**Path:** `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/` · **Layer:** L0 — LAYER 0 · **Owner:** USO/l0/

## What this directory is

Directory for definitions actually authored by this object under its Semantic rail. A definition filed here is one this bounded object wrote; it is "Local vocabulary or semantic representation actually authored by this object," bound to the Semantic rail at `USO/l0/semantics/`. A definition this object uses but did not write is not filed here. It arrives as an immutable external binding — "exact, externally authored, immutable, version-pinned, and explicitly tagged; it is not locally redefined or counted as local authorship" — and is referenced with its tag: object identity, owner, source layer, version, ingress binding, and immutable status.

In practice a person filing here adds a locally authored definition, states unresolved meaning as unresolved rather than filling it by guess, and versions a changed definition rather than silently rewriting prior use. The Harmonization Record ratifies this placement: "Locally authored definitions belong at USO/l0/semantics/definitions/; peer definitions arrive as immutable external bindings."

## Why it exists

Prevents an external definition from being copied inward and mistaken for locally owned meaning. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory.

## Holds

- Definitions actually authored by this bounded object.
- Versioned prior forms of a locally authored definition where the definition has changed.
- Statements that a term's local meaning is ambiguous or unresolved, recorded as such.
- The inherited controlled-definitions tables below, carried forward as locally authored working meanings.

## Does not hold

- Peer or standards definitions copied inward — they are bound at `L7/peering/` and their source identity, version, authority, and prior-art relation are represented at `L7/provenance/`.
- Prior art — `L7/provenance/prior-art/`.
- Semantic representation that is not a definition — `USO/l0/semantics/`.
- Exact formulas — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`; applied or engineering formulas — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`.
- Research claims and thesis statements — `USO/l5/research/lanes/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory. "No peer definition is locally redefined merely to make L0 look complete."

## Locally authored definitions

- `FIRST_IS_ALWAYS_0.md` — the owner's definition: "First is always 0", with its table (FIRST is 0, SECOND is 1, THIRD is 2, FOURTH is 3). Ordinals are semantics, numbers are magnitudes, and the relation is "is". In this tree it is the rule that makes the model's name and its paths agree: the seven layers are the seven semantics FIRST through SEVENTH and the seven magnitudes `l0` through `l6`, so the seventh layer is `l6`; the first thing at any position is its zero, `0.md`; and 7 is a magnitude, the position of the desk, the eighth reached in traversal, which is why `L7/` is numbered and not a layer.

## Inherited controlled definitions

The two tables below are the controlled-definitions tables inherited from the prior scaffold, whose `definitions/0.md` is retired at `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/USO/l0/definitions/0.md`. The rows are carried, not re-adjudicated; the retired file's other prose is not carried.

| Term | Working meaning | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| agnostic | Committed to structure and handling rather than to any particular theory, tool, vendor, or domain, so material can be held without being endorsed. | Project working use |
| USO | The local seven-layer corpus at uso/l0 through uso/l6; the term operationally names that corpus, and expansion of the acronym remains author-controlled and is not inferred here. | Project rule |
| layer | One numbered band of the corpus, L0 through L6, fixing what kind of statement may be owned there and what it must name from below. | Project rule |
| Scientific Desktop | The L7 working surface outside the USO where intake, pinned authority bindings, compositions, and process lessons are handled before anything is routed into a layer. | Project rule |
| artifact | A concrete, referenceable object in this repository or in an owning repository; one thing can correspond to several artifacts under different roles or contexts. | Project working use |
| claim | A true-or-false statement about limitations on the values of an unambiguously defined property under stated conditions, always carrying an epistemic status. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E) |
| authority artifact | An artifact an owner designates as controlling for a stated scope and version, which consumers may bind to but never rewrite in place. | Project rule |
| semantic authority | The standing to fix what a term or definition means for a stated scope; held by an owner, and never acquired by a consumer through use, mapping, similarity, or custody. | Project rule |
| owner | The agent or repository bearing responsibility for an artifact's existence and content, and therefore the only party that can correct it. | Adapted from PROV-DM (W3C Recommendation 30 April 2013) |
| consumer | Any layer, composition, or repository that uses an artifact it does not own and must therefore name it rather than restate or repair it. | Project rule |
| scope | The stated extent within which a definition, claim, or designation applies; a definition suitable for general application may need a more specific one for a restricted application. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E), Clause 1.1 |
| term | A linguistic construct that refers to an entity; the sign used, not the meaning carried. | Adapted from ISO/IEC 2382:2015 |
| label | A short tag attached to an entry to record its status or handling; a label is not a definition and carries no semantic authority. | Project working use |
| lexical change | A change to wording, spelling, or formatting that does not alter a controlling technical use, and therefore requires no entry in this list. | Project rule |
| concept | The abstract unit of meaning that determines category membership, distinct from any term used to express it. | Adapted from ISO/IEC 2382:2015 |
| definition | A statement fixing the essential delimiting characteristics of a concept, such that the term can be replaced by it without introducing contradiction or circularity. | Adapted from JCGM 200:2012 (VIM) |
| source-scoped meaning | A meaning that holds only within the source, version, and context that issued it, recorded as its own row and never merged with a competing meaning. | Project rule |
| controlling use | The meaning designated to govern a NAMED scope after explicit adjudication; outside that scope other recorded meanings remain live. | Project rule |
| canonical | Owner-designated as controlling for a stated scope and version; it does not mean proven, validated, or universally true. | Project rule |
| interpretation | An assignment of meaning to a formal or textual structure; formal consistency under one interpretation does not select that interpretation. | Project rule |
| definition status | The axis recording how settled a definition is, such as provisional, source-scoped, adjudicated controlling, or superseded; separate from any claim's epistemic status. | Project rule |
| terminology | The set of designations used in a subject field together with the practice of establishing and maintaining them; no source read this pass defines it, so this is the scaffold's working use. | Project working use |
| vocabulary | A terminological dictionary containing designations and definitions from one or more specific subject fields. | Adapted from JCGM 200:2012 (VIM), quoting ISO 1087-1:2000, 3.7.2 |
| controlled vocabulary | A vocabulary restricted to an agreed set of terms, so that statements governed by it may use only those terms; no source read this pass defines it, so this is the scaffold's working use. | Project working use |
| taxonomy | A scheme that partitions a body of knowledge into classes and defines the relationships among the pieces. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E) |
| thesaurus | A structured vocabulary that records relations between terms such as synonymy, broader, narrower, and related; no source read this pass defines it, so this is the scaffold's working use. | Project working use |
| ontology | The logical structure of the terms used to describe a domain of knowledge, including both the definitions of the applicable terms and their relationships. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E) |
| type | A predicate characterizing a collection of things; asserting a type is a membership claim, not a warrant for the content typed. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E) |
| syntax | The structural components of a language and the rules defining how its constructs may be assembled into sentences. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 31320-2:2012(E) |
| semantics | The meaning of the syntactic components of a language, fixed separately from the rules that make a statement well-formed. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 31320-2:2012(E) |
| semantic mapping | A schema of metadata labels applied to data so it can be introduced into a corpus, that sense adapted from IEEE Std 2755-2017, 2.3; that it records correspondence only and establishes no identity is this scaffold's own rule, not the standard's. | Adapted from IEEE Std 2755-2017 |
| identity | (as a relation, the sense the Basis anchors) a recorded assertion that two names denote the same thing, so substitution is safe throughout a stated scope; this is the scaffold's own rule and no source read this pass supports the name-to-name direction. (Of an instance) the inherent property that distinguishes an instance from all other instances, independent of its property values or the classes it belongs to, that sense adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 31320-2:2012(E). | Project rule |
| equivalence | A recorded relation asserting that two expressions present aspects of the same thing within a named scope; it does not make them one entity. | Adapted from PROV-DM (W3C Recommendation 30 April 2013) |
| synonym | A term recorded as interchangeable with another within a stated scope, where the interchangeability is asserted by a person and not inferred by a tool. | Project working use |
| quasi-synonym | A term recorded as close to another but not interchangeable, kept as its own row so the difference is not lost. | Project working use |
| broader term | A term recorded as covering a wider extent than another within a stated vocabulary. | Project working use |
| narrower term | A term recorded as covering a narrower extent than another within a stated vocabulary. | Project working use |
| related term | A term cross-referenced as connected to another without being its synonym, its broader term, or its narrower term; no source read this pass fixes that three-way distinction, so this is the scaffold's working use. | Project working use |
| epistemic status | The axis recording how well a claim is supported by evidence appropriate to its type, separate from how settled its definitions are. | Project rule |
| scientific validity | Support for a claim by independent evidence adequate to the claim type; repository custody, an exact hash, authority, internal consistency, and provenance do not supply it. | Project rule |
| integrity | The degree to which a system, product, or component prevents unauthorized access to or modification of programs or data; a custody property, not a truth property. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E) |
| validation | Confirmation through objective evidence that the requirements for a specific intended use or application have been fulfilled, as distinct from verification that specified requirements have been met. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E) |
| contradiction | Two statements that cannot both hold under the same interpretation and the same scope. | Project rule |
| tension | A pull between statements that may each be defensible under differing scopes or interpretations; recorded as such rather than resolved by fiat. | Project rule |
| inconsistency | A set of statements that cannot be jointly satisfied under a stated interpretation; a property of the set, not a verdict on any single member. | Project rule |
| adjudication | The explicit act of deciding, for a named scope, which recorded meaning or which owner controls, with the decision and the non-selected alternatives written down. | Project rule |
| ratification | The recorded acceptance that an adjudicated decision now stands for the named scope; it fixes status, not truth. | Project rule |
| provisional | Admitted and recorded but not yet adjudicated; a provisional object may bear on several artifacts and may have several candidate owners. | Project rule |
| live alternative | A recorded meaning, method, or artifact that remains current even though a composition did not select it; non-selection never sends it to L6 history. | Project rule |
| candidate owner | A layer or repository that might own an artifact once its meaning, scope, and owner are clear; candidacy is not ownership and not routing. | Project rule |
| layer owner | The lowest layer able to state a local artifact without importing unresolved assumptions from a higher layer. | Project rule |
| routing | Moving an adjudicated local artifact from L7 ingress to its owning layer, done only after its current meaning, scope, and owner are sufficiently clear. | Project rule |
| pin | A recorded exact binding in L7 to one specific version of an external authority artifact, holding the reference without copying the content. | Project rule |
| pinned authority | An external authority currently bound by a pin; several may be active at once, and pinning one neither ranks, merges, invalidates, nor supersedes another. | Project rule |
| fixed referent | The specific thing a binding points at, held constant by its fixed aspects so a later reference still lands on the same thing. | Adapted from PROV-DM (W3C Recommendation 30 April 2013) |
| immutable binding | A binding recorded so that it is never edited in place; a change means admitting a new binding and preserving the earlier one plus the successor relation. | Project rule |
| composition | An L7 assembly that uses named artifacts from several owners for a stated purpose; its choices are local to it and are never universal supersession. | Project rule |
| provenance | A record describing the people, institutions, entities, and activities involved in producing, influencing, or delivering a piece of data or a thing. | Adapted from PROV-DM (W3C Recommendation 30 April 2013) |
| rejection | A recorded decision that an admitted object is not accepted for a stated use, with the reason stated; distinct from failure, recall, and supersession. | Project rule |
| failure | Termination of the ability to perform a required function, or performance outside previously specified limits. | Adapted from ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765:2017(E) |
| recall | Withdrawal of a released artifact by its owner, obliging consumers to stop relying on the withdrawn version while the record of it is kept. | Project working use |
| supersession | An owner's replacement of one version by a later one for a stated scope; a composition-specific or application-specific choice is never supersession. | Project rule |
| successor relation | The recorded link from a revised artifact back to the original it revises, with both preserved. | Adapted from PROV-DM (W3C Recommendation 30 April 2013) |
| federation | An arrangement that keeps independently maintained authorities intact and separately governed, so disagreement between them is retained rather than resolved; used here for thesauri, and any extension beyond thesauri is a project analogy that must be labelled as such. | Project analogy |
| thesaurus union | A reconciliation of several source thesauri into one new combined vocabulary, which thereby creates a new authority artifact with its own owner, scope, and version. | Project working use |
| harmonization | Deliberate reconciliation of differing terminologies toward common usage across communities; no source read this pass defines it, so this is the scaffold's working use. | Project working use |
| similarity measure | A computed score of resemblance between terms or texts, usable to prioritize human review and never usable to create semantic authority. | Project working use |
| early merge | Collapsing competing source-scoped meanings, dispositions, or candidate owners into one before adjudication; prohibited in this scaffold. | Project rule |

The second table is the prior scaffold's "Controlled definitions — added by later passes", carried under the same terms.

| Term | Working meaning | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| scale type | The kind of scale a value is reported on, such as nominal, ordinal, interval, ratio, or absolute, fixing which comparisons and operations on that value are meaningful. | Project working use |
| domain | The named subject area, or the set of admissible inputs, that a definition, declaration, model, or method is stated for and outside which it is not asserted to hold. | Project working use |
| value class | The declared kind a value belongs to, fixing what it may range over and which operations are admissible on it, recorded with the declaration rather than inferred from a stored form. | Project working use |
| calibration | The recorded relation between an instrument's indication and the quantity value assigned to it, with its date, conditions, and the authority the assignment is referred to. | Project working use |

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty apart from the inherited tables. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. A rail may remain sparse or gate-only when no local representation has been authored.
- definitions: Ratified. "Locally authored definitions belong at USO/l0/semantics/definitions/; peer definitions arrive as immutable external bindings."
- The inherited rows are not re-adjudicated here. Their status under the harmonized specification — including whether any "Adapted from" row is to be re-expressed as an L7 binding, and whether the rows' prior-scaffold paths and terms are to be updated — is for the owner to decide row by row.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l0/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# First is always 0

**Owner's definition, 2026-09-06.** Quoted as written: "First is always 0". Given with a table of three columns, transcribed exactly as shown:

| semantic | > | magnitude |
|---|---|---|
| FIRST | is | 0 |
| SECOND | is | 1 |
| THIRD | is | 2 |
| FOURTH | is | 3 |

And, the same day, quoted as written: "layer 7 will always be layer 6 :)".

**Standing.** A locally authored definition of this object, the Seven-Layer Model, under its Semantic rail. Ordinals are semantics; numbers are magnitudes; the relation between them is "is". Where the definition is applied in the tree is stated by the sheets that cite it: `USO/0.md`, `L7/0.md`, `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/0.md`, `L7/peering/0.md`. The record is `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md` §8, which also carries the owner's account of the tree's names as design, not decoration.

# USO/l0/universality/

**Object:** Directory for the Universality rail content owned by L0.
**Path:** `USO/l0/universality/` · **Layer:** L0 — LAYER 0 · **Owner:** USO/l0/

## What this directory is

Directory for the Universality rail content owned by L0. Universality is one of the four rails the L0 sheet names — "Ontological, Semantic, Epistemic, and Universality rail boundaries for this object" — and this directory is where the universality distinctions this bounded object establishes are held, before any admissible state is declared at `USO/l1/`.

In practice a person filing here records a universality distinction this object actually authored, binds it to this rail, states any ambiguity or unresolved extent without filling it by guess, and versions a changed distinction rather than rewriting prior use. A universality claim that depends on a proof, an experiment, a numerical method, or a research conclusion is not stated here; L0 does not own those.

## Why it exists

Preserves Universality as its own rail instead of letting another rail silently supply it. The L0 acceptance check that "No neighboring rail silently supplies another rail's local meaning" is the reason this rail has a directory of its own rather than being folded into ontology or semantics.

## Holds

- Universality-rail distinctions locally authored by this bounded object.
- Statements of ambiguity or unresolved universality, recorded as unresolved.
- Versioned prior universality distinctions where a distinction has changed.
- References to any externally owned universality input, carried as an exact named external semantic binding with object identity, owner, source layer, version, ingress binding, and immutable status.

## Does not hold

- Externally owned definitions copied inward — bound through `L7/peering/` and represented at `L7/provenance/`.
- Ontological, semantic, or epistemic distinctions — `USO/l0/ontology/`, `USO/l0/semantics/`, `USO/l0/epistemic/`.
- Admissible states or possibility bounds — `USO/l1/state-space/`.
- Proofs or exact relations that would establish generality — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`.
- Research conclusions about the object's generality — `USO/l5/research/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L0/semantics contains the semantic representation authored by the bounded thing. An externally owned definition is not copied into L0 merely to populate the directory. The same applies to this rail: an external universality statement is not copied here to make the rail look complete.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. The L0 output rule applies: "A rail may remain sparse or gate-only when no local representation has been authored."
- The prior scaffold had no universality directory; no inherited disposition touches this path.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l0/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.
