# FILE: USO/l1/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L1/0.md` — 1,123 words*

# USO/l1/

**Object:** Admissible states and static possibility bounds.
**Path:** `USO/l1/` · **Layer:** L1 — STATE SPACE · **Owner:** USO/l1/ — this layer owns the admissible state-space representation grounded in L0: what may exist or be represented before lawful operation is introduced.

L1 is the layer that freezes the board. Under the L0 contract established at `USO/l0/`, L1 states which states and distinctions are possible or admissible, which are excluded, and which invariants and identity conditions hold statically. It performs no operation on those states. The spec's analytic view states the separation directly: "L0 is deliberately separated from L1. L0 establishes the local semantic/foundational contract; L1 establishes admissible states under that contract." The same analytic views place L1 in the "L3 / L2 / L1 technical-possibility bundle — Engineering mathematics, exact formalization, and admissible state space can be examined together when the question requires all three dependencies," and note that "L3-L2 is a tighter engineering/formal sub-bundle when L1 is already pinned and not itself under question"; "These bundles are semantic and dependency views, not filesystem nesting and not mandatory traversal paths." The core question is: "What states and distinctions are possible or admissible under the established L0 contract?"

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | What states and distinctions are possible or admissible under the established L0 contract? |
| Function | Freeze the board: dimensions, admissible states, invariants, exclusions, equivalence/identity conditions, and static compatibility. |
| Directional inputs | Construction: the current L0 contract. Inspection: higher-layer evidence may trigger a question or proposed revision, but cannot silently change admitted states. |
| Output | An L1 state-space contract defining what L2 may represent and operate upon. |
| Hard boundary | No carrying, transition, selection, learning, calculation, propagation, or transformation. Formal operation begins at L2. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify the state-space version, pinning/immutability status, admission checks, and compatibility validation. It may enforce the frozen board, but it does not create the states or their meaning. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents
- admissible and excluded states
- dimensions, domains, ranges, equivalence classes, identity conditions
- static invariants and compatibility constraints

### Permitted actions
- admit, exclude, distinguish, bound, freeze
- test state membership and compatibility
- issue a new version when the possibility landscape changes

### Acceptance / boundary check
- Every admitted state is grounded in L0.
- Invariants are mutually compatible or the collision is explicit.
- Changes return to L1 and create a new version.

## Directory structure

```text
USO/l1/0
└── state-space/0
```

**Boundary / traversal rule.** L1 establishes the admissible state space. Formal operation begins at L2.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| USO/l1/ | Layer 1 container for the admissible state-space representation grounded in L0. | Keeps static possibility separate from the formal operations that begin at L2. |
| state-space/ | Directory for admissible and excluded states, dimensions, invariants, identity conditions, and static compatibility. | Establishes what may exist or be represented before lawful operation is introduced. |

## In the case of

In the case of: State-space containment and scientific claim boundaries

(The spec prints the four headings below at H2; their depth is reduced here only to nest under this sheet's section. Words are unchanged.)

### State-space boundary — leaving the declared state space

A valid object or transition must remain inside the declared state space and its admissibility rules.

If a result falls outside the declared state space, then either:

- the construction is invalid under the current model; or
- the current state space is incomplete for the phenomenon, and a new explicitly justified extension must be proposed.

The model may not silently step outside its state space and still claim continuity with the same system.

### Mathematical permission is not automatic scientific or engineering permission

A mathematical construction is allowed to remain mathematically valid even when no engineering realization exists.

The construction stops at the first unresolved required bridge.

That is not rejection. It is honest placement.

Example used in session: peer-reviewed FTL/warp-drive mathematics may be legitimate mathematics while still lacking an engineering bridge. The math can stay where it is. It does not receive a free promotion into realizable propulsion.

### No exotic capability gets a free explanatory advantage

FTL, unlimited power, perfect information, zero latency, infinite endurance, and similar assumptions may be studied conditionally.

They may not be used as free empirical resources unless the capability itself carries its own evidentiary and engineering burden.

The session rule became:

> Build whatever the math permits. Claim only what the layer has earned.

### Observable science is bounded without forbidding speculation

The architecture is not anti-speculation.

Hypotheses may be created and mathematically explored.

The discipline is that unobserved or unengineered capability cannot be silently promoted downward into the foundational or physical claim space.

**State-space and claim boundaries**

An object or formal operation may not silently leave the declared state space and still claim continuity with the same L1 contract. An out-of-space result either fails under the current model or motivates an explicit proposal for a revised state space.

Mathematical permission is not automatic engineering or empirical permission. A mathematically valid object may stop at L2. Engineering feasibility must be earned at L3, realized work at L4, and scientific interpretation at L5. Packaging or publicity at L7 never promotes maturity by itself.

The architecture therefore permits speculation while making maturity explicit: build whatever the mathematics legitimately permits; claim only what the completed handoffs have earned.

**Author note.** STATE and IMMUTABLE remain useful descriptive/mnemonic language, but the controlling layer name is State Space.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters from `USO/l0/` and leaves toward `USO/l2/`; inspection enters from `USO/l2/` and proceeds toward `USO/l0/`. This follows the spec's two valid directions — scientific construction outward L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7, and authorized external inspection inward L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0 — and its rule that "Higher layers do not silently repair lower layers": higher-layer evidence may trigger a question or proposed revision of the admitted states, but the change returns to L1 and creates a new version.

# USO/l1/state-space/

**Object:** Directory for admissible and excluded states, dimensions, invariants, identity conditions, and static compatibility.
**Path:** `USO/l1/state-space/` · **Layer:** L1 — STATE SPACE · **Owner:** USO/l1/

## What this directory is

Directory for admissible and excluded states, dimensions, invariants, identity conditions, and static compatibility. It is the one child of `USO/l1/` and the place where the L1 state-space contract is written: the output the layer sheet defines as "An L1 state-space contract defining what L2 may represent and operate upon."

In practice, a person filing here does one of the layer's permitted actions — "admit, exclude, distinguish, bound, freeze", "test state membership and compatibility", or "issue a new version when the possibility landscape changes" — and nothing else. Every admitted state is grounded in L0 (`USO/l0/`). Invariants are mutually compatible or the collision is explicit. Changes return to L1 and create a new version.

## Why it exists

Establishes what may exist or be represented before lawful operation is introduced. The layer's rationale follows from this: keeping static possibility here "Keeps static possibility separate from the formal operations that begin at L2."

## Holds

- admissible and excluded states
- dimensions, domains, ranges, equivalence classes, identity conditions
- static invariants and compatibility constraints
- the state-space version issued when the possibility landscape changes ("Changes return to L1 and create a new version."); an out-of-space result "motivates an explicit proposal for a revised state space" and the revision, when adopted, is that new version
- an explicit record of any invariant collision ("Invariants are mutually compatible or the collision is explicit.")

## Does not hold

- the semantic/foundational contract the states are grounded in — `USO/l0/` (locally authored definitions at `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`)
- carrying, transition, selection, learning, calculation, propagation, or transformation — "Formal operation begins at L2": `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/` (relations/, operators/, proofs/)
- approximation, numerical representation, tolerance, uncertainty, and feasibility — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`
- realized work, computation as run, or written artifacts — `USO/l4/work/`
- research judgment about whether an admitted state is scientifically warranted — `USO/l5/research/`
- superseded state-space versions kept as internal history — `USO/l6/`
- externally owned state-space definitions; these remain owned by their source and are bound through the L7 boundary (`L7/peering/`) rather than copied here
- the reserved terms "origidity; certainty; intolerance" — reserved as candidate L1 semantics with topology unadjudicated; this directory is not their home

## Boundary / traversal rule

L1 establishes the admissible state space. Formal operation begins at L2.

Hard boundary of the layer: No carrying, transition, selection, learning, calculation, propagation, or transformation. Formal operation begins at L2. Directional inputs: Construction: the current L0 contract. Inspection: higher-layer evidence may trigger a question or proposed revision, but cannot silently change admitted states.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- "origidity; certainty; intolerance" — Reserved: "Candidate L1 semantics preserved; topology remains unadjudicated." Given no home here; see `USO/l1/0.md`.
- "STATE and IMMUTABLE remain useful descriptive/mnemonic language, but the controlling layer name is State Space."
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l1/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l2/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L2/0.md` — 1,177 words*

# USO/l2/

**Object:** Exact formal objects and lawful operation over L1.
**Path:** `USO/l2/` · **Layer:** L2 — FORMAL MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l2/ — this layer owns the exact formal representation of the bounded thing and the lawful operations defined over its L1 state space.

L2 is the layer of the scientific interior where exact formal objects are stated over the admissible states that `USO/l1/` has frozen. It asks the spec's core question: "What follows exactly when lawful operations are defined over L1?" Everything here is exact. The layer states notation, types, relations, operators, constructors, carriers, exact models, derivations, and proof obligations; it does not choose meaning (that is `USO/l0/`), it does not admit or exclude states (that is `USO/l1/`), and it does not approximate, tolerate, measure, or realize (those begin at `USO/l3/` and `USO/l4/`).

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | What follows exactly when lawful operations are defined over L1? |
| Function | Define notation, types, relations, operators, constructors, carriers, exact models, derivations, and proof obligations. |
| Directional inputs | Construction: the L1 state-space contract. Inspection: higher-layer work may expose a formal obligation, but the L2 object must still be stated over L1. |
| Output | An exact L2 formal contract supplying L3 with behavior, invariants, formulas, and unresolved proof obligations. |
| Hard boundary | No engineering tolerance, approximation quality, physical realization, measurement, or empirical truth is claimed here. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify formal artifact type, dependencies, proof/test status, domain/codomain pointers, and execution restrictions. It assists machines without replacing the formal or semantic statement. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents
- formal symbols and types
- relations, functions, operators, gates, constructors, carriers, transition systems
- axioms, definitions, lemmas, theorems, derivations, exact formulas

### Permitted actions
- formalize, derive, prove, type-check, construct, test closure
- declare domain/codomain/partiality
- mark conjectural or unproved claims explicitly

### Acceptance / boundary check
- Every symbol binds to L0 meaning and L1 admissibility.
- Every operation has declared domain/codomain or partiality.
- Exact results are supported by proof/derivation or marked as unproved.

## Directory structure

```text
USO/l2/0
└── formal-mathematics/0
    ├── relations/0
    ├── operators/0
    └── proofs/0
```

**Boundary / traversal rule.** Engineering tolerance, numerical approximation, and numerical uncertainty belong to L3, not to L2 exact formal ownership.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| USO/l2/ | Layer 2 container for exact formal representation and lawful operation over L1. | Separates exact mathematical structure from engineering approximation and empirical realization. |
| formal-mathematics/ | Directory for the exact formal system: symbols, types, models, derivations, and obligations. | Keeps formal mathematics grouped under one exact owner before engineering choices are introduced. |
| relations/ | Directory for exact relations among admitted formal objects. | Keeps relational structure distinguishable from the operators that act and the proofs that warrant claims. |
| operators/ | Directory for exact operators, functions, gates, constructors, and other lawful formal actions. | Keeps operation explicit rather than burying it inside prose or engineering implementation. |
| proofs/ | Directory for proofs, lemmas, derivations, and explicit unproved obligations. | Keeps warrant for exact claims inspectable and separate from the claim’s notation or later numerical realization. |

## In the case of

**Author note.** Current correction: “finite tolerance” is not an L2 ownership rule. Engineering tolerance and numerical uncertainty belong at L3.

The spec's L1 sheet fixes the lower edge of this layer and is restated here only by its boundary rule: "L1 establishes the admissible state space. Formal operation begins at L2." The spec's L1 cases state that "A mathematically valid object may stop at L2. Engineering feasibility must be earned at L3, realized work at L4, and scientific interpretation at L5." An object that stops here is honestly placed; it receives no promotion by being exact. Whether a particular formula is exact or applied is not settled by this sheet; the spec's L3 material that bears on this edge is quoted at `USO/l3/`, not here.

## How the layer is used

The three rows of the acceptance check are the whole test for filing here. A symbol that binds to no L0 meaning or to no admitted L1 state is not yet an L2 object; the dependency is raised at `USO/l0/` or `USO/l1/`, not repaired here. An operation without a declared domain, codomain, or partiality is incomplete. An exact result without a proof or derivation is filed as unproved, explicitly, and is carried in the contract as an unresolved proof obligation. What leaves this layer is the exact L2 formal contract that `USO/l3/` receives: behavior, invariants, formulas, and the unresolved proof obligations, stated without tolerance.

The interior stays physically flat: `USO/l2/` is a sibling of `USO/l1/` and `USO/l3/` under `USO/`. The spec's analytic views — the L3 / L2 / L1 technical-possibility bundle and the tighter L3 / L2 sub-bundle "when L1 is already pinned and not itself under question" — are "selectable analytic rails, not new containers and not a requirement that every inquiry traverse every layer." They do not nest this layer inside another.

Where a thing is examined rather than built, this layer owns the representation the spec assigns it in the OSI-origin inquiry table: "Exact formal relations, operators, proofs, or formal constraints used to state the architecture where such formalization is actually warranted." A contained architecture realized at `USO/l4/` is not remapped to this layer because a formal question about it arises here.

Canonical-tree rule, in this layer's words: every branch drawn in the directory structure above is an admissible capability of this layer, not a population requirement. `formal-mathematics/` and its three children may stand empty for as long as no exact object of the bounded thing has been stated; unused now does not mean removed.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

Of the split term semantic-formulas, only the exact-formula part lands in this layer; the semantic reading stays at `USO/l0/` and the applied, tolerant, adaptive, or engineering formula goes to `USO/l3/`. This sheet does not decide which side any particular inherited formula falls on.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters from `USO/l1/` and leaves toward `USO/l3/`; inspection enters from `USO/l3/` and proceeds toward `USO/l1/`. These are the spec's two valid directions — scientific construction outward L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7 "only as far as established dependencies justify," and authorized external inspection inward L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0 — and "Description does not become prescription merely because the structure can be traversed."

# USO/l2/formal-mathematics/

**Object:** Directory for the exact formal system: symbols, types, models, derivations, and obligations.
**Path:** `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/` · **Layer:** L2 — FORMAL MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l2/

## What this directory is

Directory for the exact formal system: symbols, types, models, derivations, and obligations. It is the single owner of the bounded thing's exact mathematics inside `USO/l2/`, and its three children — `relations/`, `operators/`, and `proofs/` — separate the relational structure, the lawful actions, and the warrant for exact claims from one another.

In practice, a person filing here states formal symbols and types, exact models and carriers, and the derivations and obligations that go with them, over the L1 state-space contract at `USO/l1/state-space/`. The permitted actions are the layer's: formalize, derive, prove, type-check, construct, test closure; declare domain/codomain/partiality; mark conjectural or unproved claims explicitly. The acceptance check is the layer's: every symbol binds to L0 meaning and L1 admissibility; every operation has declared domain/codomain or partiality; exact results are supported by proof/derivation or marked as unproved. An object that fails the first row is raised at `USO/l0/` or `USO/l1/`, not repaired here.

## Why it exists

Keeps formal mathematics grouped under one exact owner before engineering choices are introduced. Engineering choices are introduced at `USO/l3/`; the exact statement they act on is filed here first.

## Holds

- formal symbols and types
- exact models, carriers, and transition systems, stated over the L1 state-space contract
- axioms and definitions in their formal form, bound to L0 meaning
- exact formulas
- the grouping of relations (`relations/`), operators (`operators/`), and proofs, lemmas, derivations, and explicit unproved obligations (`proofs/`)
- the exact L2 formal contract handed to `USO/l3/`: behavior, invariants, formulas, and unresolved proof obligations

## Does not hold

- semantic and foundational meaning of the symbols — `USO/l0/semantics/` and `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`
- admissible and excluded states, dimensions, invariants, identity conditions — `USO/l1/state-space/`
- approximation and discretization — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/approximation/`
- numerical uncertainty and error characterization — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/uncertainty/`
- engineering tolerance and admissible engineering bounds — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/tolerances/`
- computation as actually run, and any measurement or realized output — `USO/l4/work/computation/` and `USO/l4/work/`
- research interpretation of what a formal result means — `USO/l5/research/`
- superseded prior formal states and their history — `USO/l6/retired/` and `USO/l6/history/`
- externally owned formal results and their source binding — `L7/provenance/` and `L7/peering/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

Engineering tolerance, numerical approximation, and numerical uncertainty belong to L3, not to L2 exact formal ownership. The layer's hard boundary applies to every file here: no engineering tolerance, approximation quality, physical realization, measurement, or empirical truth is claimed here.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term semantic-formulas: Split and reserved; an exact formula is L2.
- Author note: "finite tolerance" is not an L2 ownership rule; engineering tolerance and numerical uncertainty belong at L3.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l2/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/

**Object:** Directory for exact operators, functions, gates, constructors, and other lawful formal actions.
**Path:** `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/` · **Layer:** L2 — FORMAL MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l2/

## What this directory is

Directory for exact operators, functions, gates, constructors, and other lawful formal actions. This is where lawful operation over L1 is made explicit: the transition systems, carriers, and actions that `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/` names are given their operators here, each acting on objects that `USO/l1/state-space/` admits. Formal operation begins at L2 (the L1 boundary rule, stated at `USO/l1/`).

In practice, a person filing here declares, for every operator, its domain and codomain or its partiality — the layer's second acceptance row — and binds its symbols to L0 meaning and L1 admissibility. Closure may be tested here: whether the operator's output remains inside the admitted state space. An output that leaves the declared state space is not repaired by the operator; the question returns to `USO/l1/`. Operators are stated exactly; their numerical realization, error, and tolerance are not owned here.

## Why it exists

Keeps operation explicit rather than burying it inside prose or engineering implementation. An operator that exists only as a sentence in a derivation, or only as a line of code, cannot be inspected on its own domain, codomain, and partiality.

## Holds

- exact operators, functions, gates, constructors, and other lawful formal actions
- transition systems and carriers, stated as the operators that act on admitted states
- declared domain/codomain/partiality for each operator
- closure tests, stated exactly against the L1 state-space contract

## Does not hold

- the exact relations the operators preserve or induce — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`
- proofs that an operator has its stated properties, and the obligations left open — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/`
- the states operated on, and their admissibility — `USO/l1/state-space/`
- algorithms, abstract data-structure choices, discretization, and numerical representation of an operator — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`
- an operator as executed, deployed, or run — `USO/l4/work/computation/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

Engineering tolerance, numerical approximation, and numerical uncertainty belong to L3, not to L2 exact formal ownership.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l2/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/

**Object:** Directory for proofs, lemmas, derivations, and explicit unproved obligations.
**Path:** `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/` · **Layer:** L2 — FORMAL MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l2/

## What this directory is

Directory for proofs, lemmas, derivations, and explicit unproved obligations. It holds the warrant for exact claims made in `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`, `relations/`, and `operators/`, and — with equal standing — the explicit record of what has not been warranted. The layer's third acceptance row is the rule of this directory: exact results are supported by proof/derivation or marked as unproved.

In practice, a person filing here writes the proof, lemma, or derivation over the L1 state-space contract, with each symbol bound to L0 meaning and L1 admissibility, or files the obligation as unproved, explicitly. Marking conjectural or unproved claims explicitly is a permitted action, not a failure; an unresolved proof obligation is part of the exact L2 formal contract that `USO/l3/` receives. A claim that cannot be proved over the current L1 contract is not proved by widening that contract here; the widening is proposed at `USO/l1/`.

## Why it exists

Keeps warrant for exact claims inspectable and separate from the claim’s notation or later numerical realization. A reader can then ask of any exact claim whether its warrant exists, where it is, and what it still owes, without confusing the proof with the symbol or with a computed instance.

## Holds

- proofs, lemmas, and derivations of exact claims
- explicit unproved obligations, marked as unproved
- conjectural claims, marked as conjectural
- the unresolved proof obligations that the exact L2 formal contract hands to `USO/l3/`

## Does not hold

- the relations being proved — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`
- the operators whose properties are proved — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/`
- the admissibility of the states a proof ranges over — `USO/l1/state-space/`
- a numerical check, simulation, or error bound offered in place of a proof — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`
- a proof-checking or test run as executed and its recorded outputs or failures — `USO/l4/work/computation/`
- research judgment about the weight or meaning of a result — `USO/l5/research/review/` and `USO/l5/research/findings/`
- a superseded proof retained for inspection — `USO/l6/retired/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

Engineering tolerance, numerical approximation, and numerical uncertainty belong to L3, not to L2 exact formal ownership. No engineering tolerance, approximation quality, physical realization, measurement, or empirical truth is claimed here.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l2/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/

**Object:** Directory for exact relations among admitted formal objects.
**Path:** `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/` · **Layer:** L2 — FORMAL MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l2/

## What this directory is

Directory for exact relations among admitted formal objects. A relation filed here holds between objects that `USO/l1/state-space/` admits and that `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/` has given formal symbols and types; it states what holds exactly, not what acts (that is `operators/`) and not why the claim is warranted (that is `proofs/`).

In practice, a person filing here writes the relation with its domain declared, states whether it is total or partial over the admitted objects, and binds each symbol to its L0 meaning and its L1 admissibility. A relation asserted without proof or derivation is filed as unproved, explicitly, and its obligation is recorded at `proofs/`. Nothing here carries a tolerance, a unit of measurement, or a numerical approximation.

## Why it exists

Keeps relational structure distinguishable from the operators that act and the proofs that warrant claims. Separating the three lets an inspector locate the first failing element — the relation, the operation, or the warrant — without reading the others.

## Holds

- exact relations among admitted formal objects, including exact formulas stated as relations
- declared domain and partiality for each relation
- the binding of each relation's symbols to L0 meaning and L1 admissibility

## Does not hold

- operators, functions, gates, constructors, and other lawful formal actions — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/`
- proofs, lemmas, derivations, and explicit unproved obligations — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/`
- the admissible and excluded states the relations range over — `USO/l1/state-space/`
- the meaning of the related terms — `USO/l0/semantics/`
- approximate, tolerant, or applied forms of a relation — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`
- a relation as computed or measured — `USO/l4/work/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

Engineering tolerance, numerical approximation, and numerical uncertainty belong to L3, not to L2 exact formal ownership.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term semantic-formulas: Split and reserved — "an exact formula is L2; an applied, tolerant, adaptive, or engineering formula is L3"
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l2/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l3/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L3/0.md` — 1,096 words*

# USO/l3/

**Object:** Approximation, numerical representation, tolerance, uncertainty, and feasibility.
**Path:** `USO/l3/` · **Layer:** L3 — ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l3/, the owner of engineering approximation and feasibility applied to the L2 formal contract.

Layer 3 is the "Layer 3 container for numerical and engineering feasibility applied to the L2 formal contract." It receives the exact contract from `USO/l2/` together with declared constraints, and returns to `USO/l4/` a specification of what can be built or run and within what conditions. It does not own the realized output of `USO/l4/`, and it does not create empirical evidence. Its core question is: "How can the exact L2 relation be made feasible under declared constraints?"

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | How can the exact L2 relation be made feasible under declared constraints? |
| Function | Translate formal behavior into implementable numerical/engineering methods with explicit units, precision, approximation, tolerance, uncertainty, complexity, and operating regions. |
| Directional inputs | Construction: the L2 formal contract plus declared environmental, resource, and performance constraints. Inspection: L4 evidence may force re-evaluation without silently changing L2. |
| Output | An L3 engineering specification telling L4 what can be built/run and within what conditions. |
| Hard boundary | L3 is not the realized L4 output and does not itself create empirical evidence. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify units, precision, approximation profile, tolerance/uncertainty bounds, implementation target, and validation conditions. It carries machine constraints while /0 carries the human semantic description. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents
- algorithms and abstract data-structure choices
- units, numerical representation, precision, approximation, discretization, tolerance
- error/uncertainty budgets, sensitivity, stability, convergence, complexity
- simulation, optimization, feasibility arguments

### Permitted actions
- approximate, discretize, optimize, estimate, bound error, choose representation
- compare candidate methods to L2 invariants
- state operating assumptions and valid region

### Acceptance / boundary check
- Error/uncertainty, units, precision, and tolerance are explicit.
- Required L2 invariants are preserved within declared bounds.
- Resource/complexity claims are analyzed or labeled estimates.

## Directory structure

```text
USO/l3/0
└── engineering-mathematics/0
    ├── approximation/0
    ├── uncertainty/0
    └── tolerances/0
```

**Boundary / traversal rule.** L3 owns engineering approximation and feasibility; it does not own the realized L4 output.

The neighbouring boundaries are stated by the neighbouring sheets and are repeated here only where they name this layer. From `USO/l2/`: "Engineering tolerance, numerical approximation, and numerical uncertainty belong to L3, not to L2 exact formal ownership." and the L2 author note: "Current correction: “finite tolerance” is not an L2 ownership rule. Engineering tolerance and numerical uncertainty belong at L3." From `USO/l4/`, the construction input is "pinned L3-L0 contracts plus resources required for conversion." What `USO/l4/` does with that input is stated at `USO/l4/`, not here.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| USO/l3/ | Layer 3 container for numerical and engineering feasibility applied to the L2 formal contract. | Separates implementable approximation and constraint handling from exact L2 mathematics and realized L4 work. |
| engineering-mathematics/ | Directory for algorithms, numerical representation, units, precision, feasibility, stability, and complexity. | Keeps the engineering translation of exact formal behavior explicit and inspectable. |
| approximation/ | Directory for approximations and discretizations used to make exact relations workable. | Makes loss or approximation visible instead of letting it appear as exact mathematics. |
| uncertainty/ | Directory for uncertainty and error characterization associated with an engineering representation. | Keeps uncertainty explicit rather than silently absorbing it into a result. |
| tolerances/ | Directory for declared tolerances and admissible engineering bounds. | Keeps acceptance bounds separate from the exact L2 relation they approximate or implement. |

Every branch under `USO/l3/` may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.

## In the case of

### Working life boundary hypothesis

L2 may formalize exact relations about life, but L2 does not mathematically support life.

Mathematics that depends on continuation, tolerance, uncertainty, adaptation, feedback, or environmental exchange begins at L3. L4 realizes or records the resulting conversion.

**Clarification.** This boundary remains a research hypothesis until tested.

**Author note.** INFINITE is retained as a working term for open-ended tolerance/refinement across implementations and time, not as permission to move exact L2 objects here.

This hypothesis is carried here exactly as the specification marks it. It is not resolved by this sheet, and nothing filed under `USO/l3/` tests it by being filed.

### Analytic views that include L3

The specification's analytic-view table names two views in which this layer is examined with its neighbours. They "indicate traversal, dependency, or analytic view; they do not create child-directory containment."

- "L3 / L2 / L1 technical-possibility bundle — Engineering mathematics, exact formalization, and admissible state space can be examined together when the question requires all three dependencies."
- "L3 / L2 nested sub-bundle — Within the broader L3-L2-L1 view, L3-L2 is a tighter engineering/formal sub-bundle when L1 is already pinned and not itself under question."

**Author note.** These bundles are semantic and dependency views, not filesystem nesting and not mandatory traversal paths. The same scientific object may be railed through different subsets of the architecture according to the question being asked, while each representation remains owned by the layer that can state it correctly.

### An architecture as an object of inquiry

The specification's OSI-origin table states what this layer may ask or represent: "L3 - Engineering Mathematics — Engineering constraints, tradeoffs, approximation, tolerances, uncertainty, feasibility, or other engineering conditions that shaped or test the architecture." "The contained architecture is not remapped merely because the inquiry expands."

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters from `USO/l2/` and leaves toward `USO/l4/`; inspection enters from `USO/l4/` and proceeds toward `USO/l2/`. This follows the specification's two valid directions — scientific construction outward L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7, and authorized external inspection inward L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0 — with "Inspection: L4 evidence may force re-evaluation without silently changing L2."

# USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/

**Object:** Directory for algorithms, numerical representation, units, precision, feasibility, stability, and complexity.
**Path:** `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/` · **Layer:** L3 — ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l3/

## What this directory is

"Directory for algorithms, numerical representation, units, precision, feasibility, stability, and complexity." It is the single engineering owner under `USO/l3/`: the place where the exact L2 formal contract is translated into implementable numerical/engineering methods, with the three named aspects of that translation held in its children `approximation/`, `uncertainty/`, and `tolerances/`.

A person filing here is doing one of the layer's permitted actions — "approximate, discretize, optimize, estimate, bound error, choose representation", "compare candidate methods to L2 invariants", "state operating assumptions and valid region" — and the record must pass the layer's acceptance check: "Error/uncertainty, units, precision, and tolerance are explicit. Required L2 invariants are preserved within declared bounds. Resource/complexity claims are analyzed or labeled estimates." The children `approximation/`, `uncertainty/`, and `tolerances/` carry the three Prose cells the specification gives them; the remaining items of the layer's Owns row are held at this level.

## Why it exists

"Keeps the engineering translation of exact formal behavior explicit and inspectable." The exact relation stays at `USO/l2/`; what is done to it to make it feasible is written down here rather than left implicit in realized L4 work.

## Holds

- algorithms and abstract data-structure choices
- units, numerical representation, precision, discretization
- error/uncertainty budgets, sensitivity, stability, convergence, complexity
- simulation, optimization, feasibility arguments
- the stated operating assumptions and valid region of each method
- comparisons of candidate methods to L2 invariants
- its three children: `approximation/`, `uncertainty/`, `tolerances/`

## Does not hold

- exact relations — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`
- exact operators, functions, gates, constructors — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/`
- proofs, derivations, and unproved obligations — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/`
- the semantic reading of a formula — `USO/l0/` ("Semantic reading remains L0")
- the realized L4 output — `USO/l4/` (L3 "is not the realized L4 output and does not itself create empirical evidence")
- externally owned standards or source texts relied on by a method — bound through `L7/`, not copied here

## Boundary / traversal rule

L3 owns engineering approximation and feasibility; it does not own the realized L4 output. Construction into this directory comes from "the L2 formal contract plus declared environmental, resource, and performance constraints"; on inspection, "L4 evidence may force re-evaluation without silently changing L2."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term semantic-formulas: Split and reserved — "an applied, tolerant, adaptive, or engineering formula is L3".
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l3/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/approximation/

**Object:** Directory for approximations and discretizations used to make exact relations workable.
**Path:** `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/approximation/` · **Layer:** L3 — ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l3/

## What this directory is

"Directory for approximations and discretizations used to make exact relations workable." An approximation filed here stands in for an exact relation owned at `USO/l2/`; a record here makes its approximation and precision explicit, states its "operating assumptions and valid region", and shows that "Required L2 invariants are preserved within declared bounds." The layer's permitted actions that land here are "approximate, discretize, … choose representation" and "compare candidate methods to L2 invariants."

In practice the record must satisfy the layer's acceptance check: the approximation's error is explicit (its characterization may live in `../uncertainty/`), "Required L2 invariants are preserved within declared bounds", and any claim about cost is "analyzed or labeled estimates." The approximation does not replace the L2 relation and does not become exact by being used.

## Why it exists

"Makes loss or approximation visible instead of letting it appear as exact mathematics." When an exact relation is discretized or replaced by a workable method, the loss is written here where it can be inspected, rather than absorbed into a result at `USO/l4/`.

## Holds

- approximations and discretizations of exact L2 relations
- discretizations and the chosen numerical representation and precision
- the operating assumptions and valid region of each approximation
- comparisons of candidate approximation methods to L2 invariants

## Does not hold

- the exact relation itself — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`
- the exact operator or constructor being approximated — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/`
- proof that the exact relation holds — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/`
- the uncertainty or error characterization of the approximation — `../uncertainty/`
- the declared tolerance the approximation must meet — `../tolerances/`
- the computation, experiment, or writing that realizes the approximation — `USO/l4/work/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L3 owns engineering approximation and feasibility; it does not own the realized L4 output. "Inspection: L4 evidence may force re-evaluation without silently changing L2" — a failed approximation is re-evaluated here; the exact relation at `USO/l2/` is revised only explicitly.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- semantic-formulas: Split and reserved — "an applied, tolerant, adaptive, or engineering formula is L3"; the Record ratifies the layer, not a directory beneath `USO/l3/`, so which child holds such a formula is not adjudicated by this sheet.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l3/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/tolerances/0.md
*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 480 words*

# USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/tolerances/

**Object:** Directory for declared tolerances and admissible engineering bounds.
**Path:** `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/tolerances/` · **Layer:** L3 — ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l3/

## What this directory is

"Directory for declared tolerances and admissible engineering bounds." A record here declares the bound within which an engineering representation is accepted as preserving the exact L2 relation it approximates or implements — the "declared bounds" of the layer's acceptance check, "Required L2 invariants are preserved within declared bounds." The owned item is "tolerance"; the permitted actions that land here are "bound error" and "state operating assumptions and valid region."

In practice the record names the L2 relation or invariant the tolerance is declared against, the engineering representation it applies to, the bound itself with units and precision, and the operating region in which the bound is admissible. A tolerance is a declared acceptance bound; it is not the measured or estimated uncertainty of a result, which is filed in `../uncertainty/`, and it is not evidence that the bound was met, which comes from `USO/l4/`.

## Why it exists

"Keeps acceptance bounds separate from the exact L2 relation they approximate or implement." Tolerance is not an L2 ownership matter; the L2 sheet states that "Engineering tolerance, numerical approximation, and numerical uncertainty belong to L3, not to L2 exact formal ownership." This directory is where that tolerance is owned and declared.

## Holds

- declared tolerances against named L2 relations or invariants
- admissible engineering bounds and the operating region in which they apply
- the units and precision in which each bound is stated
- the assumptions under which a bound is declared admissible

## Does not hold

- the exact relation or invariant the tolerance is declared against — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`
- the approximation or representation the tolerance applies to — `../approximation/`
- the error or uncertainty characterization of that representation — `../uncertainty/`
- the test, run, or measurement showing whether a bound was met — `USO/l4/work/`
- an externally owned tolerance standard relied on for a bound — bound through `L7/`, not copied here

## Boundary / traversal rule

L3 owns engineering approximation and feasibility; it does not own the realized L4 output. A declared tolerance tells `USO/l4/` "what can be built/run and within what conditions"; it does not report what was built or run.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- INFINITE: Descriptive — per the L3 author note at `USO/l3/0.md`, "INFINITE is retained as a working term for open-ended tolerance/refinement across implementations and time, not as permission to move exact L2 objects here." It names no directory and no owner beyond the principal sheet.
- semantic-formulas: Split and reserved — "an applied, tolerant, adaptive, or engineering formula is L3".
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l3/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/uncertainty/

**Object:** Directory for uncertainty and error characterization associated with an engineering representation.
**Path:** `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/uncertainty/` · **Layer:** L3 — ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS · **Owner:** USO/l3/

## What this directory is

"Directory for uncertainty and error characterization associated with an engineering representation." A record here characterizes the error or uncertainty of a named engineering representation — an approximation, discretization, numerical representation, or method held under `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/` — and states how that characterization was obtained. The layer's permitted actions that land here are "estimate, bound error" and "state operating assumptions and valid region"; the owned items are "error/uncertainty budgets, sensitivity, stability, convergence, complexity" (complexity claims are held at `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`).

In practice the record names the representation it belongs to, gives the error or uncertainty budget with its units and precision, and states the region over which the budget is claimed to hold. It does not create empirical evidence: a measured uncertainty comes from work at `USO/l4/` and returns here as "L4 evidence [that] may force re-evaluation without silently changing L2." An uncertainty is not a tolerance; the declared bound a result must meet is filed in `../tolerances/`.

## Why it exists

"Keeps uncertainty explicit rather than silently absorbing it into a result." The acceptance check for the whole layer requires that "Error/uncertainty, units, precision, and tolerance are explicit"; this directory is where the uncertainty part of that check is met.

## Holds

- error and uncertainty budgets for a named engineering representation
- sensitivity, stability, and convergence characterizations
- the method by which an error was bounded or an uncertainty estimated, with its assumptions
- the valid region over which the characterization is claimed

## Does not hold

- the approximation or discretization being characterized — `../approximation/`
- declared tolerances and admissible engineering bounds — `../tolerances/`
- the exact relation, whose exactness carries no engineering uncertainty — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`
- the measurement, run, or observation that produced an empirical figure — `USO/l4/work/`
- an externally owned uncertainty standard or guide relied on by a method — bound through `L7/`, not copied here

## Boundary / traversal rule

L3 owns engineering approximation and feasibility; it does not own the realized L4 output. "L3 is not the realized L4 output and does not itself create empirical evidence."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Under the "Working life boundary hypothesis" at `USO/l3/0.md`, "Mathematics that depends on continuation, tolerance, uncertainty, adaptation, feedback, or environmental exchange begins at L3." Clarification: "This boundary remains a research hypothesis until tested." Nothing filed here tests it by being filed.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l3/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l4/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L4/0.md` — 1,798 words*

# USO/l4/

**Object:** Conversion / realized work / identifiable output.
**Path:** `USO/l4/` · **Layer:** L4 — LAYER 4 · **Owner:** USO/l4/ owns conversion: realized work and its identifiable output or recorded failure.

L4 is the layer at which resources are committed and a work product exists. The specification's core question for this layer is: "What conversion between intellectual property and energy, in either direction, was actually made, written, run, measured, instantiated, operated, manufactured, or recorded?" Everything below L4 (USO/l3/, USO/l2/, USO/l1/, USO/l0/) specifies; L4 makes, runs, measures, or captures. Everything above L4 (USO/l5/, USO/l6/, L7/) interprets, remembers, or presents what L4 produced. The Harmonization Record states the layer's name in one sentence: "L4 is Conversion."

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | What conversion between intellectual property and energy, in either direction, was actually made, written, run, measured, instantiated, operated, manufactured, or recorded? |
| Function | Own Conversion: any intellectual property to energy, or energy to intellectual property. Commit resources to realize an intellectual object, or capture an energetic event, work, or failure as an identifiable intellectual object. |
| Directional inputs | Construction: pinned L3-L0 contracts plus resources required for conversion. Inspection: realized output or failure returns evidence upward; L7 authority or release status is not a scientific construction dependency. |
| Output | An audit-ready conversion product or recorded failure with traceable inputs, transformations, conditions, tests, results, and rework. |
| Hard boundary | L4 does not authorize its own L5 interpretation and cannot flatten lower contracts into a convenience summary. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify build/run instructions, environment, resource requirements, inputs/outputs, integrity checks, permissions, and fault routing for realized work. It does not supply the L5 interpretation of the result. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents
- IP-to-energy realization: writing, models, toys, computation, code execution, experiments, integration, transport, deployment, operation, and manufacturing.
- Energy-to-IP capture: observations, measurements, records, datasets, failures, and other identifiable intellectual objects.
- A contained architecture, including an OSI stack, as actually specified, made, taught, tested, or operated.

### Permitted actions
- make, write, code, run, measure, test, collect, integrate, convert, store, transport, deploy, manufacture
- verify conformance and rework against the governing input contract
- record result or failure without converting it into a conclusion

### Acceptance / boundary check
- Governing lower dependencies are pinned and resolvable.
- A distinguishable IP/energy conversion output or recorded failure exists.
- Inputs, conditions, transformations, results, and evidence remain traceable.
- L4 existence does not authorize its own L5 interpretation.

## Directory structure

```text
USO/l4/0
└── work/0
    ├── writing/0
    ├── experiments/0
    └── computation/0
```

**Boundary / traversal rule.** Writing belongs to L4 as performed work. Its research meaning belongs to L5; a released publication belongs to L7. Contained architectures may expand internally without changing their 7LM owner. When the question is how the OSI architecture operates, the complete OSI L1-L7 stack remains inside 7LM L4, and OSI L7 is not 7LM L7. When the question instead concerns OSI origins, development, justification, standardization, or design constraints, the inquiry may expand across the appropriate 7LM layers without remapping the OSI stack itself. An OSI network fault is therefore an L4 operational fault unless separately shown to affect another layer's owned object.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| USO/l4/ | Layer 4 container for performed and realized work that returns an identifiable output or recorded failure. | Keeps the event of making or doing distinct from the later L5 interpretation of what the result means. |
| work/ | Directory for traceable realized work governed by pinned lower-layer dependencies. | Provides one place for the performed event without flattening the L3-L0 package that governs it. A complete internal architecture, including OSI, remains an L4 object even when it has its own internal layers. |
| writing/ | Directory for writing and document production as performed work. | Distinguishes making the document from its L5 research meaning and its L7 released publication. |
| experiments/ | Directory for experiments as actually executed, including observations, measurements, tests, and failures. | Distinguishes performed experiment from L3 measurement design and L5 interpretation. |
| computation/ | Directory for computation as actually run, including executable processing and recorded outputs or failures. | Separates executed computation from the formal L2 relation and engineering L3 method that specify it. OSI application and network layers remain execution detail inside L4; OSI L7 is not the 7LM Surface / Peering layer. |

The canonical-tree rule applies to every branch here: a branch may remain empty when unused; presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.

## L4 conversion rule

Any intellectual property to energy, or energy to intellectual property.

IP to energy: an intellectual object is made operative through writing, computation, experiment, integration, transport, deployment, teaching, operation, or manufacturing.

Energy to IP: an energetic event, operation, measurement, observation, success, or failure is captured as an identifiable intellectual object.

A toy, model, OSI stack, writing event, computation, manufactured thing, or full system can be L4. Role remains decisive: an exact model may be L2, its engineering realization may be L3, the model as made or operated is L4, and its research meaning is L5.

**Research note.** MOST IMPORTANT: THE CUT LIVES HERE

**Standing.** The cut is the realization boundary: L3 and below specify what is admissible, exact, or engineerable; L4 is where resources are committed and an identifiable work product, measurement, execution, or failure is produced.

## In the case of

### Cross-layer activity cases

| Case | Placement rule |
|---|---|
| Research | L5 owns interpretation/review; L6 preserves internal memory/history; L7 owns external provenance and exposes permitted presentations; L4 performs research work events; lower layers own their specific representations. |
| Writing | The act/file production is L4. The research argument is L5. Internal version and correction history are L6. Public provenance, governance, and the released publication are L7. |
| Experiment | Executed experiment, observation, measurement, and raw work product are L4; engineering method/tolerance L3; exact relations L2; possibilities L1; interpretation/conclusion L5. |
| Contained architecture (OSI) | Operationally, the full OSI L1-L7 architecture remains an L4 object. Questions about its origins, development, justification, standards history, or design constraints may be railed through other 7LM layers; this analytic expansion does not remap OSI layers to 7LM layers. |
| Code | Algorithmic/engineering representation can be L3; formal proof/model L2; execution, deployed bytes, storage, interfaces, and operational result L4. |
| Data | Collected/stored instances and transformations are L4; engineering representation/precision L3; formal encoding may be L2; state categories L1; external source/lineage is L7 provenance; internal development history is L6; interpretation is L5. |
| External definition | Owner remains external at L7 peering; source/version/authority/prior-art relation is L7 provenance; applicable legal/access conditions are L7 governance; internal use history is L6; interpretation is L5; no local L0 object exists unless this bounded thing authors a semantic version. |
| Publication | Production is L4; scholarly/research meaning is L5; internal history is L6; provenance, public/legal governance, and the released publication object are L7. |

**Author note.** Writing is L4 as work. An exemplar/model/thesis/file is made here; its research meaning may be L5 and its released publication may be L7.

### Contained architectures: OSI

The specification's global statement of containment, which the boundary rule above applies:

"When the question is how the OSI architecture operates, the complete OSI model remains inside 7LM L4. OSI L1 through OSI L7 are internal layers of a contained realized architecture; OSI L7 is not 7LM L7, and the OSI layer numbers do not map onto the 7LM layer numbers."

"When the question changes to the origins, development, justification, standardization, institutional history, or design constraints of OSI, the inquiry may rail the OSI object across the 7LM architecture. The operating OSI stack remains an L4 object; the inquiry generates layer-owned representations of that object according to what is being asked."

**Author note.** The OSI model does not become 7LM L0-L7. The question about the OSI object expands. Operational containment remains L4, while representations produced by the inquiry are owned by the 7LM layer that can state them correctly.

The analytic view of this layer in the specification's outline reads: "L4 realization bay — A whole realized architecture may be contained here and may expand internally according to its own native structure without commandeering 7LM layer numbers." The architecture-wide rule behind it: "Operational containment and analytic expansion are different relations. A complete architecture may remain owned at one 7LM layer while questions about its origin, justification, development, or effects generate representations at other 7LM layers. The contained architecture is not remapped merely because the inquiry expands."

### Auditonomous academic-book traversal

- L0 owns the locally authored Auditonomous definition.
- L1 owns the Auditonomous state space.
- L2 may own exact formal relations or proofs about the project when actually established, but it does not support life or own the project's formulas for life.
- L3 owns the project's formulas for life when they are applied, open-ended, constrained, approximate, or engineering formulas.
- L4 owns the Auditonomous model as made, written, instantiated, or operated, including production of the book artifact.
- L5 owns the book's research argument, interpretation, and teaching.
- L6 owns the reconstructable development, correction, failure, and supersession history.
- L7 owns release and publication of the academic book and the peer binding to the separately owned Autonomous definition.

Separate layer-owned representations overlay the same bounded subject.

The L4 Auditonomous model does not contain the L1 state space or L3 formulas as its own layer contents; it is governed by and related to those separately owned representations.

Overlay does not collapse ownership or duplicate the whole book at every layer. A peer definition may reach the receiving L0 through the two L7 faces while remaining an immutable, externally authored binding.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters from `USO/l3/` and leaves toward `USO/l5/`; inspection enters from `USO/l5/` and proceeds toward `USO/l3/`. These are the two valid directions of the specification — scientific construction outward L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7, authorized external inspection inward L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0.

# USO/l4/work/

**Object:** Directory for traceable realized work governed by pinned lower-layer dependencies.
**Path:** `USO/l4/work/` · **Layer:** L4 — LAYER 4 · **Owner:** USO/l4/

## What this directory is

"Directory for traceable realized work governed by pinned lower-layer dependencies." This is the one place in the layer where a performed event is filed: an intellectual object made operative, or an energetic event captured as an identifiable intellectual object. Its three children are `USO/l4/work/writing/`, `USO/l4/work/experiments/`, and `USO/l4/work/computation/`, each carrying the spec's own prose for that kind of work; and a contained architecture with its own internal layers is filed here as one L4 object.

In practice, a person filing here files a work product or a recorded failure, together with what makes it traceable: inputs, conditions, transformations, results, tests, and rework. The governing lower dependencies are "pinned and resolvable"; the filing "cannot flatten lower contracts into a convenience summary". The filing records the result or failure "without converting it into a conclusion"; the conclusion is filed at `USO/l5/`.

## Why it exists

"Provides one place for the performed event without flattening the L3-L0 package that governs it. A complete internal architecture, including OSI, remains an L4 object even when it has its own internal layers."

The layer's hard boundary is the reason the package is not flattened: "L4 does not authorize its own L5 interpretation and cannot flatten lower contracts into a convenience summary."

## Holds

- IP-to-energy realization: writing, models, toys, computation, code execution, experiments, integration, transport, deployment, operation, and manufacturing.
- Energy-to-IP capture: observations, measurements, records, datasets, failures, and other identifiable intellectual objects.
- A contained architecture, including an OSI stack, as actually specified, made, taught, tested, or operated.
- An audit-ready conversion product or recorded failure with traceable inputs, transformations, conditions, tests, results, and rework.
- Conformance verification and rework against the governing input contract.

## Does not hold

- The research meaning, interpretation, review, or conclusion of the work — `USO/l5/research/`.
- Internal version, correction, failure, and supersession history — `USO/l6/history/`; retired states — `USO/l6/retired/`.
- The released publication object — `L7/publications/`; external provenance — `L7/provenance/`; public or legal governance — `L7/governance/`; peer-owned objects and bindings — `L7/peering/`.
- Engineering method, tolerance, uncertainty, and approximation that specify the work — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`.
- Exact relations, operators, and proofs — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`.
- Admissible states and possibilities — `USO/l1/state-space/`.
- Locally authored semantic distinctions and definitions — `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`.
- Generic repository support: "Infrastructure belongs at L4 only when it is the bounded work or conversion being made; generic repository support is not automatically a layer."

## Boundary / traversal rule

Writing belongs to L4 as performed work. Its research meaning belongs to L5; a released publication belongs to L7. Contained architectures may expand internally without changing their 7LM owner. When the question is how the OSI architecture operates, the complete OSI L1-L7 stack remains inside 7LM L4, and OSI L7 is not 7LM L7. When the question instead concerns OSI origins, development, justification, standardization, or design constraints, the inquiry may expand across the appropriate 7LM layers without remapping the OSI stack itself. An OSI network fault is therefore an L4 operational fault unless separately shown to affect another layer's owned object.

The cut is the realization boundary: L3 and below specify what is admissible, exact, or engineerable; L4 is where resources are committed and an identifiable work product, measurement, execution, or failure is produced.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term: INFRASTRUCTURE — "Superseded as L4 name. L4 is Conversion."
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l4/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l4/work/computation/

**Object:** Directory for computation as actually run, including executable processing and recorded outputs or failures.
**Path:** `USO/l4/work/computation/` · **Layer:** L4 — LAYER 4 · **Owner:** USO/l4/

## What this directory is

"Directory for computation as actually run, including executable processing and recorded outputs or failures." Computation filed here is the run: the executed processing, its deployed bytes, and its recorded output or failure. The specification's Code case fixes the owners: "Algorithmic/engineering representation can be L3; formal proof/model L2; execution, deployed bytes, storage, interfaces, and operational result L4."

In practice, a person filing here files what was run and what came out — inputs, environment and conditions, transformations, results, tests, and rework — under pinned and resolvable lower dependencies, and records the result or failure "without converting it into a conclusion". The conversion rule names computation directly: "an intellectual object is made operative through writing, computation, experiment, integration, transport, deployment, teaching, operation, or manufacturing." The recorded output or failure is the identifiable intellectual object the layer's Output row requires.

## Why it exists

"Separates executed computation from the formal L2 relation and engineering L3 method that specify it. OSI application and network layers remain execution detail inside L4; OSI L7 is not the 7LM Surface / Peering layer."

The relation that a computation implements stays at `USO/l2/`; the method by which it approximates that relation stays at `USO/l3/`; what actually ran is here.

## Holds

- Computation as actually run: executable processing and recorded outputs or failures.
- Execution, deployed bytes, storage, interfaces, and operational result.
- Recorded outputs of a computation, including stored results. The Data case places "Collected/stored instances and transformations" at L4 without naming a child directory; this sheet does not assign them.
- A contained architecture, including an OSI stack, as actually specified, made, taught, tested, or operated — its application and network layers included, as execution detail.
- Conformance verification and rework against the governing input contract.

## Does not hold

- Algorithmic or engineering representation, precision, and method — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`.
- Formal proof, model, operators, and formal encoding — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/proofs/`, `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/operators/`, `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`.
- State categories — `USO/l1/state-space/`.
- Interpretation of a result — `USO/l5/research/`. "L4 does not authorize its own L5 interpretation."
- External source or lineage of data and code — `L7/provenance/`; internal development history — `USO/l6/history/`.
- Machine instructions for this repository. Machine-readable metadata for realized work is described at `USO/l4/` and is not instantiated; this directory holds computation as run when there is any, not automation of the scaffold.
- Writing and experiments as run — `USO/l4/work/writing/` and `USO/l4/work/experiments/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

Writing belongs to L4 as performed work. Its research meaning belongs to L5; a released publication belongs to L7. Contained architectures may expand internally without changing their 7LM owner. When the question is how the OSI architecture operates, the complete OSI L1-L7 stack remains inside 7LM L4, and OSI L7 is not 7LM L7. When the question instead concerns OSI origins, development, justification, standardization, or design constraints, the inquiry may expand across the appropriate 7LM layers without remapping the OSI stack itself. An OSI network fault is therefore an L4 operational fault unless separately shown to affect another layer's owned object.

Contained architecture (OSI) case: "Operationally, the full OSI L1-L7 architecture remains an L4 object. Questions about its origins, development, justification, standards history, or design constraints may be railed through other 7LM layers; this analytic expansion does not remap OSI layers to 7LM layers."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. It is empty at present.
- Inherited term: INFRASTRUCTURE — "Superseded as L4 name. L4 is Conversion. Infrastructure belongs at L4 only when it is the bounded work or conversion being made; generic repository support is not automatically a layer."
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l4/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l4/work/experiments/

**Object:** Directory for experiments as actually executed, including observations, measurements, tests, and failures.

## What this directory is

"Directory for experiments as actually executed, including observations, measurements, tests, and failures." An experiment filed here is the experiment as run, not as designed. The specification's Experiment case fixes the owners: "Executed experiment, observation, measurement, and raw work product are L4; engineering method/tolerance L3; exact relations L2; possibilities L1; interpretation/conclusion L5."

In practice, a person filing here files the run performed and its raw work product — observations, measurements, tests, and recorded failures — with traceable inputs, conditions, transformations, results, and rework. This is the energy-to-IP direction of the conversion rule: "an energetic event, operation, measurement, observation, success, or failure is captured as an identifiable intellectual object." The governing measurement design stays at `USO/l3/`, and the result is recorded "without converting it into a conclusion".

## Why it exists

"Distinguishes performed experiment from L3 measurement design and L5 interpretation."

The same experiment has three owners: its design is engineering mathematics, its execution is conversion, its meaning is research.

## Holds

- Experiments as actually executed, including observations, measurements, tests, and failures.
- Energy-to-IP capture: observations, measurements, records, datasets, failures, and other identifiable intellectual objects.
- Raw work product of an executed experiment, with traceable inputs, conditions, transformations, results, and evidence.
- Conformance verification and rework against the governing input contract.

## Does not hold

- Measurement design, engineering method, tolerance, uncertainty, and approximation — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`.
- Exact relations — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/relations/`.
- Possibilities and admissible states — `USO/l1/state-space/`.
- Interpretation or conclusion — `USO/l5/research/`.
- External source or lineage of collected data — `L7/provenance/`; internal development history — `USO/l6/history/`. (Data case: "Collected/stored instances and transformations are L4; engineering representation/precision L3; formal encoding may be L2; state categories L1; external source/lineage is L7 provenance; internal development history is L6; interpretation is L5.")
- Writing and computation as run — `USO/l4/work/writing/` and `USO/l4/work/computation/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

Writing belongs to L4 as performed work. Its research meaning belongs to L5; a released publication belongs to L7. Contained architectures may expand internally without changing their 7LM owner. When the question is how the OSI architecture operates, the complete OSI L1-L7 stack remains inside 7LM L4, and OSI L7 is not 7LM L7. When the question instead concerns OSI origins, development, justification, standardization, or design constraints, the inquiry may expand across the appropriate 7LM layers without remapping the OSI stack itself. An OSI network fault is therefore an L4 operational fault unless separately shown to affect another layer's owned object.

Acceptance at this directory is the layer's: "Governing lower dependencies are pinned and resolvable. A distinguishable IP/energy conversion output or recorded failure exists. Inputs, conditions, transformations, results, and evidence remain traceable. L4 existence does not authorize its own L5 interpretation."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. It is empty at present.

- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l4/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.
