# USO/l4/work/writing/

**Object:** Directory for writing and document production as performed work.
**Path:** `USO/l4/work/writing/` · **Layer:** L4 — LAYER 4 · **Owner:** USO/l4/

## What this directory is

"Directory for writing and document production as performed work." A document filed here is filed as a thing made: the act and the produced file. The specification's Writing case fixes the four owners of one written thing: "The act/file production is L4. The research argument is L5. Internal version and correction history are L6. Public provenance, governance, and the released publication are L7."

In practice, a person filing here files the produced document and what makes its production traceable — inputs, conditions, transformations, and rework — under pinned and resolvable lower dependencies. The filing does not state what the document establishes; that is an L5 matter. "Writing is L4 as work. An exemplar/model/thesis/file is made here; its research meaning may be L5 and its released publication may be L7."

## Why it exists

"Distinguishes making the document from its L5 research meaning and its L7 released publication."

Filing a document here asserts that it was produced; it asserts nothing about its research standing or its release.

## Holds

- Writing and document production as performed work: manuscripts, exemplars, models, theses, and files as made, with their production record.
- Recorded failures of writing work, filed as identifiable intellectual objects.
- **The harmonized specification documents.** The Harmonized Authoritative Architecture Specification of the Seven-Layer Model, version of 7 September 2026, is filed in this directory as produced writing, in two files:
  - `USO/l4/work/writing/7lm-harmonized-architecture-specification.md` — the canonical Markdown form and the source of the version;
  - `USO/l4/work/writing/7lm-harmonized-architecture-specification.docx` — generated from that Markdown with built-in Word styles only (Title, Headings 1–3, Normal, Quote, Caption, List Number, Table Grid, and a monospace Code style), every top-level section starting a new page, and reading back to the Markdown unchanged. It is the draft of the next controlling document, awaiting the owner's hand; once the owner edits it, the document controls the wording again and the Markdown is re-normalized from it.
  The specification states the model: what the layers are and where each one stops, the count rule, the two directions, the canonical tree, the rules that hold across layers, and how a definition owned elsewhere is carried in. It does not restate the sheets. Each layer's full contract, and the prose and rationale of each directory, stand at that directory's own `0.md`; where a sheet and the specification differ, the specification controls. It does not carry its own history and does not carry the current condition of any repository; its versions, the dated lists of what changed, the owner's words behind each rule, and the inherited terms with their dispositions are at `USO/l6/history/specification-history.md`, and prior versions are retired under `USO/l6/retired/`.
  This placement follows the specification's own Writing case: the act/file production is L4. The specification's research meaning is an L5 matter, and its release state is an L7 matter; filing here asserts neither. The owner may re-place these files. The specification's Authority box states that it "states the controlling 7LM research architecture"; filing here does not alter that statement.

## Does not hold

- The research argument, interpretation, review, or teaching content of a document — `USO/l5/`.
- Internal version and correction history of a document — `USO/l6/history/`; superseded drafts and prior versions — `USO/l6/retired/`.
- The released publication object — `L7/publications/`; public provenance — `L7/provenance/`; governance — `L7/governance/`.
- External definitions quoted in a document: "Owner remains external at L7 peering; source/version/authority/prior-art relation is L7 provenance; applicable legal/access conditions are L7 governance."
- Experiments and computation as run — `USO/l4/work/experiments/` and `USO/l4/work/computation/`.

## Boundary / traversal rule

Writing belongs to L4 as performed work. Its research meaning belongs to L5; a released publication belongs to L7. Contained architectures may expand internally without changing their 7LM owner. When the question is how the OSI architecture operates, the complete OSI L1-L7 stack remains inside 7LM L4, and OSI L7 is not 7LM L7. When the question instead concerns OSI origins, development, justification, standardization, or design constraints, the inquiry may expand across the appropriate 7LM layers without remapping the OSI stack itself. An OSI network fault is therefore an L4 operational fault unless separately shown to affect another layer's owned object.

The Publication case states the same partition for a document that is released: "Production is L4; scholarly/research meaning is L5; internal history is L6; provenance, public/legal governance, and the released publication object are L7."

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed. At present it holds the two specification files named above.
- Inherited term: INFRASTRUCTURE — "Superseded as L4 name. L4 is Conversion."
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l4/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l4/work/writing/7lm-harmonized-architecture-specification.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L4/work/writing/7lm-harmonized-architecture-specification.md` — 3,301 words*

<!-- Canonical Markdown form of the Seven-Layer Model (7LM) Harmonized Authoritative Architecture Specification,
     version of 7 September 2026. This Markdown is the source; the .docx filed beside it is generated from the text
     below this comment and reads back to it unchanged. This document states the model. Each directory of the tree
     states its own position in its own 0.md; the versions of this document, what changed in each, and the owner's
     words behind each rule are at USO/l6/history/specification-history.md. -->

# SEVEN-LAYER MODEL (7LM)

Harmonized Authoritative Architecture Specification

The model, its layers, and its canonical directory topology

*7LM Harmonized Authoritative Architecture Specification • 7 September 2026*

> Authority\
> This specification states the controlling 7LM research architecture: the seven interior layers, the numbered face, their boundaries, and one canonical agnostic directory topology. It is authoritative for this research program. Capability branches may remain empty when unused; they remain part of the agnostic topology so a project can instantiate them without changing the architecture.

Version\
Version of 7 September 2026. The history of versions and changes, with the owner's words behind each rule, is at L8/L6/history/specification-history.md in the scaffold repository.

### How to use this document

This document states the model and nothing else: what the layers are, where each one stops, the tree, the rules that hold across layers, and how a definition you do not own is carried in. It is short on purpose.

Each directory of the canonical tree states itself in its own 0.md: the full contract of its layer, what it owns, what may be done there, what it does not hold, its worked cases, and its inherited terms. Those sheets are the documentation. This document does not repeat them. Where a sheet and this document differ, this document controls.

To place a thing, ask which layer can state it correctly: each representation belongs at the lowest layer where it can be stated correctly. The recurring cases — research, writing, experiment, contained architecture, code, data, an external definition, publication — are settled at L8/L4/0.md.

To debug, find the first layer where the invariant or contract fails, and return the correction to that owner.

To find what changed, when, and on whose word, read the records at L6/history/. This document states the architecture as it is, not how it got here.

---

## What 7LM is

7LM exists to:

1. separate ontology/semantics from state space, formal mathematics, engineering mathematics, realized work, research judgment, memory/history, and external provenance/governance/peering/publication;
2. pin lower-layer contracts so multiple research corpora can rely on them;
3. prevent semantic drift;
4. localize errors to the layer that owns them;
5. allow repair, promotion, pinning, supersession, and shedding without collapsing the whole research program;
6. keep implementation details from silently redefining lower-layer science.

The operational debugging principle is:

> Find the first layer where the invariant or contract fails, and return the correction to that owner.

---

## Two valid directions

Authorized external inspection inward:

L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0

Scientific construction outward:

L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7

---

## First is always 0

L0 through L6 are the seven interior dependency layers. L7 is the numbered external Surface / Forward Face; it participates in traversal but is not an eighth interior dependency layer.

The coherent principal L0-L7 information sheets control. Repeated contracts and contradictory anchors are superseded; unique notes, cases, rationales, and boundary research are preserved. Questionable inherited concepts are reserved with topology unadjudicated unless this specification expressly places them.

First is always 0.\
The owner's definition, given with its table:

| **semantic** | **>** | **magnitude** |
|---|---|---|
| FIRST | is | 0 |
| SECOND | is | 1 |
| THIRD | is | 2 |
| FOURTH | is | 3 |

**[Clarification]** Ordinals are semantics; numbers are magnitudes; the relation between them is "is". The seven interior layers are the seven semantics FIRST through SEVENTH and the seven magnitudes L0 through L6, so the seventh layer is L6: layer 7 will always be layer 6. L7 is a magnitude, the position numbered 7, the eighth reached in traversal: numbered, not a layer. L8 is a count, not a position: the interior's own zero over the seven layer zeros. Nothing follows the desk in traversal; past the face the count begins again at zero, in another object's tree, where this whole object is one bound object at a peer position.

**[Note]** Author note. L7, pronounced El-Seven, is the desk: the L is the lower left hand corner of the desk and the 7 is the upper right; the name draws the rectangle that this layer is, its Surface. L8 is the research packet on the desk: two zeros stacked, the interior's zero over the zeros of the seven layers. The interior directories carry uppercase too: L0 through L7 are the eight bits of a byte, 0 being a bit, and L8 is the extra bit that carries the semantic, as information needs an extra bit to catch any error. The names are design, not decoration, and were not chosen by accident.

---

## The layers

| **Layer** | **Owns** | **Stops at** |
|---|---|---|
| **L0 — Semantic / Foundational** | Local semantic/rail version of the bounded thing; origin contract, not a warehouse for external definitions. | A source L0 definition may travel through source L7 egress and receiving L7 ingress to receiving L0. At receiving L0 it remains exact, externally authored, immutable, version-pinned, and explicitly tagged; it is not locally redefined or counted as local authorship. L0 does not own prior art, provenance, public/legal governance, state transition, operator, proof, numerical method, experiment, or research conclusion. |
| **L1 — State Space** | Admissible states and static possibility bounds. | No carrying, transition, selection, learning, calculation, propagation, or transformation. Formal operation begins at L2. |
| **L2 — Formal Mathematics** | Exact formal objects and lawful operation over L1. | No engineering tolerance, approximation quality, physical realization, measurement, or empirical truth is claimed here. |
| **L3 — Engineering Mathematics** | Approximation, numerical representation, tolerance, uncertainty, and feasibility. | L3 is not the realized L4 output and does not itself create empirical evidence. |
| **L4 — Conversion** | Conversion / realized work / identifiable output. | L4 does not authorize its own L5 interpretation and cannot flatten lower contracts into a convenience summary. |
| **L5 — Research / Discourse** | Interpretation, review, judgment, argument, and teaching. | Consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact. |
| **L6 — Library** | Memory, history, reconstruction, retirement, and query orientation. | L6 records, preserves, and orients; it does not make the L5 research judgment or establish truth. |
| **L7 — Surface / Forward Face** | External provenance, public/legal governance, peering, publications, exposure, and exchange. | L7 owns the external relation, not the lower-layer scientific meaning. Presence, publication, copyright, patent status, custody, or provenance at L7 does not make an object valid, complete, true, or internally authored. |

Each layer's full contract — its core question, function, directional inputs, output, what it owns, what may be done there, the acceptance check, its machine-metadata rule, and its worked cases — stands at that layer's own sheet: L8/L0/0.md through L8/L6/0.md for the interior, and L7/0.md for the face.

Higher layers do not silently repair lower layers. If a higher-layer object requires different lower-layer meaning, the lower-layer dependency is revised explicitly rather than reinterpreted in place.

Layer ownership is local. A topic may appear at several layers, but each representation belongs at the lowest layer where that representation can be stated correctly.

Change one layer at a time. A correction at one layer does not silently rewrite another layer.

A canonical branch may remain empty when unused. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population.

Directory metadata may enforce a machine rule, but it does not create the human semantic meaning carried by /0. Conversely, /0 does not substitute for machine instructions.

Operational containment and analytic expansion are different relations. A complete architecture may remain owned at one 7LM layer while questions about its origin, justification, development, or effects generate representations at other 7LM layers. The contained architecture is not remapped merely because the inquiry expands.

---

## Topology and traversal

L7 is outside the USO and is physically a sibling boundary to L8/. L6 through L0 are the scientific interior. Physical siblinghood and semantic sequence are different relations: crossing L7 <-> L6 is a boundary/peering event, not filesystem nesting.

```text
external peer / citizen / practitioner
                 |
                 v
      [ L7 SURFACE / FORWARD FACE ]
======== boundary / peering separator ========
                    USO

        [ L6 LIBRARY / MEMORY ]
      [ L5 RESEARCH / DISCOURSE ]

   [ L4 CONVERSION / REALIZATION BAY ]
       contained operational architecture
       may expand internally here
       example: complete OSI L1-L7 stack

[ L3 ENGINEERING MATHEMATICS ]
        [ L2 FORMAL MATHEMATICS ]
              [ L1 STATE SPACE ]
---------- local L0 boundary ----------
     [ L0 SEMANTIC / FOUNDATIONAL ]
```

The canonical research tree keeps the interior physically flat: L6/, L5/, L4/, L3/, L2/, L1/, and L0/ are siblings under L8/. The arrows, groupings, and visual bands in this outline indicate traversal, dependency, or analytic view; they do not create child-directory containment.

| **Analytic view** | **Meaning** |
|---|---|
| L6 / L5 upper interior | Memory/history and research/discourse are the upper scientific interior. They remain separate layer owners even when shown as one research-facing band. |
| L4 realization bay | A whole realized architecture may be contained here and may expand internally according to its own native structure without commandeering 7LM layer numbers. |
| L3 / L2 / L1 technical-possibility bundle | Engineering mathematics, exact formalization, and admissible state space can be examined together when the question requires all three dependencies. |
| L3 / L2 nested sub-bundle | Within the broader L3-L2-L1 view, L3-L2 is a tighter engineering/formal sub-bundle when L1 is already pinned and not itself under question. |
| L0 local semantic boundary | L0 is deliberately separated from L1. L0 establishes the local semantic/foundational contract; L1 establishes admissible states under that contract. |

**[Clarification]** *Author note.* These bundles are semantic and dependency views, not filesystem nesting and not mandatory traversal paths. The same scientific object may be railed through different subsets of the architecture according to the question being asked, while each representation remains owned by the layer that can state it correctly.

---

## Canonical Research Directory Topology

One complete agnostic directory structure is available to all research repositories. A branch may remain empty when unused; the canonical tree is not reduced merely because one project does not need a capability.

Notation: /0 means the directory carries its local 0.md human-readable semantic description. Directory metadata is attached to the directory itself and is therefore not drawn as another branch in the tree.

```text
<repo>/
├── README.md
├── L7/0
│   ├── provenance/0
│   │   ├── prior-art/0
│   │   ├── sources/0
│   │   ├── lineage/0
│   │   └── authority/0
│   ├── governance/0
│   │   ├── copyright/0
│   │   ├── licensing/0
│   │   ├── patents/0
│   │   ├── permissions/0
│   │   ├── access/0
│   │   └── restrictions/0
│   ├── peering/0
│   │   ├── private/0
│   │   │   ├── ingress/0
│   │   │   │   └── peer-1/0
│   │   │   │       └── object-1/0
│   │   │   └── egress/0
│   │   │       └── peer-1/0
│   │   │           └── object-1/0
│   │   └── public/0
│   │       ├── ingress/0
│   │       │   └── peer-1/0
│   │       │       └── object-1/0
│   │       └── egress/0
│   │           └── peer-1/0
│   │               └── object-1/0
│   └── publications/0
└── L8/0
    ├── L6/0
    │   ├── history/0
    │   └── retired/0
    ├── L5/0
    │   ├── research/0
    │   │   ├── lanes/0
    │   │   ├── review/0
    │   │   └── findings/0
    │   └── institution/0
    │       ├── peers/0
    │       └── undergraduates/0
    ├── L4/0
    │   └── work/0
    │       ├── writing/0
    │       ├── experiments/0
    │       └── computation/0
    ├── L3/0
    │   └── engineering-mathematics/0
    │       ├── approximation/0
    │       ├── uncertainty/0
    │       └── tolerances/0
    ├── L2/0
    │   └── formal-mathematics/0
    │       ├── relations/0
    │       ├── operators/0
    │       └── proofs/0
    ├── L1/0
    │   └── state-space/0
    └── L0/0
        ├── universality/0
        ├── pedagogy/0
        ├── epistemic/0
        ├── ontology/0
        └── semantics/0
            └── definitions/0
```

Canonical-tree rule. Unused now does not mean removed. The same complete topology is available to Autonomous, Auditonomous, OMMP, classroom research, institutional research, and future research repositories.

**[Clarification]** README.md is where a reader starts; it is not the documentation. The documentation is the sheets, one 0.md per directory, and this specification. The README states what the repository is, the reading order (the desk L7/0, the interior L8/0, then every sheet), the root, and where the prior README is, and nothing else. The root holds README.md, L7/, and L8/, and may hold repository infrastructure that is not a layer: the one CI check at .github/workflows/integrity.yml, which refuses unresolved conflict markers, a root that is not canonical, and a sheet whose heading is not its path, reports directories without a sheet, and moves nothing; and .gitignore. Every 7LM repository has the same root.

**[Research Note]** Each /0 begins with the object directory and path: /data/location/object

**[Clarification]** Standing. Each local 0.md first identifies the directory/object and its path, then supplies that directory's semantic description. Machine instructions belong to the directory metadata, not to the semantic prose.

**[Clarification]** The topology states positions and meaning. It states no rule about what any intelligence, a person, a session, a model, or an orchestrator, may or may not touch. Where such rules exist they belong to repository infrastructure outside the layers, and general repository infrastructure is not automatically a layer.

L7 = Desktop

L8 = USO, the bounded scientific object's interior: the research packet on the desk

The USO is the compartmentalized scientific interior that allows the components/representations of a bounded thing to occupy their correct layer without forcing the entire repository or corpus to share one maturity status. General repository infrastructure is not automatically a layer.

A bounded exemplar can be written or made at L4 while its semantic, state-space, formal, engineering, research, memory/history, provenance/governance, and surface representations remain separately addressable. The layer tree is the discourse structure of a thing, not a command to refile unrelated repository infrastructure.

---

## A definition you do not own

The Autonomous/Auditonomous root issue supplies the concrete origin of the agnostic peering rule: one bounded research object must be able to depend on another without owning, copying, redefining, or maintaining both objects' foundational semantics. Auditonomous can use the exact Autonomous definition without becoming a second semantic authority for Autonomous. peer-1/object-1 is the agnostic form of this concrete ownership problem.

**[Clarification]** The Autonomous and Auditonomous repositories are implementation work in progress; their current layouts do not control the architecture. Architecture and implementation status are separate claims.

### Contextual semantic route

Source L0 definition → source L7 egress → receiving L7 ingress → receiving L0. A source object's L0 definition is an L7 egress object at its boundary; to us it is L7 ingress directed to our L0. Its semantic identity remains source-owned L0 throughout the exchange.

The receiver does not have to redefine the peer definition. Every internal representation that uses it carries the exact bound version with an immutable external-ownership tag: object identity, owner, source layer, version, and ingress binding. An upstream change creates a new binding; it does not mutate the pinned object.

Declared semantic-deference rule. For the bounded purpose of interpreting the peer, the receiver intentionally takes the owner's exact bound definition as authoritative for what the owner means and does not re-prove or locally redefine that meaning. This declared trust is not a claim that the definition is objectively true, not an endorsement of downstream claims, and not a transfer of authorship.

An argument about Autonomous cannot silently stand in for an argument about Auditonomous. A cross-object claim requires an explicit named relation or handoff. The same identity-tagging rule applies to agnostic methods as they are made and carried through the architecture.

Autonomous has one bounded semantic job: state and freeze the narrowest reasonable agreement shared across exact, independently bound standards definitions. It must not manufacture consensus where sources materially disagree; every disagreement remains source-tagged and visible.

Auditonomous consumes the pinned Autonomous consensus baseline and authors the Auditonomous difference as an explicit local delta. The baseline and delta remain separately owned and overlaid rather than blended into a dual-owned definition.

Canonical direct L7 branches are L7/provenance/, L7/governance/, L7/peering/, and L7/publications/.

### Standing rules

A definition lives once, at its owner. L7 holds the boundary binding and provenance relation. The exact bound version may be carried inward with its immutable ownership tag, but it never becomes a copied substitute or locally authored definition.

To change an immutable external object, return to its owner. The repository using the object does not edit the peer-owned source.

Material crossing outward may lose fidelity and may never gain scientific maturity merely by being exposed.

No outsider traverses the USO by default. What an outside reader or system needs is represented at the forward face.

Machine-readable metadata may support automation, but the architecture does not assume that automation has been enabled or validated. Current research practice may remain manual until the metadata contract is tested.

---

## Contained architectures

An architecture may be contained at one 7LM layer operationally while questions about that architecture expand across 7LM epistemically. When the question is how the OSI architecture operates, the complete OSI model remains inside 7LM L4: OSI L1 through OSI L7 are internal layers of a contained realized architecture, OSI L7 is not 7LM L7, and the OSI layer numbers do not map onto the 7LM layer numbers. When the question changes to the origins, development, justification, standardization, institutional history, or design constraints of OSI, the inquiry may rail the OSI object across the architecture; the operating stack remains an L4 object and the inquiry generates layer-owned representations of it. Which layer answers which question is tabulated at L8/L4/0.md, and the analytic views that select a subset of layers are stated at L8/L3/0.md.

---

## The rest of the documentation

The sheets. Every directory carries its own 0.md, headed by its path, stating that position in full. Read L7/0.md for the face and L8/0.md for the interior, then the sheet of whatever position you are working at.

The record. What changed, when, on whose word, and what each prior version carried is at L8/L6/history/. The prior texts that integrity needs reconstructable are retired at L8/L6/retired/. Integrity is enforced; no deletion is implied.

---

## Exemplars

The architecture is instantiated in four repositories: the scaffold at https://github.com/aybllc/7lm, the exemplars at https://github.com/aybllc/autonomous and https://github.com/aybllc/auditonomous, and the curated-source archive at https://github.com/aybllc/l6.

Architecture and implementation status are separate claims. What each repository holds, and where it is not yet as this specification states, is documented in that repository's own sheets and in its records at L6/history/. Their layouts do not control the architecture.

---

## Harmonization Record

Normative authority and non-erasure rule. This document and the layer sheets control. Harmonization removes stale duplicate contracts but preserves unique research notes, author notes, examples, cases, rationales, hypotheses, and inherited terms. A reserved term remains available for research without manufacturing a directory or layer owner.

Architecture and implementation status are separate claims. The canonical topology declares admissible and owned positions even when a present repository has not instantiated them. Current exemplar layouts are implementation work in progress and do not rewrite the architecture.

**[Clarification]** Questionable inherited semantics are preserved in their stated or recoverable meaning. Unless the disposition at the receiving sheet expressly ratifies an owner and path, topology remains unadjudicated. No convenience placement settles an open scientific question.

Each inherited term is disposed of at the sheet of the layer that received it, under that sheet's "Inherited terms and dispositions". The full table of inherited terms with their dispositions, and the dated record of each disposition, stands at L8/L6/history/specification-history.md.

# FILE: USO/l5/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L5/0.md` — 1,344 words*

# USO/l5/

**Object:** Interpretation, review, judgment, argument, and teaching.
**Path:** `USO/l5/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/ — this layer, as the owner of research interpretation, review, judgment, argument, research governance, and teaching for the bounded scientific object.

L5 is the layer of the USO interior that answers "What may responsibly be concluded, questioned, taught, compared, or decided from the available work?" It takes the work and evidence produced at `USO/l4/` together with the contracts pinned at `USO/l3/` through `USO/l0/`, and it produces bounded claims and decisions for retention at `USO/l6/` and presentation at `L7/`. It does not produce lower-layer facts, and nothing said here substitutes for them.

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | What may responsibly be concluded, questioned, taught, compared, or decided from the available work? |
| Function | Frame questions, interpret evidence, evaluate claims, conduct review, govern research decisions, and communicate scientific meaning. |
| Directional inputs | Construction: L4 work/evidence plus relevant L3-L0 contracts. Inspection/query: questions may enter from L7; they frame inquiry but do not create lower-layer facts. |
| Output | A bounded claim/decision with cited dependencies, limitations, review status, and reproducibility information for L6 retention and L7 presentation. |
| Hard boundary | Consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify workflow state, ACL/role constraints, review status, routing, institutional access, and release readiness. These instructions govern machine handling; they do not create the research meaning. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents
- research questions, hypotheses, rationale, analyses, interpretations, findings
- literature search and relevance judgment
- claim scope, limitations, uncertainty, explanation, conclusion
- review, audit, research governance, teaching, manuscript meaning

### Permitted actions
- compare claim to evidence; interpret; criticize; accept/reject/qualify/supersede
- request revision from a lower owner without silently mutating it
- prepare a claim for preservation and presentation

### Acceptance / boundary check
- Claim is no broader than evidence/maturity.
- Methods, exclusions, uncertainty, and inferential limits are disclosed.
- Research meaning is distinguished from L4 writing and L7 release.

## Directory structure

```text
USO/l5/0
├── research/0
│   ├── lanes/0
│   ├── review/0
│   └── findings/0
└── institution/0
    ├── peers/0
    └── undergraduates/0
```

**Boundary / traversal rule.** L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| USO/l5/ | Layer 5 container for research interpretation, review, judgment, argument, research governance, and teaching. | Keeps scientific meaning and decision distinct from the L4 work product and the L7 presentation surface. |
| research/ | Directory for the active research record and discourse owned at L5. | Keeps inquiry and interpretation separate from institutional participant structure. |
| research/lanes/ | Directory for distinct lines of inquiry within the research record. | Prevents separate questions or investigative paths from being collapsed into one undifferentiated research stream. |
| research/review/ | Directory for review, criticism, audit, and qualification of research claims. | Keeps review activity distinguishable from the findings being reviewed. |
| research/findings/ | Directory for findings that may responsibly be stated from the available work and evidence. | Keeps stated findings tied to L5 judgment rather than treating raw L4 output as a conclusion. |
| institution/ | Directory for the institutional research setting and participant positions. | Keeps the social/institutional setting distinct from the research record itself. |
| institution/peers/ | Directory for peer participant positions within the institutional setting. | Keeps peer participation distinct from other institutional roles. |
| institution/undergraduates/ | Directory for undergraduate participant positions within the institutional setting. | Keeps undergraduate participation distinct from peer and research-owner roles. |

## In the case of

**Research note.** Forward-facing discourse forums, chat, and institutional documentation may allow fellows or participants to operate under a shared institutional identity while access controls separate participant roles and lower-layer discourse backends.

**Standing.** A forum or chat interface may be realized operationally at L4 and exposed at L7, while institutional roles, discourse, interpretation, review, and research meaning carried through it remain L5-owned. L7 governance concerns the external/public legal and access relation, not scientific judgment.

**Author note.** External definitions can be interpreted here. Interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0.

The specification's analytic-expansion table states what an inquiry may ask or represent at this layer when a contained architecture becomes an object of inquiry. Its row for "L5 - Research / Discourse" reads: "Institutional debate, research interpretation, committee or scholarly discourse, pedagogical framing, competing explanations, and judgments about why the architecture took its form." The operating architecture under inquiry stays owned at `USO/l4/`; only the representations the inquiry produces are owned here. The specification's author note on that table: "The OSI model does not become 7LM L0-L7. The question about the OSI object expands. Operational containment remains L4, while representations produced by the inquiry are owned by the 7LM layer that can state them correctly."

The specification's analytic-view table places this layer with `USO/l6/` in one research-facing band and keeps the two owners separate: "Memory/history and research/discourse are the upper scientific interior. They remain separate layer owners even when shown as one research-facing band." The author note on that table adds: "These bundles are semantic and dependency views, not filesystem nesting and not mandatory traversal paths." `USO/l5/` and `USO/l6/` are siblings under `USO/`; neither contains the other.

The contextual semantic route bears on the boundary rule above. The specification's declared semantic-deference rule states: "For the bounded purpose of interpreting the peer, the receiver intentionally takes the owner's exact bound definition as authoritative for what the owner means and does not re-prove or locally redefine that meaning. This declared trust is not a claim that the definition is objectively true, not an endorsement of downstream claims, and not a transfer of authorship." L5 interpretation of a peer definition works under that rule; the bound definition itself arrives through `L7/peering/` and is owned by its source. The specification adds: "An argument about Autonomous cannot silently stand in for an argument about Auditonomous. A cross-object claim requires an explicit named relation or handoff."

**Clarification.** A scale, a ladder, or a vocabulary of levels or statuses is semantics of layer 4 and up: as written it is an L4 object, and what it means is L5 discourse. It is not an L1 state space and not L2 mathematics. A ladder of review statuses is research governance and stands at L5, not at the L0 epistemic rail.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters from `USO/l4/` and leaves toward `USO/l6/`; inspection enters from `USO/l6/` and proceeds toward `USO/l4/`. These are the two valid directions the specification states — scientific construction outward L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7, and authorized external inspection inward L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0 — and a question that enters from `L7/` frames inquiry here but does not create a lower-layer fact. The specification's direction rule governs both: "Scientific construction proceeds from L0 outward only as dependencies are established. External inspection begins at L7 and proceeds inward. Description does not become prescription merely because the structure can be traversed." Construction reaches this layer only as far as the `USO/l4/` work and the lower contracts it cites justify; what leaves toward `USO/l6/` is the bounded claim/decision the information sheet describes, and what `L7/` presents is that claim's release, which this layer does not own.

# FILE: USO/l5/institution/0.md

# USO/l5/institution/

**Object:** Directory for the institutional research setting and participant positions.
**Path:** `USO/l5/institution/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/

## What this directory is

This is the directory for the institutional research setting and participant positions. It holds the social/institutional setting in which the research at `USO/l5/research/` is conducted: which participant positions exist, and how the roles of peers, undergraduates, and the research owner are distinguished. The layer owns review, audit, research governance, and teaching; this directory holds the participant structure through which those are carried, not the research record itself.

In practice a person filing here records a participant position — a peer position under `USO/l5/institution/peers/`, an undergraduate position under `USO/l5/institution/undergraduates/` — and keeps it separate from any claim, review, or finding the participant produces, which is filed under `USO/l5/research/`. Holding a position confers no evidential weight: the layer's hard boundary is that consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact.

## Why it exists

Keeps the social/institutional setting distinct from the research record itself. Who participated, and in what role, is answered here; what was concluded, and on what evidence, is answered at `USO/l5/research/`.

## Holds

- the institutional research setting
- participant positions: peer positions (`USO/l5/institution/peers/`) and undergraduate positions (`USO/l5/institution/undergraduates/`)
- the distinction between peer, undergraduate, and research-owner roles

## Does not hold

- the research record: lanes, review, findings — `USO/l5/research/`
- teaching as research meaning — owned at `USO/l5/` (layer sheet, Owns / represents); this directory holds only the participant positions
- external peers as bound objects, and peer-owned ingress/egress — `L7/peering/`
- the external/public legal and access relation — `L7/governance/`
- ACL/role constraints and institutional access as machine-readable metadata — directory metadata described at `USO/l5/`; not instantiated in this repository
- a forum or chat interface as realized and operated — `USO/l4/work/`; as exposed — `L7/`
- the record of how participation developed over time — `USO/l6/history/`

## In the case of

**Research note.** Forward-facing discourse forums, chat, and institutional documentation may allow fellows or participants to operate under a shared institutional identity while access controls separate participant roles and lower-layer discourse backends.

**Standing.** A forum or chat interface may be realized operationally at L4 and exposed at L7, while institutional roles, discourse, interpretation, review, and research meaning carried through it remain L5-owned. L7 governance concerns the external/public legal and access relation, not scientific judgment.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0. The machine-metadata row of the layer sheet names "ACL/role constraints" and "institutional access" as things machine-readable metadata may identify; those instructions govern machine handling and do not create the research meaning, and they are not instantiated in this repository.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- The word "institution" here means the institutional research setting as the specification's prose states it; the specification does not name any actual institution, and this sheet does not.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l5/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l5/institution/peers/

**Object:** Directory for peer participant positions within the institutional setting.
**Path:** `USO/l5/institution/peers/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/

## What this directory is

This is the directory for peer participant positions within the institutional setting. A peer position is a participant role in the research conducted at `USO/l5/research/` — a participant in review, audit, research governance, or teaching — distinguished from the research-owner role and from undergraduate positions. The position is filed here; the review, criticism, or claim the peer produces is filed at `USO/l5/research/review/` or the corresponding research directory.

In practice a person filing here records the peer position and the role it carries in review, audit, research governance, or teaching. The position gives the peer's judgment a place in the record, not evidential weight: consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact.

## Why it exists

Keeps peer participation distinct from other institutional roles. A review can then be read with its author's position known and kept separate from the position of the research owner whose claim is under review.

## Holds

- peer participant positions within the institutional setting
- the role a peer position carries in review, audit, research governance, or teaching

## Does not hold

- the review a peer writes — `USO/l5/research/review/`
- claims and findings a peer contributes — `USO/l5/research/lanes/`, `USO/l5/research/findings/`
- undergraduate positions — `USO/l5/institution/undergraduates/`
- an external peer as a bound object and its ingress or egress objects (the `peer-1` / `object-1` positions) — `L7/peering/`. The specification uses the word peer for both this directory's participant positions and the L7 peering positions; this sheet states the two paths and does not adjudicate their relation.
- access relations and permissions — `L7/governance/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0. A peer's interpretation carries the same rule.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l5/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# USO/l5/institution/undergraduates/

**Object:** Directory for undergraduate participant positions within the institutional setting.
**Path:** `USO/l5/institution/undergraduates/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/

## What this directory is

This is the directory for undergraduate participant positions within the institutional setting. An undergraduate position is a participant role in the research and teaching conducted at L5, distinguished from peer positions and from the research-owner role. The position is filed here; any claim, analysis, or review an undergraduate participant produces is filed at `USO/l5/research/` under the same rules as any other L5 claim.

In practice a person filing here records the undergraduate position and the role it carries in the research setting; the layer owns review, audit, research governance, and teaching, and an undergraduate position is one participant position within that setting. An undergraduate participant's work is held to the same acceptance check as every other L5 claim: no broader than evidence/maturity, with methods, exclusions, uncertainty, and inferential limits disclosed.

## Why it exists

Keeps undergraduate participation distinct from peer and research-owner roles. The three roles are then separately readable in the record, and a claim is read with its author's position known.

## Holds

- undergraduate participant positions within the institutional setting
- the role an undergraduate position carries in research participation and teaching

## Does not hold

- teaching material as produced writing — `USO/l4/work/writing/`; its research meaning stays L5
- what `USO/l0/pedagogy/` owns at L0; that sheet states it
- claims, analyses, and findings an undergraduate contributes — `USO/l5/research/`
- peer positions — `USO/l5/institution/peers/`
- access relations and permissions — `L7/governance/`

## In the case of

**Research note.** Forward-facing discourse forums, chat, and institutional documentation may allow fellows or participants to operate under a shared institutional identity while access controls separate participant roles and lower-layer discourse backends.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.

# FILE: USO/l5/research/0.md
*carried unchanged by this branch (same as its base) — 459 words*

# USO/l5/research/

**Object:** Directory for the active research record and discourse owned at L5.
**Path:** `USO/l5/research/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/

## What this directory is

This is the directory for the active research record and discourse owned at L5. It is where the layer's core question — "What may responsibly be concluded, questioned, taught, compared, or decided from the available work?" — is worked on the record: research questions, hypotheses, rationale, analyses, interpretations, and findings, together with literature search and relevance judgment and the claim scope, limitations, uncertainty, explanation, and conclusion that attach to each claim.

In practice a person filing here places a line of inquiry under `USO/l5/research/lanes/`, review of a claim under `USO/l5/research/review/`, and a finding that may responsibly be stated under `USO/l5/research/findings/`. Whatever is filed compares a claim to evidence, interprets, criticizes, or accepts, rejects, qualifies, or supersedes; it may request revision from a lower owner without silently mutating that owner's object. The output form is the layer's: a bounded claim/decision with cited dependencies, limitations, review status, and reproducibility information for L6 retention and L7 presentation.

## Why it exists

Keeps inquiry and interpretation separate from institutional participant structure. The research record is about what is claimed and on what evidence; who holds which participant position is filed at `USO/l5/institution/`.

## Holds

- distinct lines of inquiry — `USO/l5/research/lanes/`
- review, criticism, audit, and qualification of research claims — `USO/l5/research/review/`
- findings that may responsibly be stated from the available work and evidence — `USO/l5/research/findings/`
- literature search and relevance judgment, and the disclosure of methods, exclusions, uncertainty, and inferential limits that each claim carries

## Does not hold

- the work product itself: writing, experiments, computation as performed — `USO/l4/work/`
- exact relations, operators, proofs — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`
- approximation, uncertainty models, tolerances as engineering objects — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`
- admissible states — `USO/l1/state-space/`
- locally authored definitions — `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`
- institutional participant positions — `USO/l5/institution/`
- retained history, superseded or retired material — `USO/l6/`
- external provenance, governance, peer bindings, and released publications — `L7/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0. The layer's hard boundary applies to everything filed here: consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact. A claim filed here is no broader than evidence/maturity.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term: thesis-statements — Relocated. "Research claims and thesis statements belong at USO/l5/research/lanes/ or the corresponding L5 research artifact." See `USO/l5/research/lanes/0.md`.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l5/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l5/research/findings/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L5/research/findings/0.md` — 526 words*

# USO/l5/research/findings/

**Object:** Directory for findings that may responsibly be stated from the available work and evidence.
**Path:** `USO/l5/research/findings/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/

## What this directory is

This is the directory for findings that may responsibly be stated from the available work and evidence. A finding is an L5 judgment: it is what the layer concludes after comparing a claim to the L4 work and evidence and to the relevant L3–L0 contracts. It is not the raw L4 output, and it is not the proof, measurement, or lower-layer fact it rests on; it cites them.

In practice a person filing here states the finding, its cited dependencies, its scope and limitations, its uncertainty, and its review status, and discloses the methods, exclusions, and inferential limits behind it — the form the layer's output requires for L6 retention and L7 presentation. A finding is no broader than its evidence and maturity. A finding that review later rejects or supersedes is qualified or superseded here as an L5 act; the finding as it stood is retained at `USO/l6/`.

## Why it exists

Keeps stated findings tied to L5 judgment rather than treating raw L4 output as a conclusion. A computation run, an experiment performed, or a manuscript written at `USO/l4/work/` becomes a finding only when L5 judges what it supports.

## Holds

- findings that may responsibly be stated from the available work and evidence
- for each finding: cited dependencies, limitations, uncertainty, explanation, conclusion, review status, and reproducibility information
- a finding that the available work does not support a claim, stated with the same disclosure

## Does not hold

- the work and evidence a finding rests on — `USO/l4/work/`
- the line of inquiry that produced it — `USO/l5/research/lanes/`
- the review, criticism, or audit of it — `USO/l5/research/review/`
- exact relations or proofs a finding invokes — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`
- tolerances, uncertainty models, and feasibility conditions a finding relies on — `USO/l3/engineering-mathematics/`
- a definition; a finding may fix what a definition's consequences are, but the definition stays at `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/` or, for a peer definition, at its `L7/peering/` binding
- refuted or superseded findings as retained history — `USO/l6/`
- the released form of a finding — `L7/publications/`

## Boundary / traversal rule

L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0. Consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact. A finding about a lower-layer object that requires different lower-layer meaning is returned to that owner as an explicit revision; it is not reinterpreted in place here.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l5/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l5/research/lanes/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L5/research/lanes/0.md` — 578 words*

# USO/l5/research/lanes/

**Object:** Directory for distinct lines of inquiry within the research record.
**Path:** `USO/l5/research/lanes/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/

## What this directory is

This is the directory for distinct lines of inquiry within the research record. Each lane is one question or investigative path — its research questions, hypotheses, rationale, analyses, and interpretations — kept apart from every other lane so that what one path concludes is not read as what another path concluded. A lane frames a question and interprets the evidence it gathers; it compares claim to evidence and states the claim's scope, limitations, and uncertainty.

In practice a person filing here opens one lane per line of inquiry, names the L4 work and evidence and the L3–L0 contracts the lane depends on, and keeps the lane's claims no broader than that evidence and maturity. A research claim or thesis statement is filed here (or in the corresponding L5 research artifact) as a claim of the lane that asserts it, with its dependencies cited. Review of a lane's claims is filed at `USO/l5/research/review/`; what the lane may responsibly state as a finding is filed at `USO/l5/research/findings/`.

## Why it exists

Prevents separate questions or investigative paths from being collapsed into one undifferentiated research stream. Each lane keeps its own question and its own claims; a claim is read as the claim of the lane that asserts it.

## Holds

- distinct lines of inquiry, one lane per question or investigative path
- research questions, hypotheses, rationale, analyses, interpretations belonging to a lane
- research claims and thesis statements, as claims of the lane that asserts them, with cited dependencies, limitations, and review status
- literature search and relevance judgment performed within a lane

## Does not hold

- review, criticism, audit, or qualification of a lane's claims — `USO/l5/research/review/`
- findings stated from the available work — `USO/l5/research/findings/`
- the work and evidence a lane interprets — `USO/l4/work/`
- proofs and exact relations a claim depends on — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`
- locally authored definitions a claim's terms depend on — `USO/l0/semantics/definitions/`
- external antecedents (prior art) — `L7/provenance/prior-art/`
- superseded lanes and retired claims — `USO/l6/`

## Inherited term: thesis-statements

Disposition: **Relocated.** Controlling harmonization: "Research claims and thesis statements belong at USO/l5/research/lanes/ or the corresponding L5 research artifact."

## The working position

A lane is also where an item goes when the owner takes it up from the catch-all `USO/l5/research/review/`, and it goes back there if set down again. Position is state; the two moves are recorded by the repository's history. What is retired and what is deleted is one rule, stated once at `USO/l6/0.md`; this position does not restate it.

## Boundary / traversal rule

L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0. Consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited term thesis-statements: Relocated here.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l5/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l5/research/review/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L5/research/review/0.md` — 610 words*

# USO/l5/research/review/

**Object:** Directory for review, criticism, audit, and qualification of research claims.
**Path:** `USO/l5/research/review/` · **Layer:** L5 — RESEARCH / DISCOURSE · **Owner:** USO/l5/

## What this directory is

This is the directory for review, criticism, audit, and qualification of research claims. Review compares a claim to its evidence, criticizes it, and accepts, rejects, qualifies, or supersedes it. Review and audit apply the layer's acceptance check: the claim is no broader than evidence/maturity, and methods, exclusions, uncertainty, and inferential limits are disclosed. The claim under review stays where it was filed — in its lane at `USO/l5/research/lanes/` or as a finding at `USO/l5/research/findings/` — and the review is filed here, naming it.

In practice a person filing here names the claim reviewed and its cited dependencies, states the review's outcome and its review status, and, where the review finds a defect in a lower-layer object, requests revision from that lower owner without silently mutating it. A review does not repair the object it reviews; the correction belongs to the owner of the layer where the invariant or contract failed.

## Why it exists

Keeps review activity distinguishable from the findings being reviewed. A reader can then see a finding, the review of it, and the review status the finding carries as three separate things.

## Holds

- review, criticism, audit, and qualification of research claims
- the review status a claim carries, as the layer's output requires ("cited dependencies, limitations, review status, and reproducibility information")
- the outcome of a review of a claim — accept, reject, qualify, or supersede (the layer's permitted actions as applied to the claim reviewed)
- requests for revision addressed to a lower-layer owner
- everything awaiting the owner's review, of any kind, until it is taken up, filed, or set aside labelled PARKED (the catch-all; see below)

## Does not hold

- the claims and lines of inquiry under review — `USO/l5/research/lanes/`
- the findings under review — `USO/l5/research/findings/`
- the L4 evidence a review re-checks — `USO/l4/work/`
- proof or measurement; review cannot supply either — `USO/l2/formal-mathematics/`, `USO/l4/work/`
- peer participant positions, including a reviewer's institutional position — `USO/l5/institution/peers/`
- superseded reviews and retired claims — `USO/l6/`
- external/public legal and access governance — `L7/governance/`

## The catch-all

The catch-all. In every 7LM repository this position is also where everything awaiting the owner's review lands, however much of it there is. What the owner sets aside stays here labelled PARKED. What the owner takes up moves to `USO/l5/research/lanes/`, and back if set down again. Finished work is filed at its layer. No other position and no new directory is needed for this; the topology already has the two moves and the exits. What is retired and what is deleted is one rule, stated once at `USO/l6/0.md`; this position does not restate it. The tree states no rule about what an intelligence may or may not touch (owner's direction, 2026-09-06).

## Boundary / traversal rule

L5 may interpret externally owned definitions, but interpretation does not transfer ownership of the peer definition into L0. Consensus, prose, authority, or usefulness cannot substitute for proof, measurement, or a lower-layer fact.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l5/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.

# FILE: USO/l6/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L6/0.md` — 1,303 words*

# USO/l6/

**Object:** Memory, history, reconstruction, retirement, and query orientation.
**Path:** `USO/l6/` · **Layer:** L6 — LIBRARY · **Owner:** USO/l6/ — the owner of the object's internal memory: prior internal states and versions, branch and decision history, failed paths and unresolved obligations, corrections, supersession, retirement, and retained memory.

L6 is the Library of the bounded scientific object. It answers the Core question "Can the object's internal history, prior states, failures, corrections, and retirement be reconstructed and correctly oriented for inspection?" It holds what the object used to be and how it changed, so that an inspector arriving from L7/ can orient before entering the active payload. It records, preserves, and orients; it does not judge and it does not own external provenance. The Analytic-view table of the specification places L6 with L5 in the upper scientific interior: "Memory/history and research/discourse are the upper scientific interior. They remain separate layer owners even when shown as one research-facing band."

## Information sheet

| Field | Statement |
|---|---|
| Core question | Can the object's internal history, prior states, failures, corrections, and retirement be reconstructed and correctly oriented for inspection? |
| Function | Preserve internal memory, prior versions, branch history, corrections, failures, supersession, retirement, ledgers, and reconstructable relations without re-owning external provenance. |
| Directional inputs | Construction: versioned artifacts and relations from L5 through L0. Inspection/query: L7 boundary events orient reconstruction; external provenance remains owned at L7. |
| Output | A reconstructable Library record identifying the relevant prior state, branch, correction, failure, supersession, retirement, and internal relation needed for inspection or reproduction. |
| Hard boundary | L6 records, preserves, and orients; it does not make the L5 research judgment or establish truth. |
| Machine metadata | Machine-readable metadata may identify retention, archival, retirement, supersession, reconstruction, and integrity instructions for memory/history. References to external provenance resolve to L7 rather than being re-owned by L6. Not instantiated in this repository: current research practice remains manual; no directory metadata, automation, or validation is enabled here. |

### Owns / represents
- prior internal states and versions
- branch history and decision history
- failed paths and unresolved obligations
- corrections, supersession, retirement, and retained memory

### Permitted actions
- record, preserve, compare, branch, supersede, retire, reconstruct
- retain prior classifications and corrections without erasure
- orient inspection before entering the active payload

### Acceptance / boundary check
- Prior internal states and changes are reconstructable.
- Correction does not require deletion of prior state.
- External source/provenance references resolve back to L7.
- Retirement preserves the record rather than erasing it.

## Directory structure

```text
USO/l6/0
├── history/0
└── retired/0
```

**Boundary / traversal rule.** For an externally encountered object, L7 is the boundary and owns its external provenance/governance relation. L6 is the first interior layer used to reconstruct prior internal states, changes, failures, supersession, and retirement before deeper inspection.

## Directory prose and rationale

| Directory / thing | Prose | Why |
|---|---|---|
| USO/l6/ | Layer 6 Library container for internal memory, history, prior states, failures, corrections, supersession, retirement, and reconstruction. | Keeps reconstructable scientific memory inside the USO while external provenance and public/legal relations remain on the L7 forward face. |
| history/ | Directory for prior states, branch history, corrections, failures, unresolved paths, and reconstructable development over time. | Preserves how the object changed instead of presenting only the latest state. |
| retired/ | Directory for retired or superseded internal states and bindings that are no longer current. | Implements retirement as preservation rather than deletion, including the history of peer bindings that are no longer active. |

## Git and scientific memory

Git history can support L6 reconstruction, but Git history and scientific memory/provenance are not the same object. Git records repository change events; L6 must preserve the scientific relations, decisions, failures, corrections, versions, and retirement needed for reconstruction. External source, authority, and prior-art provenance remain L7-owned.

The Harmonization Record states the same rule as a disposition: "Supporting-evidence disposition: Git history can support L6 reconstruction, but Git history and scientific memory/provenance are not the same object. External provenance remains L7-owned; internal reconstructable scientific memory remains L6-owned."

Commit identifiers named in an L6 record are orientation pointers to repository change events; the L6 record itself is the scientific memory.

**Clarification.** Integrity is enforced; no deletion is implied. What integrity needs reconstructable from the repository itself is retired, not deleted: the owner's authored documents, the rule texts of a prior layout, the error records. A superseded file whose every part is enumerated, in the record at L8/L6/history/, as standing elsewhere is deleted after that enumeration; git keeps the change event, the record keeps the enumeration. Nothing is deleted before it is enumerated, and no rule of no deletion is enforced anywhere: the one CI check checks integrity, not the absence of deletion.

## In the case of

**Author note.** When full interpretation begins outside the object, L7 is encountered first and L6 is the first interior layer. Interior-only inspection can therefore be described as beginning at L6.

**Harmonization Record.** "Architecture and implementation status are separate claims. The canonical topology declares admissible and owned positions even when a present repository has not instantiated them. Current Autonomous, Auditonomous, and agnostic-method layouts are implementation work in progress and do not rewrite the architecture. Disposition: blank 0.md / status markers remain historical implementation evidence at L6, not architecture."

**OSI as an object of inquiry.** The specification's table row for this layer, stating what an OSI-origin inquiry may ask or represent at L6: "Reconstructable development history, versions, superseded or retired material, internal chronology, and the record needed to recover how the local inquiry developed."

**In this repository.** Held at this layer:

- `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md` — the L6 record of the harmonization displacement event of 2026-09-04: every inherited term of the Harmonization Record's Baseline-to-current disposition table with the repository's actual prior and current paths, the dated chronology of deletions and moves already made on `main` before this pass, and the list of what is reserved and therefore has no current path.
- `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/` — the pre-harmonization scaffold of 2026-08-20, preserved verbatim at its original relative paths beneath `USO/...`, together with the owner's retired README working sketch at `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/README.md`.
- `USO/l6/retired/specification-draft-2026-09-03/` — the superseded draft of the architecture specification (PDF and its text conversion), superseded by the harmonized specification filed at `USO/l4/work/writing/`.

Not held at this layer:

- External source, authority, prior-art, and public provenance — `L7/provenance/`. The source ledger formerly at `USO/l6/provenance/source-ledger.md` is now `L7/provenance/sources/source-ledger.md`.
- Copyright, licensing, patents, permissions, access, and restrictions — `L7/governance/`.
- Peer bindings that are active — `L7/peering/`. Peer bindings that are no longer active are retired here under `USO/l6/retired/`, per the retired/ Why cell.
- Released publications — `L7/publications/`.
- The research judgment about a prior state (whether it was right, what it establishes) — `USO/l5/`.
- The current active payload of any layer — `USO/l0/` through `USO/l5/`. L6 orients inspection before that payload is entered; it does not duplicate it.

## Inherited terms and dispositions

The inherited terms that touch this position and their dispositions are in the specification's Harmonization Record. The prior scaffold's texts are retired under `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/`; what was moved, deleted, or superseded, and when, is in `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`.

## Exemplars (by reference)

The exemplar of this position is the sheet at the same path in `aybllc/autonomous` and `aybllc/auditonomous`, read with the specification's section "Exemplars". The exemplars fill in the layers by reference and are not merged into this scaffold; their layouts do not control the architecture.

## Direction

Construction enters from `USO/l5/` and leaves toward `L7/`; inspection enters from `L7/` and proceeds toward `USO/l5/`. This follows the two valid directions of the specification — scientific construction outward L0→L1→L2→L3→L4→L5→L6→L7, authorized external inspection inward L7→L6→L5→L4→L3→L2→L1→L0 — with crossing L7 ↔ L6 being a boundary/peering event, not filesystem nesting.

# FILE: USO/l6/history/0.md
*edited by this branch; path renamed on main to `L8/L6/history/0.md` — 824 words*

# USO/l6/history/

**Object:** Directory for prior states, branch history, corrections, failures, unresolved paths, and reconstructable development over time.
**Path:** `USO/l6/history/` · **Layer:** L6 — LIBRARY · **Owner:** USO/l6/

## What this directory is

"Directory for prior states, branch history, corrections, failures, unresolved paths, and reconstructable development over time." A record filed here answers how the object changed: which prior internal state existed, what corrected or superseded it, what failed, and what remains unresolved. It is written so that an inspector arriving from `L7/` can reconstruct that development before entering the active payload.

In practice, a person filing here records, preserves, compares, branches, supersedes, retires, or reconstructs; retains prior classifications and corrections without erasure; and orients inspection before entering the active payload. A record here is accepted when prior internal states and changes are reconstructable, when correction did not require deletion of prior state, when every external source or provenance reference resolves back to `L7/`, and when retirement preserved the record rather than erasing it. The record does not make the L5 research judgment and does not establish truth.

## Why it exists

"Preserves how the object changed instead of presenting only the latest state."

A repository shows its latest state by default; this directory keeps the earlier states and the relations between them addressable.

## Holds

- Records of prior internal states and versions, and of the changes between them.
- Branch history and decision history.
- Failed paths and unresolved obligations, recorded as such.
- Corrections and supersession, with the prior state retained rather than erased.
- Reconstructable development over time: dated chronologies of displacement events.
- In this repository: `USO/l6/history/harmonization-2026-09-04.md`, the record of the harmonization displacement event — inherited terms with prior and current paths, the chronology of deletions and moves already made on `main`, and the list of reserved terms with no current path.
- `specification-history.md` — the specification's versions, the dated lists of what changed in each, the owner's words behind each rule, and the exemplars' snapshot commits: what the specification carried about its own past until 2026-09-07, pulled out so that the specification states the architecture as it is. It also holds the table of inherited terms with their dispositions, which left the specification on 2026-09-07.
- `exemplars-2026-09-07.md` — the two exemplar repositories as they stood on 2026-09-07, the specification's exemplar section carried here word for word when the specification was reduced to the layer contracts: a record of a state, not a rule.
- `sheets-2026-09-07.md` — what the sheets carried about their past until 2026-09-07 (the prior scaffold's texts and where they went, deletions by commit, the owner's words quoted beside rules, the exemplar snapshots at pre-7LM commits), pulled out verbatim so that each sheet states its directory as it stands.
- `specification-removed-2026-09-07.md` and `specification-removed-2026-09-07.docx` — every passage that stood in the specification's version of 7 September 2026 and does not stand in it now, reproduced exactly as it stood, in the order it stood, each with the file that carries it now: the live sheet, the record in this directory, or the retired prior version; and, for the lines no other file carries, that this record is their only place. The list is machine-made by comparing the two versions and searching every line as an exact string. The two files are one record: the Markdown is the source, and the Word document is generated from it with the same styles as the specification and reads back to it unchanged, so the record and the document it records can be read and audited side by side.

## Does not hold

- Retired or superseded internal states and bindings that are no longer current — `USO/l6/retired/` (the spec's retired/ Prose cell).
- External source, authority, prior-art, and public provenance — `L7/provenance/`. A history record may reference a source; the reference resolves to `L7/` and is not re-owned here.
- The research judgment about a prior state — `USO/l5/`.
- Git history. Git records repository change events; a commit identifier in a record here is an orientation pointer, not the scientific memory. The memory is the record.

## Boundary / traversal rule

For an externally encountered object, L7 is the boundary and owns its external provenance/governance relation. L6 is the first interior layer used to reconstruct prior internal states, changes, failures, supersession, and retirement before deeper inspection.

## Standing

- This branch may remain empty. Presence in the agnostic topology establishes admissible capability, not mandatory population; unused now does not mean removed.
- Inherited-term disposition: the prior scaffold's history gate is retired at `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/USO/l6/history/0.md`. It defined four displacement dispositions and one retention reason of its own; that prose is not carried forward, and the harmonized layer sheet at `USO/l6/0.md` controls what this directory owns.
- Inherited-term disposition: "L6 provenance — Split." The prior scaffold's L6 provenance gate is retired at `USO/l6/retired/scaffold-2026-08-20/USO/l6/provenance/0.md`; external provenance now belongs at `L7/provenance/`.
- Machine metadata: described at `USO/l6/`; not instantiated in this repository. No automation.
